Charts: `snapshot: D1` (a snapshot the Result names, its SHA-256 checked), a body of one read-only `SELECT` or `WITH` statement (no `ATTACH`, `PRAGMA`, or writes; opened `mode=ro` with `query_only`; at most 200 rows and 8 columns; integers, reals, and text, through `database/sql` on `modernc.org/sqlite`), `x`, `y`, and `kind: bar\|line`; drawn as an SVG chart with the data table, the snapshot and its hash, and the query in the page, and the rows in the manifest. Illustrations: `src` is an image the host made with Painter or Gemini and `generator` names it (required); PNG, JPEG, WebP, or GIF up to 5 MiB, Mobbin-origin images refused (WP6.4); captioned "Generated illustration made with … ; it explains, it is not evidence". The binary never calls an image model, and the tests use a labelled fixture image. Screens: `pack: P1` and `artifact:` a screenshot of that pack, read through the proof store with its SHA-256 checked; body lines `x,y: note` (percent) become numbered marks with a notes list. Tests: `TestChartGolden` (the acquisition cohorts 3, 2, 1 in weeks 0, 1, 3, worked out by hand from the WP7.2 fixture exports; re-running the stored query on the snapshot gives the same rows; write statements are refused) and `TestRenderSample` |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP5.6 and WP7.1 (decide, respond, loops; ahead of M5 and M7)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-5.6a | WP5.6: `decide` with `[decisions] authority.<type\|layer>`; an SLA in business hours: at 24 h a page to the authority and an escalation comment mentioning founder B (`escalate_to`), at 48 h a reversible recommendation applied provisionally (`decision:provisional`, ratify/revert deadline in the body; ratification clears it), an irreversible one only re-paged and shown overdue; `constrains`-linked stories shown blocked; supersede chain; `decide --waiver <floor>`; `ops/actions.toml` read only from the protected default branch at a pinned SHA, edits needing two human CODEOWNER reviews and never `auto` on first merge; `respond` proposing a catalog action that, for `approval = human`, executes only through a GitHub Environment with required reviewers and a short-lived credential never in the agent env; timeline; close with a linked Bug and retro entry. Accept: non-catalog command refused, same-PR catalog edit unusable, human approval required and verified, the 48-hour reversible/irreversible pair | Built ahead of M5 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12). Nothing runs a sweep on a clock, so something must invoke it; `decide`'s registry inputs had a required Decision and no way to name the waived item; `respond` had no parameter or close input. The plan fixes no SLA start, ratify deadline, authority key set, or Decision-type taxonomy. A repository-wide rule cannot require two reviews for one path, and solo mode has one human (D2). A waiver applied provisionally would let an SLA lapse override a floor (rule 7). D-1.2a holds: the SLAs are business hours of `[time]` | `decide`'s positional is optional: `aayu decide` sweeps every open Decision (`type:decision`), `aayu decide <n>` one; WP7.1's loops run the sweep on a schedule. The clock starts at the issue's creation (as WP2.8's "near SLA" does) and counts `sla_hours` (24) and `provisional_after_hours` (48) business hours (`config.Clock.Add`; new `Clock.Between` gives status its hours left). At the SLA: one page per Decision per day through `[notify]` (key `decision-sla:` then `decision-overdue:`; on the GitHub channel the mention goes to the authority's login) and a replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:decision-escalation -->` comment that mentions `@<escalate_to>`, or says nobody is configured; `escalate_to` stays unset by default (there is no founder B yet) and is validated as a login. At 48 h a Decision that is open, `reversible: true`, Decision-ready, not already provisional, and not a waiver gets `status:validation` (so the Ready guard does not strip it for the edit), a Decision section saying the recommendation applies from the 48-hour instant with "Ratify or revert by <RFC 3339>" one more `sla_hours` later, then `decision:provisional` last, so an interrupted run redoes it and a rerun writes nothing. Authority: `[decisions.authority]` keyed by the Decision's parent's work-item type, then `L04` for a floor waiver (the floors' layer), else the Founder seat; a seat value resolves to its paired human, else `[autonomy] approver`; no login configured means any human (day one). `decide <n> --accept <option>` is refused for agents and bot identities (`identity.IsBot`, `bot_logins`) and for anyone but the authority's login, needs the option named in Options (whole word) and a Decision-ready body, writes the Decision section, `status:done`, clears `decision:provisional`, closes it, and closes every `supersedes` target as not planned with a `<!-- aayudha:decision-superseded -->` comment. `status` counts open items an open Decision `constrains` as blocked and shows each provisional Decision's business hours left (`#n 9 h`, `overdue`, `no deadline`). `decide <story\|bug> --waiver <floor>` files a sub-issue Decision labelled `decision:waiver` (new label), `reversible: false`, recommendation `fix`, `waives #n`; reruns find the open one; the redaction floor is not waivable (V3); a waiver is never applied provisionally, whatever its body says. `verify` (D-4.9a) now applies only accepted waivers: closed as completed, by a non-bot, with `waive, accepted by @<login>` in the Decision section, so a filed waiver waives nothing (`TestVerifyMobileSkipAndWaiver` files one, sees the floor still fail, accepts it, then sees it waived); `audit` counting `decision:waiver` is n/a until WP7.6. Catalog (`internal/ops`, schema in `ops/README.md`): `version = 1`, `[[action]]` with `argv_template` (no shell; `{param}` fills a whole value; `sh -c` refused; the program is never a parameter), typed `params` (string with an anchored pattern and no leading `-`, int with bounds, enum, bool; `environment` reserved), `approval`, `environments`, `max_per_hour`, `rollback` (`none` or another action), `blast_radius` (`single\|service\|global`; global never auto); unknown keys refused. `respond` reads it through the contents API at the default branch's head SHA; the executor re-reads it at that pinned SHA only after the compare API shows the commit on the default branch. "Never auto on first merge" is enforced at read time: an `auto` action is effective only if its definition (everything but `approval`) first appeared on the default branch as `human`, walking up to 30 commits of the file's history, else it runs as `human`. Two CODEOWNER reviews: CODEOWNERS and the protected-paths gate cover `ops/`, and solo mode merges by the recorded admin bypass (D2); a second required review is n/a until a second human (WP5.1). `respond` gains `--params k=v,…` and `--close`. `respond <incident>` prints the incident wrapped as untrusted, the catalog, and the timeline, appending each finished `aayudha-ops.yml` run with who approved it; `--action` validates the action and parameters, refuses (exit 4) a human action whose environment has no required reviewers or lets any branch deploy, enforces `max_per_hour` over the last hour's runs, and dispatches `aayudha-ops.yml` on the default branch (`return_run_details`), appending to a replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:timeline -->` comment. `aayudha-ops.yml` (template, installed by `init --gates` as the fifth gate workflow; not committed on aayudha, whose own catalog is empty) runs the job in the action's environment, so a human action waits for a required reviewer before any step runs; `respond --ci` then refuses unless the run is that workflow on the default branch with the matching run name, the catalog commit is on the default branch, a non-bot reviewer approved that environment (run approvals API), and the hourly cap holds, and runs the argv with only PATH/HOME/locale, the environment secret `AAYUDHA_OPS_CREDENTIAL`, and the job's OIDC request variables (no GitHub token, no `AAYU_*`); the executor writes nothing to GitHub. `--close` files the linked Bug (`caused-by #n`), appends `{incident, bug, timeline, …}` to `retro/incidents.jsonl` on the ledger, removes `needs:bug`, and closes the incident `status:done`. Tests: `TestDecideSLA` (47:59 and 48:00 business hours; the reversible Decision gains `decision:provisional` with a deadline of Thursday 10:00 London, the irreversible one and the waiver do not; every escalation mentions the fixture login `founder-b`; rerun writes nothing; acceptance refusals and supersede), `TestDecideWaiver`, `TestRespondProposes`, `TestRespondExecutes`, `TestRespondClose`, `internal/ops` (validation, rendering, auto provenance, PR commit refused). Live on 2026-10-12 against the public scratch repository `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-ops-1791773191` (environment `production` with the founder as required reviewer and a `main`-only branch policy; deleted afterwards): a non-catalog command exited 4; the proposed run waited with no step executed until the reviewer's approval (given through the API with the founder's token, as the test's reviewer), then printed `credential=present github_token=absent oidc=available` and `approved by @rahulnakmol`; a run naming a pull request's catalog commit failed after approval with "the catalog commit is not on the protected default branch". No environment was created on aayudha |
| D-7.1a | WP7.1: `loops/*.toml` → Amp schedules/plugin, Cursor Automations, and an Actions fallback; nightly `status --digest`, weekly `retro`, monthly seat self-review, quarterly `evolve`; all honour `halt` and `[budget]`; schedules in `[time] tz`. Accept: golden outputs per host (Amp schedule JSON, Cursor automation file, Actions cron with the tz-converted expression); the Actions fallback runs `status --digest` on the dogfood repo on schedule and the fake notify sink receives one digest; `halt` makes the next scheduled run exit 4 with no writes | Built ahead of M7 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12). V7 fixes the plumbing list, so there is no `loops` command. Amp has no schedule file: per its docs (Automations, 2026-10-12) a schedule is set on one thread by an agent through `set_schedule` (`title`, `prompt`, `schedule` as an RFC 5545 rule, `schedule_label`, `run_mode`), one schedule per thread. Cursor has no committable automation file either: automations are created in the editor, at cursor.com/automations, with `/automate`, through its API, or with its Terraform provider (`cursor/cursor` ~> 0.1, resource `cursor_platform_workflow`, `trigger.cron.schedule`); its docs do not state the cron's time zone, and the only statement found is a Cursor staff reply on the forum (2026-06-25) that it is UTC, with an unverified `CRON_TZ=` prefix. GitHub's cron is UTC. `[notify]` holds non-urgent pages until business hours, and a CI run keeps no held pages, so a digest sent overnight would be lost. `retro` and `evolve` are WP7.6 and WP7.7. No billing API reports one personal repository's Actions minutes. aayudha has no `[notify]` channel and no bot App (D1) | A loop is `loops/<name>.toml`: `description`, `command` (an aayu command; refused when it uses `--yes`, a human-only input, or a command gated on a terminal, a human pass, or the merge gate), `cron` (five fields in `[time] tz`: one minute; hours as a list or `*`; days of month or of week, not both; an hourly loop has no day fields), optional `hosts` (`amp`, `cursor`, `actions`; default all), `prompt` (extra work for a host's agent), and `bot` (the command writes as the bot). `internal/loops` renders, `go generate ./...` writes aayudha's own (CI drift-checks them), and `init --gates` writes a product's when it has `loops/`: `.github/workflows/aayudha-loops.yml`, `loops/amp/<name>.json` (the `set_schedule` input: the rule as `DTSTART;TZID=<tz>:20260105T<hhmm>00` plus `RRULE:FREQ=…;BYHOUR;BYMINUTE`, local time, so DST needs nothing; `run_mode` `fresh`; a human asks an Amp thread in the project to set it), and `loops/cursor/aayudha-loops.tf` (one `cursor_platform_workflow` per loop with `git_repo`, `git_branch`, the prompt, and cron triggers, applied with `terraform apply`). For the UTC hosts each local cron becomes one UTC cron per offset the zone uses (sampled on 15 January and 15 July), moving the days of the week when the time crosses midnight UTC; a day-of-month loop that would cross is refused. Every run starts with `aayu hook loop:<name>` (the `hook` plumbing command, no new command): exit 4 while `AAYUDHA_HALT` is set, exit 6 while the `[budget]` breaker's pause file exists or, in Actions, when this workflow's finished runs this month, each rounded up to a whole minute, reach `[budget] ci_minutes_month` (other workflows are not metered); otherwise it reports whether this run is the scheduled one: in Actions only the cron for the zone's current offset (`github.event.schedule`), elsewhere a time within 59 minutes after the local time, so of the two UTC crons exactly one runs each day; in Actions it writes `due=true\|false` to `GITHUB_OUTPUT`. Agents run the command with `AAYU_AGENT=1`, so verbs refuse while paused (exit 6) and while halted (exit 4). Each Actions job also starts with the halt step, here exiting 4, and keeps the `file` channel's outbox as an artifact. Loops: `digest` (`status --digest`, Mon–Fri 09:05: at the opening of business hours rather than nightly), `decisions` (the WP5.6 `decide` sweep, Mon–Fri 09:15, 13:15, 17:15; `bot`, so the Actions job mints the App token and skips with a notice without one), `seat-review` (monthly, Amp and Cursor only, since it needs a model), `retro` and `evolve` (not rendered: n/a until WP7.6 and WP7.7; the workflow lists them). `[notify] channel = "file"` (new) appends each page as a JSON line to `target` (default `.aayudha/state/notify-outbox.jsonl`); aayudha's own config uses it, so its digest lands in the run's `aayudha-notify-digest` artifact: that file is the "fake notify sink" of the acceptance. Tests: `TestUTCCrons` (London, Kolkata across midnight, New York, hourly, the Sydney refusal), `TestDue`, `TestParseRefuses`, `TestGolden` (`internal/loops/testdata/golden`: all three hosts), `TestLoopDigestOnSchedule` (the digest job against the fake GitHub at Monday 09:05 London: the BST cron is due and the GMT one is not, one digest in the outbox, then halt exits 4 at the guard and in the verb with no writes, then the minutes budget and the pause file exit 6), and `TestInitExtras` (`--gates` renders a checkout's loops). Live on 2026-10-12 against the scratch repository `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-loops-1791775332` (the generated workflow installing this branch; deleted afterwards): with `AAYUDHA_HALT` set, a dispatched digest run failed at its halt step with exit code 4 and ran no other step; with it cleared, the guard printed `dispatched by hand` and `status --digest` ran, its page held because 04:30 London is outside business hours, which is why the digest loop runs at 09:05 |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP7.5 and WP7.8 (`tell` and `release`)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-7.5a | WP7.5: `tell docs\|guide\|changelog\|release\|launch\|brand`; tone from `brand.yaml`; claims link only to Features with authoritative packs; UTM tagging; `tell publish` human-identity only, logged; accept: a guide references only proven Features, publish refuses a bot identity | `tell` runs in the host (mode `host`), so the host's model writes the copy and the binary can only brief and check it. Packs belong to stories, not Features, and the plan does not say what makes a Feature proven, what counts as a claim, which links get which UTM values, or where publishing puts the copy: the docs site is WP8.2 and no blog or social integration exists. `grill brand` (WP5.3, #101) was built in parallel | `internal/tell` plus `internal/cli/tell.go`. New registry inputs: `--from` (the host's Markdown draft), `--tag`, and `--campaign`. Without `--from`, `tell <kind>` prints the brief: the voice, tone, and avoided words, the Features that may be claimed, and the UTM tags. A Feature is proven when at least one story or bug under it (at any depth) is done (merged, released, measured, done) and every done one is covered by an authoritative pack: one that maps every Ready criterion, matches its hashes, and whose floors hold. This is the `status` proof-coverage rule (WP2.8), so coverage must be k/k with k > 0. Outside a checkout the ledger cannot be read, so nothing is proven. Tone: `design/brand.yaml`, read with grill brand's own strict parser (`design.ParseBrand`); tell uses `name` and `voice: {tone, avoid}` and leaves the palette and pairs to grill. A missing file, tone, or avoid list falls back to the documented default: a plain, specific, calm voice and an avoid list of hype words (`seamless`, `revolutionary`, `best-in-class`, …). Single or hyphenated words on the avoid list are refused literally; longer entries ("exclamation marks") only guide the host. With `--from`, every link to an issue or pull request of the repository is a claim: `#N`, `owner/name#N`, or any `issues/N` or `pull/N` path (absolute, root-relative, or relative, as GitHub resolves them) that does not name another repository on github.com. Each must be a proven Feature, so stories, bugs, PRs, and unproven Features are refused. Avoided words are refused too, case-insensitively, outside URLs. Any refusal exits 4 and lists the problems by line. Fenced code blocks, delimited as CommonMark renders them (closed only by a run of the opening character at least as long), are neither checked nor tagged. Every other absolute http(s) link gets `utm_source=<repository name>`, `utm_medium=<kind>`, and `utm_campaign=<--campaign, else --tag, else <kind>-<date>>`; a link that already has a `utm_` parameter keeps the author's. The checked copy is written to `.aayudha/tell/<kind>/<campaign>.md`. `--publish` (human-only; never exposed over MCP) is refused, with exit 4 and no writes (not even the local copy), for an agent lane (`AAYU_AGENT=1`), in CI (`--ci`), and when `GET /user` for the human token reports a bot (`type: Bot`, a `[bot]` login, or `[identity] bot_logins`). A human's publish is one `tell.publish` action in the log. It lands `tell/<kind>/<campaign>.md` and a `.json` publication record (SHA-256, cited Features, UTM, who, and when) on the ledger. For `release` with `--tag`, it also replaces tell's `<!-- aayudha:tell -->` block in that tag's GitHub Release notes; a tag without a GitHub Release, such as `rolling-*`, is left alone. `tell.ReleaseNotes` writes deterministic notes for `release` (WP7.8): shipped stories grouped under their proven Feature, linking only the Feature, with the rest counted and not described. Tests: `TestTellGuideCitesOnlyProvenFeatures` and `TestTellPublishHumanOnly` (the thin slice shipped end to end against the fake GitHub and a local ledger), `TestLoadBrand`, `TestTag`, `TestCheckFences`, `TestCheckRefs`, and `TestReleaseNotes` |
| D-7.8a | WP7.8: `release`: the promotion ladder for a Release issue; artifact identity; stamps included stories `released`; `tell release`; shipping evidence; triggers the `measure` schedule; accept: a dogfood rolling snapshot with stamped stories | D-0.4a removes the human approval on this repository but keeps the gates and conventions, and CONTRIBUTING.md already fixes the procedure. The registry gives `release` the human identity and a terminal confirmation. WP stories here merge through `gh pr merge --admin` (solo-mode bypass), not `aayu ship`, so they keep `status:ready`. Only a story's PR links it to the commit that shipped it. The plan does not say whether "included" means what the Release lists or what the promoted range ships, what identifies a rolling snapshot (it has no GitHub Release, since `.goreleaser.yaml` ignores `rolling-*` tags, #77), or what the `measure` schedule is: loops (WP7.1) are in review, and `measure` needs exports that a human supplies. Rulesets on a personal account's private repository need GitHub Pro | `aayu release <release> --to rolling\|main` (`internal/cli/release.go`) drives CONTRIBUTING.md's procedure and nothing else. It is refused for agent lanes, in CI, and for a bot account (WP7.5's checks). It needs a checkout and a yes on a terminal or `--yes` from the human (on this repository, the D-0.4a path for the maintainer's credentials). The Release must pass Ready, be open, and, for main, have a `vX.Y.Z` Version that is not yet tagged. The ladder rule: main needs this Release's rolling rung (`releases/<n>/rolling.json` on the ledger) and that commit must still be on rolling. Branch names come from `[branches]`. The range is `git rev-list --no-merges origin/<target>..origin/<source>`. Its stories are those named, through `Refs #n`, an `Aayudha-Story` trailer, or a `story/<n>-` branch, by the merged PRs into dev that GitHub links to each commit. The Release's `includes` links expand a Feature to its stories and bugs. Each listed item must be in the range or already released, measured, done, or cancelled; anything else refuses (exit 4) before anything is written. Every story the range ships is stamped, listed or not, because the artifact holds it: those not yet released become `status:released` (from `ready` or `merged` alike). Ones the Release did not list are reported. `--dry-run` reports all of this and writes nothing. Then: the candidate `promote/rolling-<ts>` (or `promote/main-<v>`) is cut from origin/target in a temporary worktree with `git merge --no-ff --signoff -m "Rolling snapshot: <ts>"` (or `"Release: <v>"`) and pushed. For rolling, `evals.yml` runs on the source ref when the repository has it, and a red run stops the promotion. CONTRIBUTING's `gh run watch` there has no `--exit-status`, because `evals-gate` reads its results; `release` stops earlier, with the same outcome. `ci.yml` is dispatched on the candidate with `gate=rolling\|stable`, through `return_run_details`. The run must be on the candidate's SHA, and a failed run has its failed jobs re-run once (the transient `release-snapshot` signing timeouts). A gate that still fails exits 4 and promotes nothing. That exact commit is pushed to the target as a fast-forward and tagged: lightweight `rolling-<date>`, or `rolling-<date>.<n>` for the n-th snapshot of the day, or annotated `vX.Y.Z` (`Release vX.Y.Z: <title>`). For main, the binary waits for the tag's `release.yml` run and re-runs it once. A run still red after the bookkeeping exits 5 with the `gh run rerun` hint. Any failure after the target moved (the tag, the tag's run, stamping, notes, records, or evidence) exits 5. The message names the commit now on the target and the steps left; they have no resume yet and are finished by hand. The candidate branch is deleted on every exit. Every push, dispatch, and re-run is an action in the log. Artifact identity: the commit, tree, and source SHA; the gate's run artifacts (name and digest) for rolling, and the GitHub Release assets (name, `sha256:` digest, size) for main. A tag with no GitHub Release is noted; any other lookup failure is a failure after landing. `tell release` uses WP7.5's `ReleaseNotes`, checks them with `tell.Check`, and publishes them under the human account to `tell/release/<tag>.md` on the ledger and, for `v*`, to the GitHub Release. Records: `releases/<n>/<target>.json` on the ledger. The Release gets `status:released` and a Shipping evidence entry per rung (the runs, artifacts, stories, notes, and measure due dates); the main rung closes it. Measure schedule: each Goal or Initiative above a stamped story gets `measure/due/<n>/<tag>.json` on the ledger (one per release, so a later release never erases or postpones an earlier obligation), due 28 days after the release (measure's default window), for the loops (WP7.1) and the human to run `aayu measure` on. The release notes are built and checked before anything lands, so a story title with an avoided word refuses the promotion rather than its bookkeeping. Tests: `TestReleaseLadder` covers the fake GitHub and a local remote: the refusals, the dry run, a gate failing twice, the re-run, stamping (an abandoned PR holding the same commit serves nothing), notes, records, a second same-day snapshot that ships an unlisted story without `evals.yml`, main refused while the snapshot is gone from rolling, main with `release.yml` re-run and release assets, and a version that already shipped. `TestReleaseFailsClosed` injects GitHub failures: before landing nothing is promoted, and after landing the exit is 5 naming what landed. `TestReleaseRefusesBeforeLanding` covers an avoided word in the notes, a conflicting candidate, an unreadable brand, red evals, and a gate run on another commit; a tag the remote refuses after rolling moved is in `TestReleaseFailsClosed`. `TestReleaseRedTagWorkflow` covers a `release.yml` still red after its re-run (exit 5 with the `gh run rerun` command) and a product without one. `TestReleaseLive` (AAYU_LIVE=1) runs on an `aayu-scratch-*` repository created with `AAYU_LIVE_ADMIN_TOKEN`, made public so it can carry aayudha's `rolling-main-promotion` and `release-tags` rulesets, and deleted. It has stand-in `ci.yml`, `evals.yml`, and `release.yml` workflows, a Goal > Feature > Story tree, and the story's PR squash-merged into dev. The ladder ran under the human's fine-grained token through the admin-only rulesets: rolling in about 50 s, then main in about 45 s, with the `checksums.txt` asset digest recorded (passed on 2026-10-12, last on the final head of #110). The repository's own promotions are left to the orchestrator |
| D-7.8b | WP7.8: `release` stamps the stories it ships `released` | Found running the first `aayu release` on aayudha (Release #147). `release` read only the first `Refs #n` of a merged PR, so #97 (`Refs #64`, `Refs #65`) shipped #64 but not #65; and it stamped any story a PR in the range referenced, including stories that never merged (the WP8.1 umbrella #70, referenced by PR #142 alongside story #130), which would claim undone work | `release` reads every `Refs`/`closes` line of a merged PR (#146). A referenced story that is not `status:merged` and not listed by the Release is reported as "referenced but not merged" and not stamped; it adds no measure obligation. Listed stories still must be in the range or already released |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP5.3–WP5.5 (grill, slice and plan, build and ship)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-5.3a | WP5.3: question sets per type in `references/<type>.md`; baseline required before a target on Goal/Initiative; refuses Ready without a validator pass; writes via `actions` under the human's identity when a human runs it, the bot otherwise; `grill brand` → `design/brand.yaml` with an AA palette check; bubbletea TUI for the CLI, prompt for MCP/skill; untrusted wrapping of pasted material; accept: ten tier-1 fixtures end in an issue passing `ready` 10/10, tier 2 on `grill goal` and `grill story` meets the WP3.4 bar with the results committed as the baseline, a Goal with no reachable baseline is refused Ready naming the metric | Feedback and Incident are intake: `ready` refuses them by design (WP2.7, WP2.9), so no Feedback or Incident issue can pass `ready`. The plan names no brand file format, no AA thresholds per use, no baseline window, and no rule for metrics no adapter serves (WP7.2's snapshot metrics). bubbletea v2.0.10 and later need Go 1.26 (D5). CI cannot push a results file to `dev` (D-3.4a). Two more tier-2 cases do not fit the 12 USD cap | `grill <type>` takes all ten types plus `brand` (new enum value). A human run writes with the human token, untagged; an agent lane (`AAYU_AGENT=1`, MCP) writes under the bot, or the tagged human fallback (WP1.4). New issues carry `type:<type>` and `status:planned`, or `status:backlog` for intake, as the forms file them. Goal and Initiative: a Metric `<source>:<metric> [filter]` (the `measure` syntax) is fetched through its WP5.2 adapter over the 28 whole UTC days before today and written into the Baseline section (value first, then metric, population, half-open window, fetch time, and the source query in a fence), replacing what the draft held; when the adapter is missing, unreachable, or write-scoped the draft fails Ready (exit 4, nothing written) with `baseline: no reachable baseline for metric "<metric>" (a Target needs a reproducible baseline first): <adapter error naming [integrations.<name>]>`. A snapshot metric (`activation`, …) must already hold its measured value in Baseline, else the same refusal. Without `--title`/`--body`, a human on a terminal gets the bubbletea TUI (`charm.land/bubbletea/v2` v2.0.9): one question per field in schema order with the reference's questions as hints, parent and labels last, a review screen running the same validation that jumps to the first problem and creates only a passing draft; pasted text only becomes answer text. MCP: the `grill` prompt serves every reference and takes an optional `material` argument, wrapped as `<untrusted source="pasted">`. `grill brand --body <yaml>` parses `design/brand.yaml` strictly (`version`, `name`, `voice {tone, avoid}`, `palette {name: "#rrggbb"}`, `pairs [{fg, bg, use}]`) and checks every pair's WCAG contrast, floored to two decimals, against 4.5:1 for `text` and 3:1 for `large-text` and `ui` (SC 1.4.3, 1.4.11); any failing pair or missing text pair refuses (exit 4, nothing written); `--create` writes the file in the checkout for a PR. Tier 1: `evals/cases/grill/<type>-…` for all ten types record `grill <type> … --create`, then `ready 21 --check` for the eight non-intake types; for Feedback and Incident the run ends at the created issue, and the case's Ready check is the schema validation of the recorded item (`TestTier1GrillEveryType`). Tier 2: `grill/story-empty-claims` and `grill/goal-first-claim` carry rubrics and send their question set through `[tier2] references` (WP7.3's key); `max_usd` is 15.0. The results of the first passing `evals.yml` run on `dev` are committed as `evals/results/<sha>.json` in the next PR |
| D-5.3b | WP5.3 tier 2: `grill goal` and `grill story` meet the WP3.4 bar | Run 38204111801 on 5752ac6 (evals.yml, push to dev): `grill/goal-first-claim` scored 5, 5, 5, 5, 5 (pass^5 1, mean 5.0); `grill/story-empty-claims` scored 1 five times, every run the deterministic floor "fails Ready: Effort is required at Ready": the skill's "Drafting without a human" rule sent every decision the request leaves open to `open_questions`, effort included. The run took about 26 of the job's 30 minutes with seven cases, so the slice and plan cases of WP5.4 do not fit, and the brief cases (D-7.3b) fail the suite bar, so the run's verdict cannot stand for one WP's cases | The one fix: the grill skill and `references/story.md` say effort is the skill's estimate of size, required at Ready, never an open question (priority only when given). `evals.yml` gains a dispatch input `cases` (a regular expression over case IDs, passed to `eval -tier 2 -cases`) and a 60-minute timeout; the WP5.3 and WP5.4 bar is read from a dispatch on dev with `cases` = `^(grill\|slice\|plan)/`, whose results file is committed as the baseline. Outcome: run 38206893151 on bf0a837 (after the fix, push to dev) scored `grill/story-empty-claims` 5, 2, 5, 2, 5 and `grill/goal-first-claim` 5×5; the dispatched run 38207978439 on 967054b (`cases` `^(grill\|slice\|plan)/`, 3.84 USD) scored the story 5, 5, 5, 5, 2 (mean 4.4, pass^5 0) and the goal 5×5 (mean 5.0, pass^5 1). Every story run now passes Ready; the low scores are the judge finding a requirement the request does not state (a screen-reader criterion, unchanged rendering for claimants with claims). `grill story` therefore misses the per-case pass^5 bar (WP5.3's pair: pass^5 0.5, mean 4.7). Per the founder's instruction (one fix, no loop) it is left for the founder: a scope guard like the stub's (D-3.4c) or an `evolve` case is the next step. `evals/results/967054b….json` is committed as the record of that run; it is not a passing baseline for `grill story` |
| D-5.3c | WP5.3 tier 2 for `grill story` (D-5.3b left it below the bar) | Founder decision, 2026-10-12: the extra screen-reader criterion is the stub's defect class (D-3.4c), so it gets the same treatment, not another dispatch | The grill skill and `references/story.md` say a Completion Goal states only what the request or its parent asks for: accessibility, performance, and security are floors `verify` enforces on every story and are not criteria unless requested, nor are checks for users the request does not mention. `grill/story-empty-claims` gains five `scope` rules (screen reader, keyboard or focus, other accessibility, performance budgets, claimants who already have claims), checked in tier 1 and before the judge in tier 2; `TestGrillStoryScope` keeps both sides (the recorded criteria and a reworded requested one pass, each unrequested kind fails) |
| D-5.4a | WP5.4: `slice` makes XS-L stories with criteria, deps, and a footprint computed from the Feature's expected paths via `LAYERS.md` globs and confirmed by the plan, and closes an XL as "split into"; `plan` has checkpoints per layer, contracts, tests, instrumentation, rollback, with an EM plan-time review for `autonomy.never` layers recorded in the pack; accept: evals on a 3-layer and a 6-layer (L07 migration) Feature give stories ≤ L passing `ready` (tier 1, 100 %), each plan one checkpoint per footprint layer and a rollback for any L07 checkpoint, tier 2 at the WP3.4 bar, an XL input returned as ≥ 2 stories with the XL closed "split into" | Both verbs run in the host (mode `host`), so the binary needs the drafts as input. The Story schema has no footprint field, and expected paths are globs, not changed paths. A Feature is a container: closing it would orphan its stories. The pack is written by the merge gate before `ship`, and nothing yet carries a plan into it. The eval scratch repository has no `LAYERS.md`, and the WP3.4 tier-1 replay cannot reach files the recorded run wrote | `slice <feature> --draft <file> [--create]` and `plan <story> --draft <file> [--create] [--approve]` (new registry inputs; `--create` pinned off over MCP, `--approve` human-only); without `--draft` each prints what to work within (the Feature's expected paths and footprint; the story's footprint, criteria, and never layers). `internal/planning` holds the deterministic checks. A story's footprint is held in `layer:Lxx` labels: `slice` computes it from the story's `paths` (globs inside the Feature's Expected paths) by glob overlap with the `LAYERS.md` globs (the registry's layer table when the checkout has none, as `status --streak` does). `slice` refuses (exit 4, nothing written) a Feature without Expected paths, a story that fails Ready under the Feature (so XL, unmapped criteria, missing effort), paths outside the Feature's, a Feature layer no story covers, `depends_on` that is out of range or cyclic, and a hand-written Links field; `--create` writes the stories in dependency order as sub-issues with `status:planned`, their labels, and `depends-on #N` links. An XL input is a Story labelled `effort:XL` (a Feature is always sliced, never closed): it needs at least two stories, which go under its Feature, and it is closed as not planned with `status:cancelled` and a comment "Split into #a, #b". `plan` needs a story or bug that passes Ready and refuses a second checkpoint for a layer, a footprint layer without one, one outside the footprint (re-slice), a checkpoint without tests, an L07 checkpoint without a rollback, and a criterion no checkpoint covers; a story without layer labels takes the plan's layers as its footprint (written as labels). `--create` writes `specs/<story>/plan.json` to the ledger (the plan, the body hash it was made for, and `em_review {required, layers}` when the footprint meets `[autonomy] never`); `plan --approve` by the EM seat's paired human, else the `[autonomy] approver`, else the one human in solo mode (recorded as `solo`) adds `approved_by`, `approved_at`, `as`; `ship` (WP5.5) reads it and records it in the ship record next to the authoritative pack. Evals: `slice/grill-followups-3-layer` (L02, L03, L05), `slice/proof-retention-6-layer` (L02, L03, L04, L06, L07, L09, with the config migration), `slice/xl-story`, `plan/config-migration` (L07 with rollback), `plan/purge-kind-flag` (L02, L03); recorded calls may name the case's files as `{case}/…`, and the tier-1 fake answers label writes with a list. Tier 2: `[tier2] draft = "slice"\|"plan"` and `issue` run the drafts through the same checks against the case's fixture before the judge (`slice/proof-retention-6-layer`, `plan/config-migration`); `max_usd` is 18.0 |
| D-5.4b | WP5.4 tier 2 meets the WP3.4 bar | Run 38204807563 on a73790a (evals.yml, push to dev, all nine cases): `plan/config-migration` scored 5, 5, 5, 5, 5; `slice/proof-retention-6-layer` scored 2, 2, 4, 5, 2: the judge found every Feature requirement covered but three drafts required behaviour the Feature does not name (a `--dry-run` mode, repeatable flags, API retries, a manual workflow trigger, per-kind counts) | The one fix: the slice skill gets a scope step: every criterion restates something the Feature asks for; extra flags, retries, limits, triggers, reports, and outputs go to `open_questions`; fixture values in a criterion's state stay allowed. The WP5.3 and WP5.4 bar is read from the evals.yml run on dev after this fix. Outcome: the dispatched run 38207978439 on 967054b scored `slice/proof-retention-6-layer` 5, 5, 5, 5, 4 (mean 4.8, pass^5 1) and `plan/config-migration` 5×5 (mean 5.0, pass^5 1): WP5.4's tier 2 meets the WP3.4 bar; the results file `evals/results/967054b….json` is committed as its baseline |
| D-5.4c | WP5.4: `plan` evals meet the WP3.4 bar | The promotion-candidate tier-2 run 38230815198 scored plan/config-migration 5 2 5 5 5 (pass^5 0): one plan also rejected legacy configs and added a backup-free downgrade, behaviour the story does not ask for. The scope guard (D-3.4c) applied only to work items and brief prose | `plan`'s skill forbids unrequested behaviour (downgrades, refusing older inputs, new flags) and routes such choices to `open_questions`; a rollback restores, never drops data. Case scope rules now also run on `slice`/`plan` drafts, open questions excepted (TestPlanScope) |
| D-5.4d | WP5.4 tier 2, `plan/config-migration`: the v0.2.0 rolling promotion failed (release #268) | Evals run [38953604673](https://github.com/rahulnakmol/agentic-ayudha/actions/runs/38953604673) on `b629966`: the gating `skills` group failed on `plan/config-migration`, scores 4, 1, 5, 5, 5 (mean 4.0, pass^5 0 against the v0.1.0 baseline 1.0 in `evals/results/78bd4ae…-skills.json`). Run 2's floor: `out of scope: adds refusing older configs, which the story does not ask for ("reject legacy")`. The results file and the log keep only that run's call metadata (1270 output tokens), not the plan, so the sentence cannot be read back. The match is the bare verb "reject", not "rejects" or "rejected", which in a plan sentence follows "not", "never", "does not", or "must". The story asks that "existing configs keep their meaning", so "does not reject legacy configs" is the likeliest reading. **Who was wrong:** the case's rule, most likely: it has no exception for a negation, so a plan that promises existing configs keep loading is refused like one that adds the refusal. Not proven, because the reply is not kept | Case: the rule's `allow` removes negated forms ("not", "never", "without", "no", "n't", up to two words before reject or refuse) before matching, so an affirmative "rejects legacy" is still refused under either reading. Harness: a scope floor now quotes the clause around its match (`adds … ("reject legacy" in "config load does not reject legacy retention_days")`), so the next floor can be decided from the results file. Regression test: TestPlanScope "promises not to reject legacy" fails on the old rule. One rerun of the `skills` group after the merge |
| D-5.4e | WP5.4: "keep stories to the Feature's scope; extras become open questions" (S-5.4b) is enough for slice to stay in scope | The skills group failed its pass^5 on `slice/proof-retention-6-layer` in the push runs 39005126929 (191cd26: 5 5 5 4 2) and 39009127596 (ef41886: 4 4 2 5 5), against main's 1.0. Neither the slice skill nor the case had changed since #236, so this is not a regression, and the case's rule is not wrong: the judge's reason for each 2 names a requirement the Feature does not state. One run had a story reject retention values that are not whole numbers of days; the other had a workflow whose only trigger is the schedule. The skill's scope rule named only additions (flags, retries, triggers, outputs). Both inventions were restrictions, about 2 runs in 25 | Who was wrong: the skill. Step 4 of the slice skill is rewritten to cover both directions: nothing added, and nothing restricted (no input to reject, no validation or error rule, no "only", "never", or "exactly" the Feature does not state, with the Feature's own two examples). Each criterion must restate words of the Feature before it is written; anything else becomes an open question. The case gains `scope` rules for the two caught restrictions (D-3.4c), so the same invention fails before the judge. The bar is unchanged |
| D-5.4f | D-5.4d: the case's scope rules remove negated forms first, so a plan that promises the opposite is not floored | The skills group failed twice in a row on `plan/config-migration`, mean 4.2 and 4.0 but pass^5 0 against the v0.2.0 baseline (1.0): push run [39013697227](https://github.com/rahulnakmol/agentic-ayudha/actions/runs/39013697227) on fb84b73 scored 5 5 5 5 1, and push run [39014607367](https://github.com/rahulnakmol/agentic-ayudha/actions/runs/39014607367) on 233c3df scored 5 4 5 5 1. Both 1s were the same floor, the case's `downgrade` scope rule, on plans that rule a downgrade out: "no downgrade path other than restoring the backup" and "No data is dropped, and no downgrade transform or re-serialisation is used". Story #41's criterion 2 is a rollback that restores the file byte for byte, so both plans honour it. D-5.4d gave the `reject legacy` rule an allow phrase for negated forms but left the `downgrade` rule without one. So the case's rule was wrong, not the skill and not a regression: the plan skill and the case had not changed since D-5.4d, and the runs in between passed (42bab33: 5 5 5 4 4) | The `downgrade` rule gets the same negation allowance as `reject legacy`: "no", "not", "never", "without", or "n't" up to two words before "downgrade" removes the match first. A plan that adds a downgrade ("a downgrade collapses the per-kind values back into one scalar") is still floored. `TestPlanScope` keeps both sides with the two recorded phrasings. Confirmed with a filtered run of just this case on dev after the merge (a dispatch on the PR branch cannot reach the tier-2 jobs); its result is recorded in D-3.4i |
| D-5.5a | WP5.5: `build` refuses unless Ready and the body hash equals the snapshot; per-story lease (assignee + lock comment with TTL); worktree `.aayudha/worktrees/<id>` from `origin/dev`; `Aayudha-Checkpoint: n/N` commits; replace-in-place progress comment and checkpoint record on `ledger`; `verify` per checkpoint; `[autonomy] budget {wall, cost, attempts}` → `status:blocked` + page on breach or a second failure on a never layer; `--resume` from the last trailer; retries 429/5xx with jitter. `ship`: green gate; human pass = approving review from the seat's human (solo → `[autonomy] approver`), `type != Bot`; never layers need CODEOWNER approval regardless of streak; approval ID and streak state written to the pack; squash-merge; Complete section; `merged`; cleanup. `gc` prunes stale worktrees and leases. Accept: dogfood story Ready → merged; a bot review is rejected as a pass; a stale lease is reclaimed by `gc` | The plan names no TTL, no way to find leased stories, and no home for the checkpoint record. The binary never sees the host model's spend. Floors whose tools are absent fail by rule 7, and most laptops lack the pinned scanners. A POST or PATCH that answered 5xx may have landed. The pack is the merge gate's verified artifact, written before `ship` runs. aayudha has no `[autonomy] approver` and one human, so GitHub refuses the only possible approval (D2) | Lease: the lock comment `<!-- aayudha:lease holder=<login>@<host> expires=<UTC> -->`, the holder's login as assignee (an App's `[bot]` login cannot be assigned), and the new label `aayudha:leased` so `gc` and `doctor` find leases; `LeaseTTL` is 4 h, renewed by every build run; a live lease of another holder refuses the build (exit 4); an expired one is taken over. A story branch already on origin without a local worktree needs `--resume`, which cuts the worktree from that branch and reports its last `Aayudha-Checkpoint` trailer. N is the plan's checkpoint count when `specs/<n>/plan.json` is on the ledger (WP5.4), else the larger of k and the change's layer count; a retry of a checkpoint that missed keeps its k and adds `Aayudha-Attempt: <a>`. Each checkpoint runs `aayu verify` in the worktree; a floor that fails only because its tool is not installed there is reported but does not count (the merge gate runs the pinned tools and stays authoritative). `build/<n>/state.json` on the ledger holds the start time, checkpoints with their verify result, failed attempts, failures per layer, and any block; the `<!-- aayudha:progress -->` comment shows the same with the budget. Budget: wall time since the first run, spend from the story's actions-log costs plus the host's `AAYU_COST_USD`, and failed checkpoint attempts; a breach, or a second miss on an `[autonomy] never` layer, sets `status:blocked`, pages through `[notify]`, and exits 4; a human's `aayu ready` restarts the budget. The GitHub transport retries a 5xx with exponential backoff from 1 s only where repeating cannot write twice (GET, HEAD, PUT, DELETE, GraphQL queries), and adds up to 25 % random jitter to every wait, the rate-limit waits included. `ship`: the human pass must come from the Engineering Manager seat's paired human, else the `[autonomy] approver`, else any human who is not the author (bots and `[identity] bot_logins` never count); when the pack's footprint meets `[autonomy] never`, the approver must also be a CODEOWNER (base-branch CODEOWNERS) of every changed path in those layers, whatever the streak, and the EM's plan-time review must be approved on the ledger. The streak is computed at ship time from merged-PR history (WP5.7); a footprint the streak makes autonomous with no never layer ships without a review (`human_pass.kind = autonomous`). The approval ID, the pass rule, the never layers, the plan review, and the streak state go into `ship.json` beside the authoritative pack, not into `pack.json`, which stays as the gate wrote and verified it. `ship` releases the lease. `--solo-bypass` stays the human-only path (D-M4.5a). `gc` (human identity) reclaims expired leases, leases of closed stories, and stray labels, and removes the worktree and local branch of a closed story when nothing in it is uncommitted or unpushed (else keeps it, saying why); `doctor`'s `leases` check lists stale leases |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP4.8b and WP5.5b (review quality, delegation)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers. Research and sources:
[docs/research/agent-delegation-and-review.md](research/agent-delegation-and-review.md).

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-4.8b | WP4.8: `inspect` sends the diff, plan, pack, and criteria to one cross-family reviewer and posts one comment | Founder request (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): review every agent-authored PR better, borrowing from Alibaba's open review skill and Greptile, with automatic evaluation plus a peer review, measured by precision and recall on seeded defects. Every published reviewer adds a verify or filter stage after generation; self-rated confidence alone filtered poorly (research §1) | WP4.8b, added after WP4.8 |
| S-5.5b | The plan has no way to hand a story to another agent; `build` assumes the host's own agent writes the change | Founder request (2026-10-15): from Claude Code or Amp, send a Ready story to the Codex, Cursor, Claude Code, or Amp cloud agent with slash commands, review its PR, and close the loop; cloud by default, local fallback in an aayu worktree; one brief; generated environments; worktrees for every code-changing verb | WP5.5b, added after WP5.5 |
| D-5.5b.1 | The request names `aayu delegate <story> --to <agent>` and prefers extending `build` if that keeps the registry clean | Delegation needs exactly `build`'s gates (Ready and body hash, lease, budget, halt), its worktree, branch, commit, and PR rules, its checkpoint record, and its RACI row (AI Native Engineer). A `delegate` verb would duplicate all of them and add a 33rd verb | `build` gains `--delegate <codex\|cursor\|claude\|amp\|auto>` and `--local`; the slash commands give the short form. No new verb |
| D-4.8b.1 | WP4.8b: retrieve the changed symbols, their callers, related tests, and the `LAYERS.md` footprint | The merge gate's `aayudha/inspect` job holds the model key and must never run or check out PR code (D-4.8a); a whole-repository symbol graph needs every file of the head | `inspect.BuildContext` reads the head through the contents API at its SHA (`headSource`): each changed directory is an area; Go files are parsed (never built) for the changed declarations, their call sites in the same package, and the `Test*`/`Fuzz*`/`Benchmark*`/`Example*` functions that name them; other languages use the diff's hunk headers and sibling test files. At most 40 files and 200 kB each are read, 40 kB of context reaches the prompt, and a new file's source is not repeated (the diff shows it). Each area's plan lists files, layers, changed symbols, callers, related tests, whether a test changed, generated files, and risks: one per touched layer with a known risk (L02, L04, L06, L07, L09) and one per pattern over the added lines (concurrency, external input, secrets, destructive operations, ignored errors, boundaries), plus "no test changed in this area". Identifiers in the plan are cut to identifier characters; the retrieved code is an `<untrusted source="code-context">` block. Callers outside the package are not found (later: a repository index) |
| D-4.8b.2 | WP4.8b: findings with severity, evidence (file:line), suggested fix; noise filtering (confidence threshold, de-duplication, style nits the linters own); learning from dismissed findings | Model-chosen line numbers drift; self-rated confidence filtered poorly on its own in every published reviewer (research §1); aayudha has no vector store; in solo mode every agent (Cursor and Amp included) writes under the founder's account, so a 👎 reaction or a reply cannot be told apart from the builder's own | Findings v2 carry `category`, `quote`, `fix`, and `confidence`; aayu places each by its quote (an added line first), keeps the model's line only when it is in a hunk, else posts it in the summary only (line 0). `Filter.Clean`: a file outside the diff is dropped; `style`, and P2/P3 titles naming typos, formatting, imports, or naming, are linter-owned; files matching `[inspect] generated` or with a `Code generated … DO NOT EDIT` header are not reviewed; P2/P3 below `[inspect] min_confidence` (0.5) are dropped, P0/P1 never; P3 nits are counted, not posted; duplicates (same file, within 3 lines, title-and-detail word overlap ≥ 0.35, or the same fingerprint) merge keeping the highest severity. Dismissal is human-only: `inspect <pr> --dismiss <id> --reason …` (refused for agents, hidden from MCP) appends to `inspect/dismissed.jsonl` on the ledger; a finding is suppressed when it matches one dismissal's fingerprint, or resembles (word overlap ≥ 0.6, same file or directory) `[inspect] suppress_after` (2) distinct dismissals, never a P0 in security, data, or concurrency. A fix whose every P0–P2 finding was refuted or dismissed, with no unmet criterion, is lifted to merge with a note; the floors still downgrade it (rule 7). The comment lists what was not posted and why, and a collapsed review plan |
| D-4.8b.3 | WP4.8b: peer mode, two different-family reviewers cross-check before posting; general mode, one reviewer | The registry's review lane is one model; a delegated Codex PR is built by the review lane's own family (OpenAI) | `[inspect] mode` (general) and `delegated_mode` (general since D-4.8b.7, for PRs with a `delegate/<n>/state.json`), or `inspect --mode`. Reviewer A is the review lane's model; when its family equals the builder's and it is a registry default, the first of `verbs.PeerReviewCandidates` (GPT-6.1 Sol, Grok 4.7, Gemini 3.1 Pro, Claude Opus 5.5) of another family reviews instead, with a note (a configured one is refused). Reviewer B is `[inspect] peer_model` or the first candidate whose family differs from both. Each reviews the same input; each one's single-source findings go to the other with `VerifySystem`, an asymmetric "prove it wrong" check (after OpenCodeReview's filter, as an idea: refute only what the diff contradicts, never for low importance); refuted findings are dropped, except a P0 in a protected category, which is posted as disputed. Dispositions combine to the worse; a criterion is unmet when either says so. When B fails, the review falls back to A in general mode with a note. The builder's family for a delegated PR comes from the delegation state on the ledger |
| D-4.8b.4 | WP4.8b: posts structured, de-duplicated comments; requests fixes | A bot or the founder's account cannot request changes on the founder's own PRs (D2), and receivers act on review comments | One PR review per run with event COMMENT (never APPROVE or REQUEST_CHANGES) and one inline comment per new P0–P2 finding placed on a line, each with its ID marker, fix, and any dispute; the IDs posted go into inspect's state so a later cycle posts only new ones. The summary comment and its state stay replace-in-place. The summary speaks as the Engineering Manager twin (Kiran, `seats/engineering-manager/persona.toml`) with the evidence rule; findings stay in the schema |
| D-4.8b.5 | WP4.8b: evaluate with seeded-defect PRs, precision and recall, general vs peer, before and after, through the eval harness | The harness scores skills with a rubric judge; a reviewer is scored against known defects, and the review calls cost about 70 per run of the set | `[review]` cases in `evals/cases/inspect/`: twelve synthetic PRs (nine with planted defects of eight kinds, ten defects in all; three clean controls), generated as real `git diff` output, with the head's files stored as `<path>.txt` so this repository's build, linters, and scanners never compile or flag them. Tier 1 checks each case's construction (every defect on added lines). Tier 2 runs each case k = 2 times in three variants: `legacy` (the 0.1 prompt and schema, kept in `internal/evals/review.go`), `general`, and `peer`, four cases at a time, and scores what each would post: a finding within 3 lines of an unmatched defect in its file is a true positive, a second one a duplicate (false positive), any other a false positive; a run scores 1 + 4·F1 (a clean control 5 with no false positive, else 1). The results file gains per-variant precision, recall, F1, and clean-control false positives. The `review` group is advisory (never gates promotion) and runs only on `workflow_dispatch` (new group keys `k` and `events`; `cmd/eval` reads the workflow's `EVENT`), so `evals.yml` needs no change |
| D-5.5b.2 | WP5.5b: hand the receiving agent the Ready snapshot, plan, and evidence map; prefer the most portable trigger with a per-host adapter; the PR must reference the story | The receivers differ in transport: Cursor has a REST API that opens the PR; Amp's CLI starts an orb thread; claude-code-action answers an `@claude` comment but does not open PRs; Codex cloud has only the experimental `codex cloud exec` and a "Create PR" click (research §5). PRs from Cursor and Amp are opened as the human, so authorship identifies nothing | `internal/delegate`: one `Brief` for every receiver (the Ready snapshot's criteria from the ledger, the story body quoted as data, the plan's checkpoints, an evidence-map skeleton filled with the plan's tests, branch and PR rules with `Refs #n`, Conventional Commits and DCO, `[delegate] checks`, CODEOWNERS paths, the cycle cap, AGENTS.md abridged), passed through the actions log's redaction and a credential-shape filter; its SHA-256 is recorded. Adapters: `CursorAPI` (`POST /v1/agents` with `autoCreatePR`, `GET …/runs/{id}`, `POST …/runs`), `AmpCLI` (`amp -ox --project`, `amp threads continue`), `ClaudeMention` (an `@claude` comment on the story, then on the PR; aayu opens the PR from the pushed `claude/issue-<n>-…` branch), `CodexCloud` (`codex cloud exec`, `status`, and `diff`; aayu applies the returned diff in `.aayudha/worktrees/<n>-codex` and opens the PR through build's checkpoint, verify, and push). The PR is found by its PR URL, a `Refs #n` body or story branch, or the receiver's branch. Fixtures in `internal/delegate/testdata/` are synthetic, in the documented shapes; `AAYU_LIVE=1` tests skip without credentials |
| D-5.5b.3 | WP5.5b: the review loop ends at merge-ready or the cap; the receiving agent addresses the findings | inspect runs in the merge gate, not in `build`; there are no webhooks from Cursor v1, Amp, or Codex; inspect stops calling the model after two fix cycles | Each later `build <n> --delegate <r>` syncs: the receiver's status, the PR, and inspect's state from its PR comment. On `fix` for the current head it sends one follow-up per head (`delegate.FollowUpMessage`: the finding IDs, places, and suggested fixes) through the adapter, or, locally, runs the CLI on it in the worktree and pushes a checkpoint; on `merge` it reports merge-ready and names `aayu ship <pr>`; on `rebuild`, or when the head moved after inspect's cap, it pages a human and exits 4. The hand-off, follow-ups, and local runs are actions (`delegate.handoff`, `delegate.follow-up`, `delegate.local-run`); `delegate/<n>/state.json` and a replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:delegate -->` comment on the story record every step. The sync runs when the founder's host (or `/codex` … `/amp`) asks; a scheduled loop is later |
| D-5.5b.4 | WP5.5b: cloud by default, local fallback in an aayu worktree with a scrubbed environment and no merge rights | A local CLI runs as the user, whose HOME holds git and gh credentials | `--local`, or a receiver whose cloud agent is not connected (no Cursor key, no `codex` with `[delegate] codex_env`, no claude-code-action workflow, no `amp` with `AMP_API_KEY`), runs `codex exec --sandbox workspace-write`, `cursor-agent -p --force`, `claude -p --permission-mode acceptEdits`, or `amp -x` in `.aayudha/worktrees/<n>-<agent>`, under `[delegate] local_timeout_minutes`. Its environment keeps the toolchain variables and the receiver's own key only (no `GITHUB_*`, `GH_*`, `AAYU_*`, or other secret-named variable), with an empty `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, no git credential helper, and `GIT_SSH_COMMAND=false`, so it cannot push; aayu commits (`Aayudha-Delegate: <r>`), verifies, pushes, and opens the PR. Receivers in the cloud push through their own GitHub Apps; none can merge: `dev`'s rules and `ship`'s human pass hold |
| D-5.5b.5 | WP5.5b: environments generated from one source and kept in sync; a doctor check per receiver | Codex cloud environments are defined only in its UI; Cursor reads `.cursor/environment.json` from the default branch; Amp reads `.agents/setup`; claude-code-action runs in the repository's workflow (research §6) | `.agents/setup` is the source. `aayu doctor --fix` writes `.cursor/environment.json` (`install: bash .agents/setup`; other keys kept), committed here for `dev`. The `delegate-env` check reports, per receiver: source executable and its Go pin not older than `go.mod`; Amp native; Cursor drift; Codex manual (the exact Install script, internet preset, and `[delegate] codex_env`); Claude, whether a claude-code-action workflow runs `.agents/setup` first. aayu writes no `.github/` file for it. A repository without `.agents/setup` passes with "not set up" |
| D-5.5b.6 | WP5.5b: worktrees for everything that changes code, a view of parallel work, `gc` cleanup | `build` and `evolve` already cut worktrees; `polish` asked the human to check the PR head out in the current checkout; nothing listed work in flight | Audit: `build` (`.aayudha/worktrees/<n>`), `evolve` (`evolve-<skill>-<n>`), and the delegate local mode (`<n>-<agent>`) cut worktrees; `polish` off the PR head now prepares `.aayudha/worktrees/pr-<n>` on the PR's branch and says to apply the critique there (the critique directory is shared by the clone's worktrees); `review` writes no code. `aayu status --work` lists every aayu worktree (story, agent, branch, PR, age, uncommitted or unpushed work) and every open cloud delegation from the ledger. `gc` removes `<n>-<agent>` and `pr-<n>` worktrees of closed stories and PRs, as it does `<n>` |
| D-5.5b.7 | WP5.5b: pick the cheapest adequate receiver for the story's size and say why; slash commands through the host plugins | Prices change often, and a story's size is its Effort label | `--delegate auto`: XS and S go to the first connected of Codex (plan allowance, no VM charge), Claude Code, Amp, Cursor; M and L to Cursor (follow-up runs), Amp (threads), Claude Code, Codex; with none connected, the first local CLI; the reason (with the cost note) is in the result and the story comment. `/codex`, `/cursor`, `/claude`, `/amp` are `hosts/commands/*.md`, which the Claude Code, Codex, and Amp plugins (WP3.2b) load; each checks Ready (or takes a goal through `grill story` and `ready`), delegates, syncs on request, and stops at merge-ready or the cap, never running `ship` |
| D-4.8b.6 | WP4.8b accept: precision and recall for general vs peer mode, before and after, on a dispatched tier-2 run | Run 38690306973 (`evals.yml`, dispatch, group `review`, dev at 8dbe384, k = 2, 18.30 USD charged): ten planted defects and three clean controls, 24 runs per variant. Before (the 0.1 prompt): precision 0.55, recall 0.90, F1 0.68 (TP 18, FP 15, of which 5 on clean controls). General mode: precision 0.73, recall 0.95, F1 0.83 (TP 19, FP 7, of which 4 on clean controls). Peer mode: precision 0.46, recall 1.00, F1 0.63 (TP 18, FP 21, of which 2 on clean controls; 3 runs errored on gateway 524s from grok-4.7). Peer finds every planted defect and the fewest false positives on clean code, but the second reviewer (grok-4.7) raises extra single-source findings that the asymmetric check rarely refutes, so precision falls, at about five times general mode's cost. Every variant flags `clean-retry-backoff` twice | Reported as measured; the group stays advisory. General mode is the better default for every PR. Delegated PRs (`[inspect] delegated_mode`) also default to general since D-4.8b.7 (founder decision); peer stays available through `inspect --mode peer`. Follow-ups: a peer threshold (post a single-source finding of B only at P0–P1 or confidence ≥ 0.8), and a look at `clean-retry-backoff`'s two findings in case the control is ambiguous |
| D-4.8b.7 | WP4.8b/WP5.5b: delegated PRs reviewed in peer mode by default (`[inspect] delegated_mode = "peer"`) | The reviewer eval (run 38690306973, D-4.8b.6) measured general mode's precision at 0.73 against peer's 0.46, with recall 0.95 against 1.00, at about a fifth of peer's cost | Founder decision, 2026-10-15: `[inspect] delegated_mode = "general"` in `internal/config/defaults.toml`, so delegated PRs get one reviewer of a family different from the builder's, like every other PR. Peer stays available per run with `inspect --mode peer` or per repository with `delegated_mode = "peer"` |
| S-5.5c | WP5.5b: `/codex`, `/cursor`, `/claude`, `/amp` ship through the host plugins | Founder request (2026-10-16): when aayudha is a plugin in any host, the user calls another agent from inside it with as little friction as possible, and every pairing works | WP5.5c, in small PRs to `dev`: the host command matrix (this row's D-5.5c.1 to D-5.5c.3), then the brief and the receiver adapters, then cloud-by-default routing on the agent-environments work's detection |
| D-5.5c.1 | WP5.5b: the four hand-off commands load in the Claude Code and Codex plugin | Codex 0.163.0 drops a plugin command whose body uses `$ARGUMENTS`, `$<n>`, `{{…}}`, `` !` ``, or an `@mention` (codex-rs `core-plugins/src/command_migration.rs`), so before this change Codex offered only `home` and `next`; Claude Code and Cursor append the user's text when a command does not use `$ARGUMENTS` | The command sources use no `$ARGUMENTS`: the host appends what the user typed (Amp's plugin asks for it when the command has an `argument-hint` and appends it as `ARGUMENTS:`). `TestPluginManifests` fails on any of Codex's refused tokens; `TestHostPluginsLoad` checks that Codex lists `aayudha:source-command-{claude,cursor,amp}` |
| D-5.5c.2 | WP5.5c: no host offers a hand-off to itself | Claude Code and Codex shared one manifest, which loaded the whole `hosts/commands/` directory, so Claude Code offered `/aayudha:claude` and Codex `codex`. Codex reads `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` before `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, and Cursor reads `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json` first. Claude Code 2.1.301 lists no command given as a single file in `commands`, though its docs allow one, but it does load several directories | The sources move to `hosts/commands/common/` (`home`, `next`) and `hosts/commands/<agent>/<agent>.md`. Three manifests (`.claude-plugin`, `.codex-plugin`, `.cursor-plugin`) each list `common/` and every agent's directory except their own, with hooks for their host (`hosts/codex/hooks.json`, `hosts/cursor/hooks.json` in Cursor's format), and `.cursor-plugin/marketplace.json` lists the plugin. `install --host amp` gives the Amp plugin every command except `amp`. The pre-tool hook already allowed `aayu ready`, `grill --assess`, `build --delegate`, and `status --work`; a test now holds that on all four hosts |
| D-5.5c.3 | WP5.5c: prove every host loads the commands | Claude Code (`claude plugin details`), Codex (`codex debug prompt-input`), and Amp (`amp plugins list`) list a plugin's commands without signing in. The Cursor CLI (2026.10.14) has no headless way to list a plugin's commands, and every run that would show them needs a sign-in | `TestHostPluginsLoad` runs live on Claude Code 2.1.301, Codex 0.163.0, and Amp in the orb. Cursor's manifest is checked only against Cursor's documented schema (`TestPluginManifests`), so Cursor as a host is proven by golden files only until a signed-in Cursor CLI runs the test. Superseded in part by D-3.2b.q: even signed in, the Cursor CLI cannot list a plugin's skills or commands, so the test covers none of Cursor; its MCP server and hook are proven live only |
| D-5.5c.4 | WP5.5b: Claude Code's cloud transport is the `@claude` mention through claude-code-action | That action needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` as a repository secret, against the founder's rule of no AI keys in a product repository's CI. Claude Code on the web starts from the user's signed-in CLI: `claude --cloud "<task>"`, with follow-ups through `claude -p … --cloud <session>`; sessions push a branch and open no PR | `delegate.ClaudeCloud` replaces `ClaudeMention`: it runs `claude --cloud <brief>` in the repository, records the session ID and its `claude.ai/code` link, treats the branch the brief names as the signal (aayu opens the PR from it), and queues follow-ups on the session. Claude Code counts as cloud-connected when its CLI is on PATH; whether that CLI's claude.ai sign-in can start cloud sessions is checked by the agent-environments detection, which the routing PR adopts. `[delegate] claude_trigger` stays in the schema, unused, so 0.1 configurations still load. The fixtures are synthetic: no claude.ai sign-in was available |
| D-5.5c.5 | WP5.5b: the local receiver keeps its own key | `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` are generic provider variables that often hold a model gateway's credential; `codex exec` reads `CODEX_API_KEY` (or its saved login), and Claude Code's print mode prefers `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` over the user's subscription when it is set | The scrubbed environment passes only variables that belong to the receiver's own CLI: `CODEX_API_KEY`, `CURSOR_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `AMP_API_KEY`. Provider keys and base URLs never pass, so a receiver runs on its user's own sign-in and never on a gateway credential |
| D-5.5c.6 | WP5.5c: every hand-off gets a fresh worktree (local) or branch (cloud); the receiver breaks the work down, uses its own subagents, verifies the Completion Goal with evidence, and returns what it proved; the calling host reports it | A re-hand-off after a failure reused `.aayudha/worktrees/<n>-<agent>` and the story branch. Receivers report in different places (a PR body, a CLI's final message) | `delegate/<n>/state.json` counts hand-offs. Hand-off *k* > 1 uses `story/<n>-<slug>-<k>` and `.aayudha/worktrees/<n>-<agent>-<k>`, and a local hand-off skips a number an old worktree or remote branch still holds; syncs keep their hand-off's branch and worktree. The brief adds "How to work and report": break the work down by checkpoint, use your own subagents, verify every criterion with evidence before you report, and write a `## What I proved` section (PR body in the cloud, final message locally), with the line that the merge gate's proof decides. `build --delegate` reads that section from the PR body or the CLI's output, keeps it on the ledger, reports it as the receiver's preview (`delegation.proved`), and quotes it in the PR body aayu opens. It is untrusted text, never a gate |
| D-5.5c.7 | WP5.5c: a receiver never gets merge rights | A local receiver can run `aayu` itself, and so could hand the story on to a third agent | `build --delegate` refuses (exit 4) when `AAYU_DELEGATED` is set, which the scrubbed environment sets for every local receiver; the brief tells cloud receivers not to hand the story on |
| D-5.5c.8 | WP5.5b: a receiver whose cloud agent is not connected falls back to its local CLI | Founder rule (2026-10-16): cloud is the default and the local CLI is an explicit switch the user chooses; a missing receiver is said plainly with its install and sign-in steps, and a connected route is offered | `build --delegate <r>` without `--local` refuses (exit 4) when `r`'s cloud agent is not connected, and says what is missing for `r` (cloud agent, CLI) and what is available: `--local` when its CLI is installed, `--delegate <x>` for every connected cloud agent. `--local` without the CLI says so and offers the cloud agent or another installed CLI. `auto` picks among cloud agents only, or among local CLIs only with `--local`. A sync keeps its hand-off's mode. aayu never installs or signs in for the user. The install and sign-in commands per receiver come from `agentenv.Detect` (D-5.5c.10) |
| D-5.5c.9 | WP5.5c: a goal in words goes through grill's vagueness gate before any hand-off | The gate (WP5.8, PR #226) keeps `aayu grill story --assess --idea "<goal>" --json` as its entry point and adds `gate.verdict` (`unpack` or `pass`) with `gate.reasons` | The four hand-off commands run the assessment, follow grill's gate on `unpack` (each reason named, the riskiest question first, a read-back of done before anything is created), create the story from the user's own words on `pass`, and hand off only after `aayu ready n` passes. `build --delegate` refuses a story that is not Ready, so the CLI enforces the gate whatever a host's model does |
| D-5.5c.10 | WP5.5c: if the cloud environment is missing or stale, set it up and verify it, then hand off; detect which CLIs and apps are installed (the agent-environments work owns both) | `agentenv` (PR #240) provides `Detect` (offline: PATH, version, each CLI's own sign-in status, aayu's secret store, the recorded Amp project) and `Ensure` (regenerate and verify in a clean container, about 4–6 minutes for this repository with Docker; `ErrNotLanded` when the verified files are not on the default branch) | `build --delegate` decides cloud availability from `Detect` (Codex: a ChatGPT login and `[delegate] codex_env`; Cursor: its API key; Claude Code: a claude.ai sign-in; Amp: signed in with a recorded project) and local readiness from the CLI being installed and signed in, or holding its own key. The missing-receiver message prints `Detect`'s install and sign-in commands and cloud detail verbatim. A fresh cloud hand-off calls `Ensure` for the receiver first: a failure, or `ErrNotLanded` with its exact git command, stops the hand-off (exit 4) and offers `--local`. Syncs do not re-run it. Tests inject `Detect`'s probes and `Ensure` |
| D-5.5c.11 | WP5.5c: the Claude Code and Codex local receivers are proven live, not only with fixtures | Founder request (2026-10-16): prove it on the megaman runner (Ubuntu 26.04, `dev` at `b629966`, Claude Code 2.1.301, Codex 0.162.1) with the founder's own AI gateway instead of a claude.ai or ChatGPT sign-in. `ScrubEnv` passes no gateway variable or base URL to a receiver (D-5.5c.5), so the test route was each CLI's own configuration in a throwaway `HOME`: `.claude/settings.json` `env` (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`), and `.codex/config.toml` with a custom `model_providers` entry (`wire_api = "responses"`, the token inline) plus `codex login --with-api-key`. This proves the hand-off mechanics, not a real user's sign-in. **Claude Code:** `AAYU_LIVE=1 go test ./internal/delegate -run Live -v` passes `TestLiveLocalReceivers/claude` (it wrote `LIVE_SMOKE.md`; "1. proved: I ran `od -c LIVE_SMOKE.md` …"). `aayu build 2 --delegate claude --local` on a Ready story in a private scratch repository made a fresh worktree `.aayudha/worktrees/2-claude` on `story/2-add-mathx-sum`. The receiver's prompt hashed to the recorded brief SHA-256 (`79429dc…`), and the receiver wrote `mathx/sum.go`, a table test, and `.aayudha/evidence/2.toml`. aayu committed checkpoint 1/1 (`Aayudha-Delegate: claude`), pushed it, and opened PR #4, quoting the receiver's "What I proved": "1. not proved: … `go test` was blocked waiting for approval". verify held. **Codex:** `TestLiveLocalReceivers/codex` fails, and `build 3 --delegate codex --local` exits 4 with "nothing to commit yet". The model answered through the gateway, but Codex's Linux sandbox cannot start: `bwrap: setting up uid map: Permission denied`, or "requires bubblewrap" with the legacy Landlock flag. megaman sets `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1` and has no `/usr/bin/bwrap`, the only path the shipped AppArmor profile `bwrap-userns-restrict` allows. **Both:** `TestHostPluginsLoad` passes for Claude Code, Codex, and Amp. `doctor`'s agents row on megaman reads "local CLIs signed in: amp" (codex and claude not signed in), and with the throwaway `HOME`, "local CLIs signed in: codex, claude; cloud agents connected: none". Cloud routines (`/schedule`), Codex cloud tasks, and Cursor are out of scope: the gateway cannot provide them | Evidence only: no product change, and `ScrubEnv` is unchanged. Codex's local receiver counts as proven once a host with a working sandbox (megaman after `apt install bubblewrap`) passes `TestLiveLocalReceivers/codex` and a `--delegate codex --local` hand-off. The scratch repository and the throwaway configuration were deleted. The run found five open problems, not fixed here. (1) `LocalArgs` gives Claude Code `--permission-mode acceptEdits`, which denies Bash in print mode, so a local Claude receiver cannot run the tests that D-5.5c.6 asks it to verify with. (2) The brief's evidence placeholder `"<package path>:<TestName>"` led the receiver to write `./mathx:TestSum/positive`. `verify.TestEvidence` matches neither a `./` path nor a subtest, so it reported "did not pass in this run" for tests that passed. (3) `delegate.Proved` takes the first `## What I proved` in the CLI's combined output. `codex exec` echoes the prompt, so for Codex it returns the brief's own instruction (the live test logged `1. proved: <how you checked …>`). (4) A local receiver that exits 0 with no change ends `build` with exit 4 and drops the receiver's final message and the delegation state, so Codex's "not proved" report was lost. (5) A host gap, not aayu's: megaman has no semgrep, gitleaks, or osv-scanner, so `verify`'s sast, secrets, and deps floors block there |
| D-5.5c.12 | WP3.2b, WP5.5c: a person installs the plugin and uses aayudha from inside Claude Code and Codex | Founder request (2026-10-16): the same megaman run and gateway route as D-5.5c.11, driven headless (`claude -p`, `codex exec`) on a private scratch repository. The person ran `aayu init`, `aayu install --host claude --apply` and `--host codex --apply`, `claude plugin marketplace add` and `plugin install aayudha@aayudha`, and `codex plugin marketplace add` and `plugin add aayudha@aayudha`, from this repository's marketplace. Claude Code got `Bash(aayu:*)` and the aayudha MCP tools. Codex ran with `-s danger-full-access`, because its sandbox cannot start on megaman (D-5.5c.11). **Results (Claude Code / Codex).** *Install:* pass / pass. `plugin details` lists 35 skills (`amp`, `codex`, `cursor`, no `claude`), the hooks `SessionStart, PreToolUse`, and `plugin:aayudha:aayudha … ✔ Connected`. `debug prompt-input` lists `aayudha:*` and `source-command-{claude,cursor,amp,home,next}`, with no `codex`. *MCP:* pass (the agent called `mcp__aayudha__grill`, `status`, and `doctor`) / partial (the server answered resource and template listings, but the model was offered no aayudha tools; cause unverified). *home and next:* pass / pass (`aayu grill story`: nothing is waiting). *Vague ask* ("Make the CLI simple and fast."): pass / fail. Claude Code ran `grill --assess` (verdict `unpack`, reasons `simple` and `fast`), asked four questions riskiest first, read back three done criteria, then filed the Goal, Initiative, Feature, and Story and passed `ready --check` after the human's answers. Codex given the same words edited `main.go` directly without aayudha. Given "Using aayudha: …", it ran `--assess` (`unpack`), but then took the open Story #4 without asking, ran `aayu ready 4`, and built it to a PR. *Concrete ask:* pass / pass (verdict `pass`, no questions, Stories #6 and #7 Ready). *status and doctor:* pass / pass (doctor blocks on git-lfs missing on megaman). *`install --env` plan:* pass / pass. *`install --loops` plan:* fail / fail until the person copied `loops/` in (then pass / pass). *Guard rail:* pass (`aayu ship 5`, `install --apply`, and `release` refused: "aayu ship is for a person, not an agent (a human-only verb)") / fail by default: the hook did not run, and `aayu ship 5` ran, exiting 4 only for want of a merge-gate pack. It is refused once hook trust is bypassed (`--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`). *Cross-agent:* Codex → Claude Code pass: #7 went to `.aayudha/worktrees/7-claude`, the brief SHA-256 `ab72d79…` matched the receiver's prompt, and PR #8 quotes "1. not proved: … `go test ./mathx` was blocked by the approval prompt".
