Cursor's plugin is proven by `TestPluginManifests` (its manifest), the golden install trees, and the live megaman run for its MCP server and hook only; whether Cursor loads the plugin's skills and commands is unproven. A live subtest of the MCP server and hook would need a Cursor sign-in and a model call on every run, so it is not added |
| D-5.3b.g | WP5.3b: after D-5.3b.f, the founder (2026-10-15) accepted the results and asked for one more round, then stop: per-topic scripted answers, a skill that never decides an unconfirmed detail, a narrower caching rule, the conversation cases in their own advisory group, and dispatches that a push cannot replace | Run 38687921977 (after D-5.3b.f) showed three causes. The scripted human answered by dimension, so a follow-up on the same dimension got the same reply and every `request` or `other` clarification ("depend on #14?", "split this L story?") was parked. The skill decided details the human left open (the confirmation on `/order/review`) and once tagged a criterion with a dimension it never asked about. The search case's `\bcach` rule also matched "cold cache", a test condition. And `evals.yml`'s one concurrency group per ref let later pushes replace a pending dispatch, twice | Topics: every catalogue dimension lists `Topics` (kebab keys, parsed strictly), and the skill tags each question `<dimension>/<topic>`, a request topic (`request/dependency`, `request/size`, `request/existing-behaviour`, `request/parent`, `request/scope`), or `other/<key>`; the turn schema requires the topic. `human.toml` has `[topics."<dimension>/<key>"]` answers, `[dimensions."<dimension>"]` fallbacks, and the global fallback (park or defer); the bakery human now answers where the confirmation goes, where options go, the #14 dependency, the story's size, and existing payment, and the search human a cold cache. Provenance names the topic (`(ux.steps/screen-count)`); a criterion from a topic or dimension never asked fails tier 1 and the tier-2 floor. Placements: `grill.Check` (so `grill`, `ready`, and both tiers) refuses a grilled criterion whose expected result places something (a backticked route, "on the review screen", "above", "below", "next to", "at the top of", …) unless that word is in the criterion's quoted human words or the request; the skill says never to decide where something appears, what it is called, its order, or a number without the human, and to defer or park it instead. The search rule now matches result caching only (`cache … results`, `results cach…`, `cache per query`, memoizing); the recorded search run measures with a cold cache and passes (TestGrillSearchScopeCaching). Group `conversations` (the two cases, 20 USD, `gate = "advisory"` until a run meets the bar; like brief, not on pushes to `dev`); `skills` back to 6 USD and no longer holds them. `evals.yml`: a dispatch's concurrency group carries its run ID, so a push or schedule cannot replace it; pushes and the schedule keep the shared per-ref group and `cancel-in-progress: false`, so a running promotion run is never cut short. Tests: TestTier1GrillConversation (unasked topic, unasked dimension, unconfirmed placement), TestTier2GrillConversation (answers by topic, dimension, fallback), TestTopicsAndPlacements, TestCheck, TestGroups. The founder's instruction is one round and then stop, whatever the dispatched result |
| D-5.3b.h | WP5.3b and D-5.3b.g: placement checks refuse a criterion whose place word is not in its quoted human words; WP3.4: tier 2 runs nightly, after relevant merges to `dev`, on promotion, and on dispatch | Run 38692692002 scored search-fast 4.8 and bakery-ux 2.8: both 1s were placement refusals of criteria that said "on that screen", "on the same screen", or "on the review screen" without quoting a reply naming it. The founder (2026-10-15) kept `grill.Check` strict and asked the skill to quote the reply instead, and asked that the advisory `conversations` and `personas` groups (about 13 and 20 USD a run) stop running nightly: on demand, and on pushes to `dev` or pull requests that change grill or persona files. Tier 2 needs the `ai-gateway` environment, which only `dev`, `rolling`, and `main` may use, so it cannot run on a pull request | Skill only: `skills/grill/SKILL.md` (outside the persona block) gains "Placement quotes": a criterion that says where something is quotes every human reply naming that place, even one given to another question (`"… on the same screen" / "… on the review screen"`), names the place instead of "that screen" or "this page", and leaves an unconfirmed place out with a `deferred:` open question; `references/story.md` and `bug.md` say the same. `TestCheck` adds a criterion whose placements come from two quoted replies (accepted) and one that says "on that screen" (refused). `conversations` and `personas` get `events = ["workflow_dispatch", "push"]`, so the schedule never runs them; `evals.yml` runs them on a push only to `dev` and only when the push changes their files: for conversations `skills/grill/`, `evals/cases/grill/`, `internal/grill/`, `internal/evals/conversation.go`; for personas `seats/*/persona.toml`, `internal/seats/persona.go` (it renders the persona blocks), `evals/cases/retro/` and `decide/`, `internal/evals/persona.go`. Pushes to `rolling` and `main` skip them too (neither gates). A pull request gets tier 1 in `go test`; its tier 2 runs on the push to `dev` that merges it. `brief`, `brief-plain`, and `skills` keep their schedule. Test: TestGroups (events of both groups) |

### Deviations recorded while fixing Bugs #170–#173, #177, and #183 (limits found while writing How it works)

Rows are named by the WP whose behaviour each Bug corrects.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-7.6d | WP7.6 accept: re-running audit with the same recorded inputs produces a byte-identical report; WP1.9: files an earlier binary wrote stay readable | Bug #183: the inputs snapshot recorded `config_hash` over the whole effective configuration, and `--replay` refused any difference, so every new key ([team] in WP5.1b, [ui] in WP1.1b) made every earlier report unreplayable (exit 4), though audit reads none of them. A whole-configuration hash cannot be recomputed by a binary with another configuration schema | New reports record `audit-config:sha256:<…>`, the hash of only what `audit` and `retro` read: `[identity] bot_logins`, `[decisions] sla_hours`, `[time]`, `[autonomy] correction_days`, `never`, `streak`, `[proof] media`, `media_secret`, `retention_days`, `[budget] per_story_usd`, and the dev branch; a replay refuses (exit 4) when that differs. A legacy `sha256:` hash is not recomputed: the replay renders the recorded hash, warns on stderr that it replays with the current values of those sections, and the byte comparison with the report is the check. Tested by `TestAuditReplaysRecordedReports` (the 12 October 06:35 and 15 October 04:35 UTC reports replay byte-identically from `internal/cli/testdata/audit-replay`: this repository's ledger up to the 15 October report as a 300 kB git bundle, with the config and `LAYERS.md` the replay reads) and `TestAuditRerunByteIdentical` (a `[ui] theme` change leaves the replay byte-identical; a `[decisions] sla_hours` change refuses it). A key audit starts reading must join `auditConfigHash` |
| D-1.6b | WP1.6 and D-1.6a: the `[budget]` breaker trips at `per_story_usd` or `per_day_usd` | Bug #177: `Breaker.Seed` was never called, so every `aayu` process started at zero and the limits bounded one process's spend; only the pause file carried a trip across processes. The actions log is per clone (`.aayudha/telemetry/actions.jsonl`, shared by its worktrees), and a CI run starts from a fresh clone | `Env.Gateway` seeds the breaker from the clone's actions log through `Breaker.SeedLog` before any call: every logged record with a cost counts toward its story and its local day in `[time] tz`, so per-day counts today's records and per-story the story's. A CI run's breaker still sees only that run's spend, and a host's own model spend inside a skill stays invisible to it (docs/guide/how-models.md). Tested by `TestBudgetBreakerAcrossProcesses` (two `aayu triage` child processes, each the test binary run as the CLI, sharing one state directory and calling a fake model endpoint through `[models.gateway]` that reports each call's cost: 0.60 + 0.60 USD on one day trips `per_day_usd = 1` with exit 6, the pause file, and one page in the `file` outbox; on one story it trips `per_story_usd = 1`; 0.90 USD yesterday does not count today) and the `TestBudgetBreaker` cases for a missing log, a malformed one, and one that cannot be read (a directory) |
| D-4.8c | WP4.8, D-4.8a, and WP4.8b: `inspect` reviews with a model whose family ≠ the builder's; the builder's family from the delegation record, else the pack's `model` (the `Aayudha-Model` trailer), else the build lane of the pack's host | Bug #171: the trailer is written into the PR's own commits, so for every PR not delegated the different-family rule rested on what the builder said about itself, and a hand-made commit can carry any trailer. The actions log is per clone and absent from the merge gate's fresh checkout. A host-managed lane (Amp) records no model. The first fix, written before inspect v2 (#186) merged, was reworked onto it | `build` writes each checkpoint's model into its ledger record (`CheckpointRecord.Model`). `inspect` lists the PR's commits and calls `inspect.BuilderOf` with the delegation record and the checkpoint models: the family is known only when a delegation covers the PR, or a checkpoint names a model family for every commit, the records name one family, and the trailer, if any, agrees. Otherwise it is unknown, and the records', the trailer's, and (with no record) the build lane's families are candidates; `reviewerModels` (WP4.8b) takes the candidates, so the first reviewer and the peer are each of none of them, or inspect exits 4. The verdict's first note names the family and its source, marks the trailer "self-reported, unverified", and calls a family from a build record "attributed, not verified": the record holds what `build` resolved (`AAYU_MODEL` or the lane), and without a bot App the founder's token, which agents use here, can write the ledger. inspect's first review of this fix (cycle 1) found both: an agreeing builder-controlled record presented as verified, and a checkpoint without a model counted as covering its commit. Tested by `TestInspectBuilderFamily` (a trailer naming anthropic over a record naming openai is reviewed by `grok-4.7`; no record names the lane's family; an honest PR is reviewed by `gpt-6.1-sol` with the record as source), `TestBuilderOf` (partial and model-less records, two families, delegation, a trailer contradicting a delegation), and the checkpoint model assertion in `TestBuildTextDryRunPlanAndBudget` |
| D-5.7b | WP5.7 and D-5.7a: `aayu halt` sets a repository variable every workflow checks; every job in every aayudha workflow starts with the halt step | Bug #173: `floors.yml`, `inspect-live.yml`, and `screen-reader.yml` had no halt step, and neither had the merge gate's `aayudha/inspect` and `aayudha/pack` jobs (in this repository and in the `init --gates` template), which D-5.7a counted as covered; nothing checked the rule, so a new workflow could drop it unnoticed | Those six jobs (and the template's two) start with the same halt step as the rest. `TestEveryWorkflowJobStartsWithHaltCheck` (repository root, run by CI's `build` job) parses every `.github/workflows/*.yml` and `internal/initrepo/*.yml` and fails naming each workflow and job whose first step is not the halt check (name `Halt check…`, `if: vars.AAYUDHA_HALT != ''`, a script that exits), then runs each halt script in bash with the variable set and requires a failing exit and the halt message. It covers `.yaml` files too. Live: on 2026-10-15 the three workflows from this branch ran on a scratch repository (`rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-halt-1792045632`, created and deleted with the admin token, since the default token cannot create repositories): with `AAYUDHA_HALT` set every job (floors-web, floors-dast, inspect-live, both screen-reader readers) failed at the halt check and skipped every later step; after it was deleted the halt check was skipped and every job succeeded. The jobs API records are in `testdata/workflow-halt`, and `TestHaltedWorkflowRuns` checks them against the current workflows (inspect's first review asked for this evidence) |
| D-7.1b | WP4.2: retention TTL from config and `aayu proof purge`; WP7.1 and D-7.1a: loops honour `halt` and `[budget]`, and `init --gates` renders a product's loops when it has `loops/` | Bug #172: no loop, workflow, or template ran `proof purge`, so media retention (V3, personal data) depended on a person. A product without `loops/` got no loops at all. Purging writes the ledger, so in Actions it needs the bot App (D1), and a bucket purge needs the media credential, which no loop job received | A default loop, `loops.Defaults["proof-purge"]` (`command = ["proof", "purge"]`, daily at 03:40 in `[time] tz`, `bot = true`): `init --gates` writes `loops/proof-purge.toml` into a checkout that lacks it and renders it with the checkout's loops, so every product gets the Actions job, the Amp schedule, and the Cursor automation; aayudha commits the same file, so `go generate` renders it here. A new loop key `secrets` names aayu secrets the Actions job passes as `AAYU_SECRET_<NAME>` (the default passes `proof-media`; a product whose `[proof] media_secret` differs edits it). Each run starts with `aayu hook loop:proof-purge` (halt exits 4, the pause file and the minutes budget exit 6) and `proof purge` refuses while halted too; retention is `[proof] retention_days`. Without an App the Actions job skips with a notice, as the `decisions` loop does, so on this repository only a host schedule can run it. Media behind an `oidc` bucket credential (D-7.2b.2a) cannot be purged by the Actions job, which has no `id-token: write`; a host schedule or the bucket's lifecycle rule covers it. Tested by `TestInitGatesRendersProofPurge` (a scratch checkout without `loops/`, and this repository's generated workflow) and `TestLoopProofPurge` (the job's guard and command as the bot against a fake S3: a June pack's screenshot deleted and its pack tombstoned, an October pack untouched, and, halted, exit 4 at the guard and the command with nothing purged) |

### Deviations recorded while fixing the stable gate's cross-OS smoke tests (before v0.1.0)

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-0.4g | WP0.4 and D-0.4a: the stable gate's `smoke` job runs every unit test on Ubuntu, macOS, and Windows | Stable gate run 38742897657 on `dev` failed on macOS and Windows. Three product defects: `status --work` compared `git worktree list`'s resolved, forward-slash paths with the unresolved checkout, so it listed no worktree on macOS (`/var` is `/private/var`) or Windows (a WP5.5b regression); the floor sandbox's `lookPath` accepted only `.exe` on Windows, so a tool installed as a `.cmd` shim (npm's `lighthouse` and `impeccable`) never resolved, and `node_modules/.bin/<tool>` resolved to the POSIX script; and the ledger lock treated Windows' "access denied" on a lock file another writer is deleting as an error, so concurrent writers failed. The rest were tests that assumed Unix: `syscall.Kill`, shell stand-in tools without a Windows extension, Unix permission bits, `/`-separated or drive-less paths, LF in a `core.autocrlf` worktree, a Windows path in a TOML basic string, and case-sensitive variable names. `internal/cli` is not hung on Windows: it takes about 880 s (`TestReleaseFailsClosed` 336 s), since git starts about ten times slower than on Linux, past `go test`'s 10-minute default | Product: `status --work` resolves both sides (`filepath.EvalSymlinks`, which also expands 8.3 names such as `RUNNER~1`); floor tools resolve through `exec.LookPath` per PATH entry, so PATHEXT applies on Windows; the ledger lock retries on "access denied" on Windows until its timeout. The smoke job runs `go test -timeout 30m`. Tests take the OS's paths and line endings, and Windows stand-in tools are `.cmd` files that run the shell script with Git for Windows' `sh`. Not supported on Windows, by test and here: `examples/benefits-qa/deploy/serve.sh` is POSIX `sh` that tracks the app by Unix pid, so its tests are `//go:build unix`; the benefits-qa interrupt test sends itself SIGINT, which Windows cannot (Ctrl+C reaches the same `signal.NotifyContext`), so it is Unix only. Different on Windows, asserted only on Unix: the age secrets file and identity have no `0600` mode, they take their directory's ACL (under the user profile: the user, SYSTEM, and Administrators), and aayu sets no ACL, so a `HOME` or `XDG_*` directory outside the profile shares its access; a floor's child sees one of `HTTPS_PROXY` and `https_proxy`, since names are case-insensitive; and an actions log path under a file reads as missing (Windows reports the path not found), as it does when the log is absent |
| D-0.4h | D-0.4f: with the `live-tests` token, the stable gate's `extended-integration` job runs every live test green | Run 38754333645 on `dev` failed two live tests that pass from a workstation with the same classic token; re-running only that job passed both, so neither was the CI credential. `TestLive` (internal/github) counted on GitHub's default labels for a second page of five, and a new repository sometimes has none yet. `TestReleaseLive`'s `release --to main` landed `main`, then GitHub refused the `v0.1.0` tag push with a bare `[remote rejected] v0.1.0 -> v0.1.0 (failed)`. The "Bypassed rule violations … creations being restricted" lines above it are GitHub's notice that the admin role's bypass was used, not the refusal. `(failed)` carries no reason; it is the answer GitHub gives for a lost race on a ref, which the ledger writer already retries (D-2.4a), and the re-run's tag push succeeded, so it was transient. This repository's `release-tags` ruleset matches the test's (creation, update, deletion, non-fast-forward; bypass: the admin role, always), and the founder's token reports `current_user_can_bypass: always`, so v0.1.0's tag push is admitted the same way | `TestLive` creates six labels, more than a page without defaults (`testdata/smoke.json` re-recorded). Every `release` push (candidate, landing, tag, candidate deletion; each idempotent) is retried up to three times, a poll interval apart, when the remote answers `(failed)` or `cannot lock ref`; any other refusal fails at once as before. `TestReleaseRetriesALockedRef`: a pre-receive hook leaves the tag's ref locked for the first push only, and `release --to rolling` still lands and tags |

### Scope change and deviations recorded while implementing WP4.2b (least-friction proof, 0.2)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-4.2b | V1 and §0.1: CI acts as a bot (App token) and writes packs to `ledger`; V3: proof media in Git LFS on private repositories, in a private bucket on public ones; M4: "ledger ops with LFS"; WP1.7: LFS quota; WP2.5 (V3 enforcement): `proof push` refuses media on a public repository until a bucket is set; WP2.6: `--integrations` offers the bucket for V3; WP4.1: artifacts `lfs\|bucket`; WP4.2: LFS by extension, `proof purge`, tombstones, CI packs authoritative; WP4.8: the merge gate "writes the authoritative pack to `ledger`"; WP7.2b.2: buckets with purge and tombstones; R5, R11, J2-1, J3-4; D1, D-2.5a, D-4.1a, D-4.2a, D-4.8a, D-4.10a, D-M4.5a, D-7.1b, D-7.2b.2a, D-7.2b.3a, as far as they describe the bot App writing proof, LFS media, purge, or tombstones | Founder decision (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): proof must be the least-friction design that still keeps the rule that the builder cannot certify its own work. A product repository needs no bot App, no media bucket, no secrets, and no purge job for proof. The plan's design needed an App installation token in CI (D1: the workflow token can never write `ledger`), media storage the team pays for and purges, and a daily purge loop that itself needed the App (D-7.1b) | The merge gate (`pull_request_target`, base-branch copy) builds the pack from what CI ran on the PR head, as before, and keeps `pack.json` with every artifact as the run artifact `aayudha-pack` for `[proof] retention_days`, clamped to the repository's Actions limit (`GITHUB_RETENTION_DAYS`, else 90). Job `aayudha/pack` (workflow token, `contents: read`, `pull-requests: write`, no App, no secret) runs the new `aayudha proof comment <story> --ci`: it re-checks the pack against the PR head and the Ready snapshot and upserts one `<!-- aayudha:proof -->` PR comment with the criteria verdict table, the floor table, the SHA-256 of `pack.json`, a link to the artifact, and the date it expires; `aayudha/pack` fails with it. CI writes nothing to `ledger`. `aayu ship` (a human) finds the pack through the Actions API (D-4.2b.a), checks every artifact's bytes against `pack.json`, and only after the human pass, just before the merge, writes `proof/<story>/<head>/authoritative/pack.json` and its data artifacts (text, never media) plus the ship record (`gate_run`, `artifact`, `pack_sha256`) under the human's identity. A refused ship writes nothing. Agent previews (`proof push`, any host conversation) stay advisory (`<!-- aayudha:proof-advisory -->`), `proof push --ci` is refused, and no preview satisfies ship. Media never go to `ledger` or Git LFS; LFS stays only for design manifests and review baselines on private repositories (WP6). V1's bot identity remains for agent lanes; it is no longer needed for proof. V3's intent (no media in git, bounded retention) holds, by a different store: the run artifact. The V-table rows are unchanged in text; this row records where V1's CI column, V3's storage column, §0.1's CI row, and M4 no longer apply |
| D-4.2b.a | S-4.2b: ship verifies that the artifact came from the merge-gate run on the exact head | The workflow-runs API gives a run's `path`, `event`, `head_sha`, `head_branch`, and conclusion, but nothing that binds it to the pull request's base branch: `pull_requests` lists the open pull requests whose head matches (PR 3 into `dev` on the scratch run 38762241501) and is empty once they close (run 38752748439 of this repository), so a second PR from the same head into another base would appear there too. Artifacts cannot be attached to a run from outside it; only the run's own jobs upload them | `gatePack` (`internal/cli/ship.go`) lists runs of `aayudha-merge-gate.yml` for the PR's head branch, newest first, and accepts the first with path `.github/workflows/aayudha-merge-gate.yml`, event `pull_request_target` (so the workflow file is the base branch's, never the PR's), `head_sha` = the PR head, and `completed/success`, whose `aayudha-pack` artifact is unexpired, unzips without escaping paths, verifies every hash, and is for this story, PR, and head. A refusal (exit 4) names every run it passed over and why. `aayudha/pack` joins ship's required checks. Residuals, in the threat model (T4): the base-branch copy is trusted, so a change to the gate itself must land through review first; PR code runs inside the gate job (sandboxed, D-4.8a) before the upload |
| D-4.2b.b | S-4.2b: retention from `[proof] retention_days` clamped to GitHub's limit | GitHub caps artifact retention at the repository's (or organisation's) setting, at most 90 days for public repositories and 400 for private ones, and exposes it to a run as `GITHUB_RETENTION_DAYS`. An artifact of a public repository can be downloaded by any signed-in GitHub user | `aayu verify --ci` reports `retention_days` = `proof.ArtifactRetention(retention_days, $GITHUB_RETENTION_DAYS)` (0 or above the limit → the limit; no limit known → 90); the gate passes it to `actions/upload-artifact` and the PR comment shows the expiry date. A ship after the artifact expired is refused: re-run the gate. On a public repository media are as visible as the PR for that window, which V3 did not allow; captures use `[verify] seed` data and the redaction pass (WP4.5) as before. The founder accepted this (D-4.2b.g) |
| D-4.2b.c | WP4.2, D-4.2a, D-7.1b, D-7.2b.2a: `proof purge`, tombstones, the `proof-purge` default loop, and media delete | Nothing writes media that need purging any more: the artifact expires on its own, and the ledger copy holds text only. A bucket costs little to keep (push, get, verify, lifecycle are separate from purge) | Removed: `proof purge`, `proof.Purge`, `Media.Delete` (S3, Azure Blob, GCS), `loops.Defaults` and the `secrets` loop key, `loops/proof-purge.toml` and its Amp and Cursor renders, tombstones on new packs, the LFS media path of `proof push`. Kept: `[proof] media` as opt-in for longer retention (`proof push --media` without a bucket now exits 4 on every repository); `proof verify`, `get`, and `list` still read 0.1 packs, including tombstoned ones and `store: lfs` (`TestVerifyReadsPacksFromBefore02`); `audit` reads the same `[proof]` keys, so `TestAuditReplaysRecordedReports` stays byte-identical. New `proof.VerifyLocal` checks a local pack's hashes (a local pack has `store: none`, which plain `Verify` skipped) |
| D-4.2b.d | D1 and WP1.4: `init --identity` replaces the ledger ruleset's admin bypass with the bot App; WP2.5: `.lfsconfig` for media on private ledgers | Ship writes the ledger under the human's identity, so replacing the admin bypass would lock ship out | `ledgerBypassApp` keeps the repository admin role and adds the App beside it. Only a repository admin could ship (the ledger push was refused otherwise) until D-4.2b.h widened it to the write role. `init` still writes the LFS attributes on a private ledger, now for design and baseline media only; the public-repository config scaffold describes the bucket as optional |
| D-4.2b.e | WP1.7: doctor checks the LFS quota | LFS no longer holds proof media | `doctor` gains a `proof` row: the effective artifact retention, a warning for a leftover `loops/proof-purge.toml` (runs a removed verb), and a warning when the merge gate is a 0.1 copy without `aayudha proof comment` (re-run `aayu init --gates`). The LFS quota row now names design and baseline media |
| D-4.2b.f | S-4.2b: prove it end to end on a scratch repository: a PR gets the artifact and the comment, ship refuses a pack from another SHA or a hand-uploaded artifact, and ship succeeds on the real one | GitHub has no API to attach an artifact to a run from outside it, so "hand-uploaded" means an artifact uploaded by a workflow the PR controls: the PR's own copy of the gate file, run on `pull_request`. The scratch repository has no model key, so `aayudha/inspect` cannot review | `TestLiveShipFromGateArtifact` (`internal/cli/proof_live_test.go`; `AAYU_LIVE=1`, `AAYU_LIVE_VERSION` = the commit the scratch gate installs; `AAYU_LIVE_KEEP=1` keeps the repository) on a public aayu-scratch-* repository with the `init --gates` merge gate and no App, bucket, or secret; the inspect step alone is an `echo`. Run on 2026-10-15 against commit 93f54c6, kept as `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-1792079559982603691`: (1) run 38764241082 on 90707dc kept artifact 11988095989 (expires 2027-01-13, the public limit of 90 days) and one PR comment (issuecomment-6196401723, all floors pass, pack hash, artifact link); (2) after a failing commit, run 38765027029 failed and ship exited 4: "run 38765027029: is failure on the head; run 38764241082: proves 90707dc, not the head 801b5da"; (3) the PR added `pull_request` to the gate file and its copy restored the passing code before `verify`: run 38765900626 (`pull_request`) went green with an `aayudha-pack` claiming every floor passes on f3a7658, while the base copy's run 38765896563 failed; ship exited 4: "run 38765900626: a pull_request run, so the workflow file came from the pull request, not the base branch"; nothing reached the ledger; (4) on 294142d, run 38766675139 passed, ship without a human pass exited 4, and after a `Solo-mode bypass:` comment `ship --solo-bypass` merged PR 3 as 47cecd8 and wrote `proof/2/294142d…/authoritative/` (pack.json, ship.json, and text artifacts: coverage, evidence, gitleaks, osv-scanner, semgrep reports) under the founder's account. The founder's token stood in for the human. The pack held no media (no web criterion); `TestBuildAndShip` covers a screenshot that ship hash-checks but never lands |
| D-4.2b.g | V3: media never public; D-4.2b.b: a public repository's run artifact can be downloaded by any signed-in GitHub user | Founder decision (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): accepted | On a public repository, proof media in the merge gate's `aayudha-pack` artifact are readable by any signed-in GitHub user until the artifact expires, at most 90 days. This is where V3's "no public media" no longer holds; its other intent (no media in git, bounded retention) stands. No code change: captures still use `[verify] seed` data and the redaction pass (WP4.5), and a team that needs less exposure deletes the artifact on GitHub or keeps the repository private. Recorded in the threat model (T14) |
| D-4.2b.h | D1, D-2.5a, D-4.2b.d: the ledger ruleset lets only the admin role (and the bot App) update `ledger`, so only an admin can ship | Founder decision (2026-10-15): anyone with write access may run ship, which merges into `dev` only (promotion to `rolling` and `main` stays a separate, admin-only release step); force pushes and deletion stay blocked. A ruleset bypass actor bypasses every rule of its ruleset (live: with `update` and `non_fast_forward` in one ruleset, the write-role bypass let the admin force-push `ledger`, "Bypassed rule violations … Cannot force-push"), so one ruleset cannot say "writers may update but nobody may rewrite". GitHub accepts `RepositoryRole` 4 (write) as a bypass actor on a personal repository, and it covers higher roles: with only that actor listed, `current_user_can_bypass` is `always` for the admin and the admin's push is a bypass. The Actions workflow token still cannot be a bypass actor (D1) | `aayu init` creates two rulesets on `refs/heads/ledger`: `aayudha-ledger` (`update`; bypass: the write role, plus the bot App after `init --identity`) and `aayudha-ledger-history` (`deletion`, `non_fast_forward`; no bypass actors). On a repository from before 0.2, a rerun creates the history ruleset first and then narrows the old one to `update`, adding the write role and keeping its other actors; an adopted ruleset is left alone. `uninit` deletes the rulesets before the ledger, since the history ruleset now blocks the ledger's deletion for admins too. This repository's own `ledger-writers` gets the same shape, with a `ledger-history` beside it. Live (`TestLiveLedgerWriters`, 2026-10-15, `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-1792086941983397991`, kept): `init` created rulesets 25184190 and 25184186; `uninit` removed both and the untouched ledger; after a second `init`, the admin fast-forwarded `ledger` and its force push and deletion were refused ("Cannot force-push to this branch", "Cannot delete this branch"); a `workflow_dispatch` run with `contents: write` was refused updating it (run 38781954189, "Cannot update this protected ref"). Not shown live: a push by a real write collaborator. The only second account available is an Enterprise Managed User, which GitHub refuses as a collaborator on a personal repository; `TestLiveLedgerWriters` runs that case when `AAYU_LIVE_WRITER_TOKEN` names a personal account. `audit` still labels each ledger bypass "founder-identity ledger write by" followed by the pusher's login, whoever pushed; the wording is kept so recorded reports replay byte-identically |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP4.5b (feature maps and behaviour checks, 0.2)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-4.5b | WP4.5: the web floors load each screenshot or recording criterion's URL and photograph it, and behaviour criteria are evidenced only by Go tests named in `.aayudha/evidence/<story>.toml`; WP4.6: k6 thresholds for API and CLI contracts; WP3.2: `install` copies only aayudha's own skills; WP7.1: the loops are the digest, decisions, retro, seat review, and evolve; the `verify` skill body is a stub | Founder decisions (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread), after the research in docs/research/verification-skills-2026-10.md: (1) generic, not UI-only: a feature map lists a repository's capabilities and each has a behaviour check of the kind that fits (a UI flow, a CLI command, an API call, a library or function call), with no assumption of user journeys or a browser; (2) the control tool uses the product's own stack (Node and Playwright for web apps, shell or a PTY driver for CLIs, plain HTTP for APIs, the product's test framework for libraries) and adds no runtime the product lacks; (3) behaviour checks block the merge gate at once when a mapped check fails, and run in CI deterministically with no model; (4) a free deterministic drift check runs on every PR; (5) no AI keys in a product repository's CI: the model-driven maintain pass runs weekly and on drift by @-mentioning an agent app the user installed (@claude, @cursor, @amp, @codex) with the maintain brief, and its result is at most one PR of map corrections through the normal gate; (6) the verify skill gains create and maintain procedures, `aayu init --verify-skill` creates it, `install` mirrors it to hosts, grill, plan, and inspect read the map, and a nightly loop sweeps every check. This extends the plan; WP4.5's static captures and WP4.6 stay | WP4.5b (five parts, WP4.5b.1–5), one small PR each to `dev`. The research's journeys become generic behaviour checks (`kind` ui, cli, api, or lib), its control CLI a contract (`<control> check`, JSON in, result and artifacts out) that each product implements in its own stack, and its source pass `aayu verify --map`. Per-PR drift is reported, not blocking (WP4.5b.1), so that the maintain pass's own PR can correct it; the behaviour checks themselves block |
| D-4.5b.1 | WP4.5b.1: the `feature-map` floor; handles are "the `name`, `label`, `within`, and `test` values of a check's steps"; stale features are "sources changed since `verified_at`" | Drift in a PR is often the change itself (a renamed button), and the maintain pass corrects the map in its own PR, so a blocking drift floor would block the very PRs it describes. Values deeper in a step (a request body's `"name"`) are data, not handles. A test name is a substring of longer test names. `verified_at` is a commit, and a shallow clone (the loops' checkout) has no history to diff | `internal/verify/featuremap.go`. Maps are found with `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` (tracked and not-ignored untracked files, so a map being written is checked before it is committed). The `feature-map` floor always passes: its value is the drift count, its detail starts "advisory:", and it writes `reports/feature-map.json`; `aayu verify --map` exits 3 on drift. No floor threshold, config key, or CONSTRAINTS.md row is added. Handles are the `HandleKeys` fields of a step's action object only (`{"click": {"name": …}}`), and a `test` handle matches as a whole word. A feature whose `verified_at` is not in the clone's history is reported stale ("never verified" when empty); stale is reported, never drift. Tests: `TestMapDrift`, `TestMapStaleOnly`, `TestMapFloor`, `TestVerifyMapExitCodes` |
| D-4.5b.2 | WP4.5b.2: the `behaviour` floor hands each check to the control tool; cli and lib checks get no network; evidence kinds as in the Completion Goal | The evidence kinds are fixed by the work-item schema (`screenshot`, `recording`, `sr-transcript`, `api-trace`, `metric`). A ui or api check needs the app `[verify] seed` starts, which is PR code, exactly as the WP4.5 web floors run it. Whether a precondition (`requires`) holds is known to the control tool, not to aayu. A PR that serves no story has no pack. The examples in this repository reach Playwright through the base branch's `packages/floors` (D-8.1b) | The contract is `<control...> check`, stdin `{product, feature, check, out, base_url, root}`, stdout `{status passed\|failed\|unreachable, reason, artifacts[{path, kind}]}`; malformed output or an artifact path outside `out` blocks the floor, a `failed` check fails it. A CLI or library check's transcript uses the existing `api-trace` kind; no evidence kind is added. ui and api checks run with network allowed (`CheckResult.network`), cli and lib checks isolated. `requires` is interpreted by the control tool, which answers `unreachable` (proving nothing). The merge gate on a PR with no story runs the two map floors only (`verify.MapFloors`). A preview with no pack keeps the floors' artifacts in `.aayudha/state/verify/` (`artifacts_dir`). The Node control tool resolves Playwright from the product's root `package.json`, then `NODE_PATH`. First live gate run: PR #223, 15 of 16 checks passed and one unreachable (`product-events.visit-and-ask`: serve.sh starts no events sink). Live block: PR #234 (a demo, closed unmerged) renamed the benefits-qa "Ask" button and the notes `done` command; the gate (run 38839008282) selected the 11 checks of the touched features and failed 5 (`ask-answer.typed`, `refer-to-hr.typed`, `refusals.personal`, `mark-done.done`, `mark-done.missing`), and the `feature-map` floor reported 5 advisory drift items (the `Ask` handle of three features, the unmapped `finish` and the gone `done` command) |
| D-4.5b.3 | WP4.5b.3: `aayu init --verify-skill <web\|cli\|api\|lib>` scaffolds a stub for that kind; `install` mirrors product `verify-*` skills to every host's skill directory; `doctor` reports a map that is missing, malformed, or drifted | One product mixes kinds (benefits-qa has ui, api, and lib checks), so the kind belongs to each check, not to the skill; what decides the control tool's language is the stack the product already has (founder decision 2). Amp, Cursor, and Codex read `.agents/skills/`, where the skill is written, so only Claude Code needs a copy. `aayu verify --map` already reports a missing, malformed, or drifted map, free and deterministic | `--verify-skill` is a bool. `internal/initrepo/verifyskill.go` detects the stack: `go.mod` → `hosts/verify-skill/go/control/main.go`, else `package.json` → `node/control.mjs` (Node and Playwright), else `other/control.sh`, a stub that blocks until it is replaced. It writes `.agents/skills/verify-<repo>/` (SKILL.md, a map with no features that passes `verify --map`, the tool) through init's owned-file writer, so uninit keeps an edited file. The examples' control tools are these templates (`TestInitVerifySkill`). `install` copies the root `.agents/skills/verify-*/` to `.claude/skills/`, never over an aayudha skill, records it under `[product_skills]` in `lock.toml`, and removes the copy of a deleted skill; a map nested in a monorepo is not mirrored. `doctor`'s `skills-copies` check reports a copy behind its source and `--fix` catches it up; map drift stays with `verify --map` and the floor. The skill body holds the create and maintain procedures (`skills/verify/references/`). Tests: `TestInitVerifySkill`, `TestInstallMirrorsVerifySkill` |
| D-4.5b.4 | WP4.5b.4: `verify --maintain` posts the brief on one tracking issue with an @-mention of `[verify] maintainer`; the loops hand off | GitHub notifies a mention only in a new comment, not an edit. The apps answer only the people who connected them (the same finding as the merge-gate thread's reviewer apps): Codex and Cursor ignore a bot's mention, and claude-code-action ignores bots unless `allowed_bots` names them. Amp has no @-mention. A nightly hand-off of unchanged drift would ask for the same PR every night | `internal/cli/verifymaintain.go`, `internal/verify/maintain.go`. Each hand-off is a new comment on the issue whose body carries `<!-- aayudha:verify-maintain -->` (opened once, titled "Keep the feature maps honest"); the comment carries the report's digest, and `drift` mode posts nothing when the last brief has the same digest. Outside CI the brief is posted under the person's account; in Actions it is posted as the bot App, which records the hand-off but does not reliably wake an app, so the loop's Amp or Cursor schedule, which runs as the person, is the dependable path. `[verify] maintainer` is `claude`, `codex`, `cursor`, or `amp` with no default (schema only, no CONSTRAINTS.md row); unset or `amp`, the brief has no mention and the Amp loop thread follows it. Loops `verify-sweep` (02:30 daily, `verify --check all --maintain drift`) and `verify-maintain` (Mondays 09:00, `verify --map --maintain always`), both `bot = true`; a loop's new `setup` (pinned `node`, `pnpm`) and `setup_run` give the Actions job the product's browser tooling. The sweep's job is red when a check fails. Test: `TestVerifyMaintainHandoff` |
| D-4.5b.5 | WP4.5b.5: `plan` writes the touched features' checks into the evidence-map skeleton; `inspect`'s context lists the features a PR touches | A plan is written before the code, so which features a story touches is known only from its footprint layers. The skeleton is rendered by the delegation brief from the plan's checkpoints. The merge gate's pack already holds `reports/behaviour.json` (each check run, its feature, why it was selected, and its result) and `reports/feature-map.json`, and inspect already quotes the pack's text artifacts as untrusted data | `grill --assess` reports `feature:<id>` (kind `feature`) for each mapped feature whose id, surfaces, or check ids share a word with the idea, naming its checks. `aayu plan <story>` lists the mapped features in the footprint's layers; a checkpoint may name `checks`, which plan refuses when a map has no such check, and the brief writes them as `checks = [...]` beside `tests`. inspect is unchanged: it reads the features and checks from the pack's reports. The evals' grill conversations do not load maps, so their baselines are unchanged. Tests: `TestAssessFeatureFacts`, `TestPlan` (unknown check), `TestBrief` |
| D-4.5b.6 | D-4.5b.1: feature-map drift in the merge gate is reported, not blocking | Founder decision 1 (2026-10-16): drift stays a warning for four weeks, then blocks, as a dated config value like `red_on_base_blocking_from` (#229), not a code change | `[verify] drift_blocking_from = "2026-11-13"` in the embedded defaults (four weeks after the decision; CONSTRAINTS.md row). The `feature-map` floor reads it with the run's clock: before the date (or with none) drift passes with "advisory until" the date; from 00:00 UTC on that date a PR with drift fails the floor ("blocking since …; update the map in this PR"), and the merge gate with it, including on a PR that serves no story. `aayu verify --map` keeps exiting 3 on drift either way. Test: `TestMapFloor` (23:59 the day before passes, 00:00 on the day fails) |
| D-4.5b.7 | D-4.5b.4: the Actions loops post the maintain brief with the app's mention | Founder decision 2 (2026-10-16): scheduled hand-offs (verify-maintain, and the sweep's follow-up) wake the agent from the user's side: a scheduled headless Amp run (`amp -x`) or a Cursor automation, running as the person. Actions only records the hand-off. No token and no `allowed_bots` are added | In CI, `verify --maintain` posts a **record**: no mention, marker `{"digest", "record": true}`, "Recorded by the Actions loop; no agent was woken". Records are de-duplicated against records and hand-offs against hand-offs, so the nightly record of a drift never stops the person's run from handing the same drift off. The person's run (the same loop's prompt, from `loops/amp/<loop>.json` through `amp -x` on the user's scheduler, or the `cursor_platform_workflow` resources in `loops/cursor/aayudha-loops.tf`) posts the brief with the mention, or for `amp` follows it; `loops/README.md` documents both, with a crontab for `amp -x`. Test: `TestVerifyMaintainHandoff` (two CI sweeps post one record without a mention; the person's sweep then posts the hand-off) |
| D-4.5b.8 | D-4.5b.4: `[verify] maintainer` has no default and is set by hand | Founder decision 3 (2026-10-16): this repository uses headless Amp; in a product repository the maintainer is the user's choice, among the agents installed | This repository's `.aayudha/config.toml` sets `[verify] maintainer = "amp"`. `aayu init --verify-skill` asks "Which agent maintains the feature maps?" and offers only the agents detected for the repository: amp, codex, cursor, or claude whose CLI is installed and signed in on this machine (`agentenv.Detect`, WP3.2c), or whose GitHub app answered on the repository (`inspect.DetectApps`). The answer is written as `[verify] maintainer` (a value already set is kept); with none detected, no terminal, or an answer it did not offer, nothing is written and init says how to set it. Test: `TestInitVerifySkillMaintainer` (only amp offered; `amp` written; `codex`, not offered, not written) |
| D-4.5b.9 | Q2 of the WP4.5b report: should feature-map directories be protected paths | Founder decision 4 (2026-10-16): they are not; the gate's weakened-check flag covers them. Checked against the code: `inspect.Weakened` (P6, #228) flagged test files, CI files, and config thresholds only, so a PR could drop a behaviour check or an assertion unflagged | The directories stay unprotected, and `inspect.Weakened` gains a feature-map case for `.agents/skills/verify-*/features/*.md`: fewer `check` blocks ("fewer behaviour checks"), fewer assertion keys (`expect`, `expectText`, `absentText`, `count`, `stdout`, `stderr`, `status`, `exit`, `body`, `test`: "fewer behaviour assertions"), or a deleted feature file. A renamed handle on a changed line is not a weakening. The flag reaches the proof comment and inspect as before. Test: `TestWeakenedChecks` |

### Scope additions and deviations recorded while implementing the October 2026 research decisions (P1, P4, P7)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers. The research is
[docs/research/video-learnings-2026-10.md](research/video-learnings-2026-10.md); the founder decided its §8 questions
on 2026-10-15 (relayed by the orchestrating thread): measure first (P1), a stricter `evolve` (P4, Q3), ratchets in
this repository only for 0.2 (P7, Q5), never score host agent transcripts (Q6), and no AI keys in a product
repository's CI. Everything below runs with no model.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-7.6e | WP7.6 `retro`: estimate against observed checkpoints, corrections, streaks, floor misses, cost per story | Founder decision 1 (P1): retro also reports, from data aayu already has and deterministically, the time per stage, the human wait from gate-green to ship, touches per story, the share of inspect findings acted on, the escape rate (Bugs traced to shipped stories), and the change mix. This extends WP7.6; it replaces nothing | A "Flow (reported, not gated)" section in every retro report, after the estimate table, and `flow` in `retro --json`. No finding, no Feedback issue, no config key, and no `status` line come from it: the numbers are a baseline, and the skill tells the twin not to change a story, a split, or a review to move one (R1 of the research) |
| D-7.6e | S-7.6e; P1 of the research: stage times from "ledger timestamps", touches from "human-only actions in the actions log", acted-on from posted finding IDs, escapes as PRs `inspect` passed that got a correction | The actions log records the GitHub write, not the verb, and in solo mode almost every write carries identity `human` (agents run under the founder's token), so it cannot tell a person's touch from an agent's. Only stories built with `aayu build` have a first checkpoint (`build/<n>/state.json`): 6 of the 46 stories this repository delivered from 2026-10-01. A workflow run has no completion time in the API; `updated_at` is the last change. inspect keeps its state, including the IDs it posted inline (WP4.8b), in its replace-in-place PR comment, so PRs reviewed before inline comments record only their fix cycles. The founder named Bugs, not corrections, for escapes | `internal/audit/flow.go`, run by `aayu retro` only (`Source.Flow`). Per story delivered in the window: Ready is its first `status:ready` label (timeline); the first checkpoint the earliest `at` in its build record on the ledger; gate green the `updated_at` of the earliest successful `pull_request_target` run of `aayudha-merge-gate.yml` on the merge PR's final head SHA that completed by the merge; ship the merge (with or without `aayu ship`). Stages: queue (Ready → first checkpoint), build (→ gate green), human wait (→ ship), total (Ready → ship); each counts only stories with both ends and reports P50 and P90 by nearest rank, with "n of N stories". Touches: comments and reviews by a person on the story or its merge PR between Ready and ship (not a `…[bot]` or `[identity] bot_logins` login, not a comment carrying an `<!-- aayudha:` marker, not an approving review, which is ship's human pass), plus each later `status:ready` label and the PR's WP5.7 corrections other than changes-requested (already counted as a review); an agent writing as the founder without a marker still counts as a person. inspect: over PRs merged into dev in the window with an inspect comment, fix cycles, and each posted ID: dismissed when `inspect/dismissed.jsonl` on the ledger names it for that PR, open when the latest inspection still lists it, else acted on; the share is acted on ÷ posted. Escape rate: stories with a Bug (from the WP5.7 fetch: `type:bug` issues updated since the oldest of the 100 newest merged PRs) opened after the merge, before the cursor and within `[autonomy] correction_days`, whose body links the story or its merge PR (`#n`, `/pull/n`, `/issues/n`), and which that PR does not itself fix (a Bug filed first and fixed by the PR is not an escape). Change mix: the Conventional Commit type of every PR title merged into dev in the window, `other` when it has none. The GitHub inputs snapshot gains `gate_runs`, `bugs`, PR `head_sha`, a PR item's `inspect` state, and a comment's `aayu` flag, written by retro only, so audit's snapshots and golden are unchanged; the build records and dismissals come from the ledger at the recorded commit, so `retro --replay` is byte-identical. A retro report written before this change replays with the binary that wrote it (it has no Flow section). Test: `TestRetroFlow` (hand-computed and asymmetric: a 24-hour human wait beside a 1-hour one, three touches against a bot comment, a marker comment, and an approval that do not count, an escape against a Bug filed before the merge and one the PR fixes, a dismissal of the same finding ID on another PR, and a PR into `main` outside the mix; the numbers survive a snapshot round trip). First run on this repository (dry run, 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-16T00:00Z, 46 stories and bugs, 135 PRs): total Ready to ship P50 2.5 h, P90 6.4 h (45 stories); queue P50 1.6 h and build P50 1.2 h (6 stories); human wait P50 0.0 h, P90 0.1 h (12 stories: in solo mode the agent ships as soon as the gate is green); touches P50 1, P90 2; inspect reviewed 25 PRs with 22 fix cycles and recorded 1 posted finding, still open at merge (0 % acted on); escape rate 0 of 46; change mix feat 52 %, fix 27 %, docs 10 %, chore 4 %, test 4 %, ci 2 %, refactor 1 % |
| S-7.7c | WP7.7 and D-7.7a: `evolve` takes one accepted retro Feedback about a skill, quarterly; eval-first gate | Founder decision 2 (P4, research Q3): evolve proposes a skill change only when at least two accepted Feedback issues point at the same skill, at most once a month per skill; it rewrites the guidance rather than appending rules; and a recurrence check reports whether a fixed problem came back | Three rules in `evolve` and one in `retro`, all deterministic: the two-Feedback rule and the monthly cap in the verb, the rewrite rule in the eval-first gate (so CI enforces it), and the recurrence check in the retro report. The `evolve` loop moves from quarterly to monthly (day 2, 10:00 in `[time] tz`; it only lists). The skill body is rewritten to match: name the owning surface (missing, wrong, or unclear instruction), stop when the Feedback issues do not share a cause, and rewrite the section as a whole |
| D-7.7c | S-7.7c; P4 of the research: "two independent stories" as evidence, safety findings excepted; recurrence "within 30 days after its evolve PR merged" | Retro Feedback names a skill only in its title (skill `<name>`, the per-skill correction-rate finding), and its body is untrusted text. "Rewrite, not append" is a judgement; a diff's line counts are the only deterministic proxy. GitHub has no search for PRs by head-branch prefix. The founder's rule has no safety exception | **Two Feedback issues:** `aayu evolve --skill <s> --feedback <n>` lists the open retro Feedback with `needs:grill` (`acceptedFeedback`) and refuses (exit 4, nothing written) unless `#n` names skill `<s>` in its title and at least `EvolveMinFeedback` = 2 such issues exist; the PR body cites them all ("Evidence: #a, #b"). There is no safety exception: a safety problem is a Bug, not a skill evolution. `aayu evolve` without `--feedback` groups the list by skill and says which skills are ready, which are waiting (and for how many), and which Feedback names no skill. **Monthly cap:** it refuses when another PR whose head branch is `evolve/<s>-…` was opened in the last `EvolveCooldownDays` = 30 days (any state; PRs listed newest first until one is older); the evolve branch's own PR does not count, so a re-run still pushes. **Rewrite:** `aayu evolve` and CI's gate now pass `git diff --raw --numstat --no-renames`, which `ParseNameStatus` reads with line counts (a `--name-status` diff still parses, without them). The gate refuses a case whose skill diff (`skills/<s>/`, `evals/skills/<s>/`) adds lines but deletes none and deletes no file: "the skill diff only appends (N lines added, none removed or rewritten)". **Recurrence:** each retro lists the evolve PRs (head `evolve/<skill>-<n>`, merged into dev) merged in the `RecurrenceDays` = 30 days before the cursor, and the problem came back when a retro Feedback issue opened after the merge, or an unfiled finding of this window, names the same skill (a recurrence finding itself does not count). When it did, retro files "evolve did not hold: skill `<s>` (PR #p, Feedback #n) came back" with the `aayu install --pin` rollback. That title names the skill, so it counts toward the next evolve's evidence. The fake GitHub's PR list now honours `state` and `direction` and records `created_at`. Tests: `TestEvolveVerb` (one Feedback refused, a Feedback naming no skill or another skill refused, two accepted, a PR 10 days old blocks and 40 days old does not, the PR cites both), `TestEvolveGateCI` (an append-only skill diff is refused beside the same change as a rewrite, which passes), `TestRetroRecurrence` (came back by a later Feedback and by this window's finding; a Feedback before the merge, a recurrence finding, and a PR merged 35 days earlier do not count) |
| S-7.7d | WP7.6, WP7.7, S-7.7c: a correction becomes retro Feedback, and evolve fixes it by rewriting a skill with an eval case, so every correction lands in the prose layer | Founder decision (2026-10-17, relayed by the orchestrating thread), from the "inverse pyramid" for correcting agents: most effort goes into the codebase (types, invariants, boundaries), then static analysis (lint rules, tests, CI checks, verify), then prose (skills, AGENTS.md, rules) only for what needs judgement, and least into human review. Each correction is first classified by the highest layer that can fix it for good, with the reason; codebase and static-check corrections become a Story or Bug whose proof is the new type, invariant, lint rule, or test; only prose corrections go through the eval-first skill rewrite; review corrections change what the environment surfaces for review. The classification is visible on the Feedback issue and counted per layer in retro's report, and evolve refuses a skill rewrite for a correction classified above prose unless the reason says why code or a check cannot catch it. The principle was not in ETHOS.md | ETHOS 10, "Fix the system before the prose". `aayu retro --classify <feedback> --layer codebase\|check\|prose\|review --reason …` records the layer; codebase, check, and review corrections file a Story; evolve takes only prose Feedback; and every retro report gains a "Fix layer of Feedback" table. The deterministic parts (the record's completeness, the Story, the label, the counts, evolve's refusal) are code; the layer itself is the twin's judgement with its recorded reason. The parallel transcript-mining thread's proposals enter here: one `retro --classify` per correction |
| D-7.7d | S-7.7d: "filed through aayu, with a Completion Goal whose proof is the new type, invariant, lint rule or test"; "a Story or Bug"; the classification visible on the Feedback issue | A Bug needs reproduction steps and an observed failure that a classification does not have. A Completion Goal criterion needs an evidence kind (`screenshot`, `recording`, `sr-transcript`, `api-trace`, `metric`), and none is "a test fails"; the proof is named in the expected result. Retro already fetches every issue's labels, but not their comments, and a snapshot that grew comment bodies would grow every report. GitHub has no structured field on an issue; aayu keeps state in replace-in-place comments (inspect, respond) | **Record:** the `fix:codebase`, `fix:check`, `fix:prose`, and `fix:review` labels (created by `init`; `workitems.FixLayerOf`) plus one comment marked `<!-- aayudha:fix-layer -->`, upserted, with the reason, the not-catchable reason, the Story, and a JSON state line. `--reason` is always required. `--not-catchable` (why no type, invariant, lint rule, or test can catch it) is required for `prose` and `review` and optional above. `--criterion` is required for `codebase`, `check`, and `review`, refused for `prose`, parsed with `workitems.ParseCriteria`, and for `codebase` and `check` must name the proof in backticks. Refusals exit 2 before any write. A reclassification swaps the label, rewrites the comment, and never files a second Story. **Story, not Bug:** the filed item is always a Story (`type:story`, `status:backlog`, bot-tagged), titled "Fix at the `<layer>` layer (Feedback #n): …", with the reason in its Summary and the criterion as its Completion Goal; `grill story` completes the rest of Ready, and a person who prefers a Bug files one and reclassifies. **evolve:** `acceptedFeedback` reads each accepted Feedback's layer from its labels and, above prose, its comment state; `evolveRefusal` refuses an unclassified Feedback, a `review` one, and a `codebase` or `check` one without `--not-catchable` (exit 4). Only Feedback it accepts counts toward the two-issue rule of S-7.7c, and `aayu evolve` with no flags shows "not for evolve: …" beside the rest. **retro:** "Fix layer of Feedback" counts every Feedback issue opened before the cursor by its label at the cursor, `unclassified` included, from the labels the snapshot already holds, so `--replay` stays byte-identical. A report from before this change replays with the binary that wrote it. Every Feedback body retro files now says to classify it first. Skills: retro gains step 3, the order of questions with stop at the first yes; evolve says a rewrite is the third layer and to reclassify rather than rewrite when code or a check could catch it. Docs: ETHOS 10, how-evals ("Fix layer first"), guide-learn, journey-8, when-to-use-what. Tests: `TestRetroClassify` (an incomplete record writes nothing; a check correction files a Story whose criterion names the test; reclassifying to prose swaps the label, keeps one comment, files no second Story), `TestEvolveVerb` (unclassified and codebase-without-reason Feedback are refused and do not count; with a not-catchable reason the codebase one counts), and `TestRetroFixLayers` (counts by layer, PRs, other types, and later Feedback excluded). The regression test is `TestEvolveVerb`, which fails on the evolve of `dev` before this change |
| D-7.7e | S-7.7d and D-7.7d: Feedback filed before the classification existed stayed unclassified, and evolve's refusal was the only place that said so; the fix Story carried no layer, so fix work was not filterable and retro could not count it | Founder follow-ups on #323 (2026-10-17, relayed by the orchestrating thread): old Feedback must not sit stuck; classify #190 and #152 with a real reason (or close #190 with evidence if the personas work fixed it), reopen any Feedback closed without a fix, make unclassified open Feedback surface on its own (a doctor row and the session-start summary, with the classify command); and give the fix Story its Feedback's `fix:<layer>` label, a label rather than a new work-item type, so retro counts fix Stories shipped per layer. Host hooks make no network call (the session-start and status-line hooks read the last home screen) | **Surfacing:** `aayu doctor` gains a `feedback-layers` row that warns with each open Feedback issue without a `fix:<layer>` label and the command (`workitems.ClassifyCommand`); the home screen (`aayu`, `aayu next`) lists them in the same words (`journey.UnclassifiedNote`, `unclassified_feedback` in `--json`); the status line counts "Feedback to classify"; the session-start hook prints the note from the last home screen, with its age past ten minutes (`Env.sessionNotes`, passed to `hosts.RunHook`; Cursor gets it as `additional_context`). The hook stays offline, so it is as fresh as the last `aayu` or `aayu next`. **Label:** the Story `retro --classify` files carries `fix:<layer>`; rerunning the command adds it to a Story filed earlier, swaps it on a reclassification, and removes it when the correction moves to `prose`, which no Story fixes. A Feedback titled "Feedback: …" by the issue form no longer repeats the prefix in its Story's title. **retro:** the "Fix layer of Feedback" table adds "Fix Stories" (Stories carrying the layer's label, opened before the cursor) and "Shipped" (those merged into dev, or done, by the cursor). **The old Feedback:** no closed Feedback exists (`gh issue list --state closed --label type:feedback` is empty), so none was reopened. #190 and #152 are classified `prose` with their reasons on the issues: no type or test can decide whether a cited sentence claims more than its source (#190) or whether an everyday restatement keeps a fact (#152), and the decidable parts are already checks (`seats.CheckGroundingWith`, D-5.1g.a; `brief` `absenceClaims`, D-7.3e). #190 is not closed, because D-5.1h to D-5.1j still find the defect and the personas group is below 4.0. Tests: `TestChecksGolden` (an unclassified Feedback and one with an unknown `fix:` label are listed; a classified one and a pull request are not), `TestHomeModes` (the home screen in every mode), `TestPluginHooksAndStatusLine` (no note with nothing to classify; the note at session start for Claude Code and Cursor, and the status-line count), `TestRetroClassify` (the Story's label, its swap, its removal at prose, the title), and `TestRetroFixLayers` (fix Stories and shipped per layer, with a Story merged after the cursor and one opened after it). The regression test is `TestRetroClassify`, which fails on the `dev` before this change |
| S-4.3r | WP4.3 and CONSTRAINTS.md: floors are static thresholds; a change to one is a protected-path edit with a human review | Founder decision 3 (P7, research Q5): in this repository only, for 0.2, floors (coverage first) can only tighten automatically when a better value holds, and loosening needs a recorded waiver. It is not a product feature for repositories aayudha sets up | A coverage ratchet for this repository's CI: `ratchets.toml` at the root holds the floor, `internal/ratchet` checks it, and the required `build` job runs it on every PR and push. No verb, config key, `init` template, or CONSTRAINTS.md row is added, and no model or secret is used: the job's own read token reads a waiver |
| D-4.3r | S-4.3r; P7 of the research: `[verify.ratchets.<name>]` with a command, a direction, a noise margin, and a committed ceiling; a new ratchet stays advisory until reruns show its noise is below the margin | CONSTRAINTS.md's coverage floor (`min_diff_percent`) is per PR and depends on the size of each diff, so it has no "better value" to hold. The zero-count floors (SAST, secrets, deps, a11y) are already at their tightest. The performance budgets apply only to stories with web criteria. A CI job cannot push to `dev`, and a bot PR per tightening is the review load the research rejected. The coverage of the module's `go test ./...` is measured on every push. Local and CI runs can differ slightly (toolchain, tools installed) | One ratchet, `[coverage]`: the statement coverage of `go test -coverprofile=coverage.out ./...` across the module (blocks counted once, as `go tool cover -func` reports), with `floor`, `margin` (0.5 points), and an optional `waiver`. `go run ./internal/ratchet/cmd/ratchet -coverprofile coverage.out [-base <sha>]` checks three rules. (1) Coverage below `floor − margin` fails, on PRs and pushes. (2) On a PR, coverage at `floor + 2 × margin` or more fails until the PR tightens the floor with `-update`, which sets it to coverage less the margin, rounded down to a tenth, and never lowers it. That is the "automatic" tightening: the tool computes it and CI requires it, so no daily job and no extra PR are needed. A push to `dev` does not apply this rule, so two PRs that each raised coverage a little do not turn `dev` red; the next PR tightens. (3) A floor lower than the base's fails unless `waiver = <n>` names a Decision that the base did not already name, and that the job reads from GitHub as an accepted floor waiver for coverage: labelled `decision:waiver`, titled "Waive the coverage floor on #…", closed as completed by a person, with "waive, accepted by @" in its Decision section (`aayu decide --waiver coverage <story-or-bug>`, then `--accept waive`; the rule `verify` applies, WP5.6). A base without `ratchets.toml` has floor 0. The `build` job gains `issues: read`. The proxy check (noise against the margin): the same tree measured 63.1 % in the orb (Go 1.27) and 63.3 % on the CI runner (Go 1.25, run 38833496218), inside the margin; the initial floor, 62.8 %, is `-update` on the CI value. AGENTS.md and CONTRIBUTING.md say how to tighten. Tests: `TestCheck` (the margin on both sides of the floor, a stale floor on a PR but not on a push, tightening, a loosening without a waiver, with the base's waiver, and with a new one), `TestTotal` (a block in two profile lines counts once), `TestAcceptedWaiver` (only an accepted coverage waiver closed by a person) |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP5.8 (the vagueness gate, 0.2)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-5.8 | WP5.3 and WP5.3b: grill runs when the human asks for it; build, slice, and plan start from an issue | Founder request (2026-10-16, relayed by the orchestrating thread): aayudha must turn vague asks into concrete expectations for technical and non-technical founders alike, "the difference between a vibe-coded delivery and a competent product team". Whenever a user gives any aayudha skill or slash command a vague request, in Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, or Amp, the skill stops and unpacks first and continues only after the human confirms; a concrete request passes without friction. Catalogues stay generic (CLIs, APIs, libraries, and developer tools, not only UI) | WP5.8 added to M5 (M). The binary decides the verdict and supplies the facts; the host's model unpacks with the human through the grill skill. The scope guard of D-5.3b.b is unchanged: nothing enters the spec unconfirmed |
| D-5.8a | S-5.8: a vagueness gate at every entry point that takes free text, running `aayu grill <type> --assess --idea` deterministically | "Vague" cannot be decided by rule, only approximated; a gate that unpacks too often adds friction to concrete asks. The four delegate commands (`hosts/commands/{codex,cursor,claude,amp}.md`) and the Amp palette (`hosts/amp/aayudha.ts`) are owned by the delegation thread (WP5.5b), whose commands already run `aayu grill story --assess --idea "<goal>" --json` and hand off only after `aayu ready` passes, and `build --delegate` refuses a story that is not Ready | `internal/grill.GateFor` (in the assessment, JSON `gate`): **unpack** when the request has a catalogue qualifier (`vague`), an abbreviation or jargon term (`term`: all caps or a numeronym, not a code identifier, not on the jargon file's Plain list, with its usual meanings, several meanings named), or, with no qualifier, nothing observable (`outcome`: no number outside issue references and terms, no quoted wording or backticked literal, no route or path, no flag), carrying the rough-idea dimensions; **pass** otherwise, with `next` telling the skill to draft from the request's own words (`stated (request)`). Entry points: the grill skill's new "The gate" section, and the build, slice, and plan skills ("Words, not an issue? Unpack first"); the MCP `grill` prompt puts the verdict first, and a new MCP `ask` prompt (`request`, `verb` build, plan, slice, or grill; slice assesses a Feature) wraps the words as untrusted, embeds the assessment, and tells the host to unpack or pass and then continue with that verb's skill; `aayu build` or `plan` given words instead of an issue fails with ``next: aayu grill story --assess --idea "…"``. The TUI asks one step per term and per rough-idea dimension. The delegate commands (WP5.5c, owned there) already run the same assessment and then the grill skill's gate step, so they are unchanged; switching their step to read `gate.verdict` and the Amp palette are left to that thread. Tests: TestGate (each kind and its near misses: an issue reference and B2B are not numbers, a backticked command is observable), TestGrillAssess (gate, terms, build given words), TestPromptsResourcesAndExitCodes (`ask`) |
| D-5.8b | S-5.8: motion, abbreviations, rough ideas, and product thinking that derives a Goal and checkpoints; product-type-aware qualifiers | Products differ: a web app's "fast" is interaction latency, a CLI's is startup and per-command p95. The binary cannot see what a request is for, only the checkout. A work item had no field for checkpoints | `internal/grill` detects product types from the checkout (`product:web` from page routes or a UI dependency, `api` from API routes, a net/http server, a web framework app, or an OpenAPI document, `cli` from a Go `package main`, a package.json `bin`, or an argument parser, `library` from a package or module manifest with no app, server, or command entry point; each fact names its evidence) and reports CLI `command:` facts (Go flag sets, cobra, commander, argparse) and web `motion:` facts (CSS and inline `transition` and `animation` durations, motion libraries such as framer-motion), with `gap:motion` (no house durations to keep to) and `gap:reduced-motion` (motion without a `prefers-reduced-motion` rule). A catalogue dimension may name `Products:`; the assessment lists only the dimensions for the detected types (all of them when none is found), and reports tokens, motion, the a11y floor, and the performance budget only for web. New catalogues `vague/motion.md` (purpose, duration and easing budget, reduced motion, where it applies, how to prove it: a recording or a metric) and `vague/idea.md` (who, job, outcome, smallest provable slice, out of scope), and `references/jargon.md` (abbreviations with their meanings, and a Plain list such as API, URL, and CLI that never stops the gate); `ux`, `perf`, and `simple` gain CLI, API, and library dimensions (`perf.startup`, `perf.endpoint`, `perf.library`, `ux.cli-output`, …) and the qualifiers slick, sleek, nice, elegant, and stunning. A grilled Story or Bug (one with Provenance) now needs `Term: "<abbr>" → <meaning>; "<the human's words>"` (or `→ deferred:`) for each abbreviation of the request, and a **Checkpoints** section (new optional schema field, so the issue forms regenerate): `N. <what the human can see> (criteria 1, 2)`, numbered in order, every criterion reached by one. The readback (`grill.Readback`, the TUI's review screen and `grill --json`'s `readback`) states the Goal, "Measured by", "Done when", the checkpoints, and the terms. The plan skill orders its checkpoints to reach the story's, and build tells the human when one is reached. Bodies without Provenance are unchanged, so Ready already granted is not withdrawn |
| D-5.8c | S-5.8: tier-1 and tier-2 cases for animations on a web app, an abbreviation-heavy request, "make the CLI simple", and a concrete request with no questions; the conversations group stays advisory and path-triggered | A conversation case runs about 2 to 3 USD; six of them exceed the group's 20 USD cap. A judge cannot tell "no questions" from "good questions", so the pass case needs a deterministic floor | Cases `grill/bakery-motion` (#42), `grill/bakery-terms` (#61), `grill/tally-simple` (#71, a new Go CLI fixture `testdata/tally`), and `grill/bakery-concrete` (#43, `gate = "pass"`); the bakery fixture gains a 150 ms button transition. New case key `gate`: tier 1 requires the recorded assessment's verdict to equal it and a pass case to record no question; tier 2 scores 1 before the judge when round 1 asks nothing on unpack or anything on pass. `evals.Concrete` checks that each `Term:` quote is the human's. bakery-ux and search-fast are re-recorded with Goal and Checkpoints. The `conversations` group holds all six, `max_usd` 20 → 40, still `gate = "advisory"` and triggered only by dispatch and pushes to `dev` that change grill files (D-5.3b.h).
