No call needed the reset retry; the `skills` group passed. From the transcripts (who was wrong): **skill**, D-5.8g's "everything the human stated is in the spec" turned declined topics into criteria that forbid them: search-fast 1–4 wrote "no spinner, skeleton, or loading message appears" from the parked "Leave the loading feedback as it is", slugkit-simple 1, 3, 4 required "option names unchanged" from the parked "The option names are fine", bakery-terms 1 required "no price and no invoice", bakery-motion 2 "no step added, removed, or merged"; **skill**, quotes-fast 4 and 5 never asked what must not change, so the human's "the amounts must stay exactly the same" never came up and the spec had one criterion (the case's `cite = 2` floor); **skill**, bakery-motion 5 put a non-link line in Links; **skill**, my round-2 example wording ("on every step of the flow") and routes in expected results made bakery-motion's criteria read as placements; **check**, `grill`'s placement check read "on every step" as a place named "every" (a scope quantifier, not a place); **config**, grill replies reach 7819–8000 output tokens. No case rule was wrong this round | Skill: **Declined is parked, not a criterion** (a turned-down change appears in no criterion, neither required nor forbidden); **Ask what must not change** (`request/existing-behaviour` in the first round when the request changes something that works); scope is said with "for every", "for any", or "whenever", and routes stay in the state unless a reply names the page; story.md says Links holds only `depends-on #N` lines. Check: a placement whose word is every, each, any, or all is a scope, not a place (TestCheck "scope is not a placement"). Config: the skill output cap is 12000 (a cap, not a charge). Run 3 is the last the budget allows (about 117 USD spent of about 180) |
| D-5.8i | S-5.8b result, tier-2 run 3 | Run 38889426725 (push of 5c618cb, 40 runs, 56.00 USD, judge gpt-6.1-sol): **suite mean 4.075**, pass^5 0.375, 0 errors; one skill call (tally-simple) dropped its connection and was retried once (`CallLog.Retried`), so no infrastructure error counted. bakery-concrete 5, 5, 5, 5, 5 (5.0, still no questions); bakery-ux 5, 5, 5, 5, 5 (5.0); quotes-fast 3, 5, 5, 5, 5 (4.6; every run asked what must not change); search-fast 4, 4, 5, 5, 4 (4.4); slugkit-simple 4, 3, 4, 5, 5 (4.2); tally-simple 4, 1, 3, 4, 5 (3.4); bakery-motion 1, 4, 4, 4, 2 (3.0); bakery-terms 4, 2, 2, 2, 5 (3.0). The founder's goal (a mean of at least 4.0 over 8 cases) is met on the third of four allowed runs; total spend 173.00 USD of about 180 (57.86 + 59.14 + 56.00), so the fourth run is not used. Remaining low scores, all **skill** (no case rule was wrong): bakery-motion 1 parked "step changes", the same words as its confirmed scope ("for every step change", my round-3 example), so the parked-idea check fired; bakery-motion 5 forbade other durations and easings (a declined change written as a criterion); bakery-terms 2 narrowed "personal data" to name, email, and phone, 3 left the button's label and form open, 4 invented a baseline of 0; tally-simple 2 tagged a default the human never accepted, 3 parked an accepted default (exit code 2) | Stop: the group stays advisory (pass^5 0.375 is below its bar) and does not gate v0.2.0. Open for a later round, not run: a parked idea's name must not reuse the confirmed scope's words; never invent a baseline number; keep the human's whole definition of a term (PII is all of the form's personal data) |
| D-5.8j | S-5.8b round 4 (founder, 2026-10-16: one more round before v0.2.0, at most 2 runs, about 120 USD; the suite mean stays at 4.0 or above and bakery-concrete asks nothing, or the change is reverted) | The low scores of run 38889426725 in the three weak cases, from their transcripts (who was wrong). bakery-motion 1 (1): the parked idea "step changes" reused the words of the criterion "whenever the step changes" (**skill**); bakery-motion 5 (2) and 1: the accepted proof default ("every duration in the changed styles is within the confirmed budget") became "0 other durations are added" and "0 other easing values" (**skill**, and the catalogue's wording invited it); bakery-motion 2–4 (4): the Summary and readback goal still said "slick" (**skill**). bakery-terms 3 (2): it drafted without asking the smallest slice, so the button had no label and the form no fields (**skill**); bakery-terms 2 and 4 (2): "today's baseline is about 0", a number nobody gave (**skill**; the generated persona's "never sets a target without a baseline" pushes towards it); bakery-terms 2 (2): the judge read the scripted human's "the form's personal data" (request/scope) as wider than the same human's PII definition "names, emails, and phone numbers" (term/pii), so either spec loses (**case**, its human contradicted itself); bakery-terms 3 and 4: Measure without "5 a week" was marked down because the rubric said "Measure and Threshold carry the human's number", where story.md has Measure name what is counted (**case**). tally-simple 2 (1): "Yes, one screen, 24 lines is fine" (a confirm) tagged `default` (**skill**); tally-simple 3 (3): the skill asked error wording and exit code in one question, the reply answered the wording only, and the rubric said "exit code 2 is the accepted default" though the human never accepted it in that run (**case**, and **skill** for bundling) | Skill: one question settles one decision; no draft for a rough idea before the smallest slice is named; a baseline is a number the human or a fact gives, else unknown and deferred, never estimated (not even 0), and the Threshold carries the human's number; `default` only for a reply that just accepts the default; parked names use words no criterion uses; **Behaviour, as agreed** (criteria check what users observe, an accepted proof is written as offered, never tightened into a ban; Summary and readback goal never repeat the vague word); motion.md's proof default checks each added duration against its confirmed value. Cases: bakery-terms' human defines PII the same way in both answers; its rubric says Measure names what is counted and Threshold carries 5 a week, and a baseline nobody gave is not invented; tally-simple's rubric holds exit code 2 only where the human accepted that default |
| D-5.8k | S-5.8b round 4, after its run 1 | Run 38901145352 (push of 871fa70, 60.42 USD, judge gpt-6.1-sol): **suite mean 4.32**, pass^5 0.375, 2 errors; bakery-concrete 5, 5, 5, 5, 5 (still no questions); bakery-ux 5, 5, 5, 5, 5; search-fast 4, 5, 0, 5, 4 (4.5; the 0 is a connection reset that failed again on its one retry); bakery-motion 5, 2, 5, 5, 5 (4.4, was 3.0); quotes-fast 5, 2, 5, 5, 5 (4.4); tally-simple 5, 2, 4, 5, 0 (4.0, was 3.4; the 0 is the group's 60 USD cap cutting the last run); bakery-terms 2, 2, 4, 5, 5 (3.6, was 3.0); slugkit-simple 5, 4, 1, 3, 5 (3.6). Five calls needed the reset retry. Guard rails hold (mean at or above 4.0, bakery-concrete asks nothing), so nothing is reverted. The gating `skills` group failed on `grill/story-empty-claims` (5, 5, 5, 5, 2: a screenshot for a press-then-see criterion). Both bakery-terms 2s rest on the PII scope: **case**, my D-5.8j fix still had the scripted human limit it to "from the form" (request/scope) while term/pii says "any of our logs"; **skill**, the criteria narrowed it to "any catering request sent through the form"; and run 1's unscripted proof question (`other/pii-proof`) got the fallback park, after which the judge read the PII criterion as a parked proof (**skill**: a stated requirement keeps its criterion whatever happens to an offered proof) | Case: bakery-terms' human gives one PII scope in both answers (any of our logs, whatever page or order). Skill: "only the feature you are building" joins the narrowing examples; a stated requirement stays a criterion when a proof offer is waved off; the evidence kind must be able to show the result (a screenshot is one state; press-then-see is a recording or an API trace). The group cap stays at 60 USD so round 4 stays near its 120 USD budget; a run cut by the cap records an error, not a 0 in the mean |
| D-5.8l | S-5.8b round 4 result, its run 2 | Run 38910426259 (push of 9b023d8, 60.00 USD, judge gpt-6.1-sol): **suite mean 4.63**, pass^5 0.75, 2 errors; bakery-concrete 5, 5, 5, 5, 5 (still no questions); bakery-ux 5, 5, 5, 5, 5; quotes-fast 5, 5, 5, 5, 5; bakery-motion 5, 5, 5, 5, 4 (4.8); slugkit-simple 4, 5, 5, 5, 5 (4.8); search-fast 4, 4, 4, 5, 5 (4.4); bakery-terms 5, 2, 4, 5, 4 (4.0); tally-simple 2, 5, 4, 0, 0 (3.67 over 3 completed runs; the two 0s are the group's 60 USD cap cutting its last runs, recorded as errors). No call needed the reset retry; the gating `skills` group passed. Open for the founder: (1) a partly accepted default ("Yes, one screen, 24 lines is fine") still lets the skill keep the unaccepted parts; (2) whether bakery-terms' scripted human should answer a proof question (or its rubric say a waved-off proof does not remove the stated requirement); (3) the conversations group's 60 USD cap now cuts the last tally runs, so a full run needs about 65 USD |

### Scope change and deviations recorded while implementing the reviewer-app decisions (WP4.8c, 0.2)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers. The research is
[docs/research/verification-skills-2026-10.md](research/verification-skills-2026-10.md) (Open Code Review, §4 and
P1–P2 there) and [docs/research/video-learnings-2026-10.md](research/video-learnings-2026-10.md) (P2, P3, P5, P6 and
Q4 there); the founder decided on 2026-10-15 (relayed by the orchestrating thread). PRs: #216, #225, #228, #229, #231.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-4.8c | Ground rule 5: the binary calls a model directly for "`inspect` in the merge gate" (and "`triage` in Actions"); §0.1: CI runs `inspect`; WP4.8: inspect uses the "review-lane model (family ≠ builder)", with `[budget] per_pr_inspect_max`; D-4.8a: the `aayudha/inspect` job holds the model key in the `ai-gateway` environment; D-4.8b.1: "the merge gate's `aayudha/inspect` job holds the model key"; WP4.8b, D-4.8b.3, and D-4.8b.7: peer mode (two model reviewers) and `delegated_mode` in the gate; the merge-gate template's setup comment (model keys in `ai-gateway`); the merge-gate guide's model-review sections | Founder decision 1 (2026-10-15): no AI keys in a product repository's CI. For every repository aayudha sets up, CI runs only deterministic checks. The merge gate's AI review moves to @-mentioning an agent app the user installed on the organisation or account (@claude, @cursor, @amp, @codex); install and doctor detect them; config picks the reviewer; the different-family rule stays, falling back to a human review when no app of another family exists; aayu reads the reply deterministically into the disposition and criterion verdicts; a missing or malformed reply fails closed. aayudha's own repository keeps its AI gateway for evals only. Decision 2: inspect takes two ideas from Open Code Review and depends on nothing from it. Decision 3: a PR may edit its own checks, but weakening is flagged. Decision 4: red-on-base proof for Bugs, advisory for four weeks by a dated config value. Decision 5: P2, P6, P3, and Q4 (high-risk PRs stay on the single reviewer) | The merge gate's `inspect` job has no environment, secret, or model call. `[inspect] reviewer` (`auto`, `claude`, `codex`, `cursor`, `amp`, `human`, or `model`; default `auto`), `[inspect] apps` (the installed apps), and `[inspect.app_families]` pick the reviewer (`inspect.ChooseReviewer`); `reviewer = "model"` keeps the 0.1 lane for a repository that accepts a key. `aayu inspect <pr>` outside CI posts the request under the person's account; the gate reads the reply (`inspect.ReadReply`, `Runner.Judge`, no model call); a new `reply` job (`aayudha/inspect-reply`) re-runs the head's inspect job when the reviewer replies. `doctor` gains `reviewer-apps`; `install` notes detected apps. This repository's own gate uses the same path (`[inspect] apps = ["codex"]`); its gateway stays for `evals.yml`. The rule-5 text, §0.1, and WP4.8 are unchanged in text; this row records where their "model in the merge gate" no longer applies. Peer mode, `delegated_mode`, `peer_model`, and `[budget] per_pr_inspect_max` apply only to `reviewer = "model"` |
| D-4.8c.1 | S-4.8c: CI @-mentions the reviewer app | Live (probe `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-probe-1792110386`, kept): the Codex app answered a person's `@codex review` with its own review, but answered the same text from `github-actions[bot]` with "create a Codex account and connect to github" (run 38830182006); the apps act for the people who connected them. A token cannot list an account's app installations (`/user/installations`: 403 for a personal access token) | The request is posted by `aayu inspect <pr>` outside CI, under the human token (`HumanGitHub`), as one comment per head (`<!-- aayudha:review-request {"head":…,"reviewer":…} -->`): the mention first, then the criteria and the stated format. `build`'s Next names `aayu inspect N`, and a `delegate` sync posts it while it waits for inspect (`requestReview`). The request and the reply job have no pack, so they read the builder's `Aayudha-Model` claim from the head commit, the same trailer `verify` copies into the pack, and pick the same reviewer as the gate (`TestInspectThroughApp`). `inspect.DetectApps` reads org installations where allowed, then the comment, review, and check-run authors of the last 10 PRs; doctor and install report what they found and the config to set, and never edit config |
| D-4.8c.2 | S-4.8c: aayu reads the reviewer's reply in a stated format | Codex will not use a custom format: any `@codex` text with review words gets its native review, and task mode needs a Codex environment set up in the ChatGPT UI. Its review is a receipt (`<!-- codex-review-receipt:v1 -->` and "Reviewed commit:" with the first ten hex digits of the commit), as a review body when it has findings and as a comment ("Didn't find any major issues") when it has none, plus inline comments with a P0–P3 badge on that commit. Its `codex-review-state:v1` summary comment is not reliable | The stated format is a fenced `aayudha-review` JSON block (`AppSchema`: the review schema plus `head` and `reviewed`), read only from the reviewer's login, newest first; one that fails JSON or the schema is `malformed-reply`, one for another head is skipped. For Codex, its native review counts: a receipt naming the exact head (review or comment) and its inline findings on that commit (P0/P1 → `fix`, else `merge`); it judges no criterion, so each is `unclear` unless a P0/P1 finding names "criterion N" (then `unmet`). In human mode an APPROVED review of the head by the approver counts. Any other post by the reviewer after the request is `malformed-reply`; nothing is `no-reply`. Both exit 4 and are not fix cycles |
| D-4.8c.3 | S-4.8c: the reply turns into the gate's verdict | A comment or review cannot be a `pull_request_target` event, and a status set by a comment-triggered job could be set by any job | Triggers `issue_comment` (created, edited) and `pull_request_review` (submitted, edited) start only the `reply` job (`actions: write`, read access, default-branch checkout, no secret; a review event runs the PR's copy of the file, which can only ask). It runs `aayudha inspect "$PR" --ci`: with a reply for the head from the chosen reviewer, it waits for the head's `pull_request_target` run and, when `aayudha/merge-gate` passed and `aayudha/inspect` failed, calls rerun-failed-jobs, so the base-copy run's inspect check carries the verdict. The `gate`, `inspect`, and `pack` jobs run only on `pull_request_target`; the concurrency group splits gate and reply. This repository's own gate failed on #225 only because it installs dev's binary, which did not know `[inspect] apps` yet |
| D-4.8c.4 | S-4.8c: reviewers @claude, @cursor, @amp, @codex; the different-family rule; Q4 | Amp has no app or bot login: it acts as the account that runs it, so its reply cannot be told from a person's. Cursor runs models of several vendors. On the founder's account (2026-10-16): Codex is installed and reviews; the Cursor app is installed but answered "Skipping Bugbot: Bugbot is disabled for this repository"; Claude did not answer (not installed, or Code Review off). In solo mode the agents use the founder's token | `amp` is known but never chosen (doctor says why). `cursor` is host-managed until `[inspect.app_families] cursor` names a family. The human fallback is an approval of the head by `[autonomy] approver`; in solo mode it is attributed, not verified, like the build record. Q4: a high-risk PR keeps the single reviewer; peer mode needs two model reviewers and does not apply to an app, and inspect notes it |
| D-4.8c.5 | Decision 2: (a) "no test for X" notes become per-criterion verdicts and are not raised on `_test` files; (b) a sealed coverage manifest. Decision 3: weakened checks flagged. P2: count tests that only restate the code | The verification research measured all 8 inspect false positives as P2 "no test for …" findings on `*_test.go` files | #216: the filter drops `test-gap` (a test-category finding on a test file) and the new `test-value` category; both are counted in the summary, never posted. `TestGapVerdicts`: a P0/P1 gap makes its criterion `unmet`, a lesser one turns `met` into `unclear`, one with no criterion is a note. `inspect.Seal` lists every changed file as reviewed, skipped (generated, deleted), or unreviewed (past the 120 kB prompt cap, or not in a reply's `reviewed`), with a SHA-256; an unreviewed file turns `merge` into `fix`. `inspect.Weakened` flags, from the diff alone: fewer assertions or a new skip in a test file, fewer CI steps, `continue-on-error`, `if: false`, `\|\| true`, and a threshold moved in its loose direction; the inspect comment and the proof comment show them; nothing blocks on them. Nothing from Open Code Review is vendored or called |
| D-4.8c.6 | P6: a deterministic risk line on the proof comment | A "protected path" in a product repository is what CODEOWNERS names; a catch-all owner names every file | #228: `inspect.RiskOf`, run by the pack job on the PR diff with the base checkout's layers, never-layers, and CODEOWNERS: high for a never-layer footprint, a specific CODEOWNERS rule, a dependency manifest with added lines, or a migration path; medium for 3+ layers, more than 400 changed lines, or more than 20 files; low otherwise. The proof comment opens with the tier, the reasons, and up to five files to read first; it approves nothing |
| D-4.8c.7 | Decision 4 (P5): for Bug stories the gate reruns each mapped proving test on the base commit; advisory four weeks, then blocking, by a dated config value | `internal/verify` is owned by the feature-map thread. Only Go tests are mapped by name today | #229: `verify --ci` in the gate (full floors, PR with a `type:bug` story) runs `redOnBase` (`internal/cli/redbase.go`): a temporary worktree at the merge base, the PR's changed `*_test.go` and `testdata` files from the head, and `go test -json -run <mapped tests>` in the verify sandbox. Each criterion is red on base, green on base, or inconclusive (did not run, e.g. a new API). The `red-on-base` check lands in the pack and the floor table: `pass` with no green criterion, `skipped` with "advisory until 2026-11-13" before `[verify] red_on_base_blocking_from` (default `2026-11-13`; empty keeps it advisory), `fail` from that date. Inconclusive never blocks. Non-Go products get no check |
| D-4.8c.8 | P3: when a required check fails, aayu @-mentions the builder agent to fix it, within the 2-round cap | The builder of a delegated PR is reached through its session (Cursor run, Amp thread, Claude cloud session, Codex task, or the local CLI), not a PR mention: Codex's task mode needs an environment, and Amp has no mention | #231: a `delegate` sync that finds a failed required check on the head (`aayudha/merge-gate`, `aayudha/pack`, `[verify] upstream_checks`; never `protected-paths` or `aayudha/inspect`) sends one follow-up per head through the receiver's channel, with each check's link and the last 60 lines of its job log, redacted and fenced as data. Rounds share inspect's two-cycle cap, then a human is paged. `build --resume` (no delegation) is unchanged: the agent running it reads the checks itself |
| D-4.8c.9 | S-4.8c: prove it live, at least a reply aayu parses and a missing reply that fails closed | No app writes the stated format on the founder's account (Codex uses its own; Claude absent; Bugbot off) | `TestLiveInspectApp` (`internal/cli/inspect_live_test.go`; `AAYU_LIVE=1`, `AAYU_LIVE_VERSION`, `AAYU_LIVE_KEEP=1`), unchanged `init --gates` merge gate, no secret, `[inspect] apps = ["claude", "codex"]`. Run 1 on `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-1792114167236777636` (kept, commit 22aad5c): PR 4 (builder trailer `claude-opus-5-5`, reviewer codex): inspect on 7f7036a failed `no-reply`; `aayu inspect 4` posted `@codex review` as the founder (issuecomment-6206078851); Codex answered with a receipt two minutes later; the reply job re-ran run 38835805257 (attempt 2) and `aayudha/inspect` went green, `merge`, from "comment 6206103247 by chatgpt-codex-connector[bot] (Codex's own review of 7f7036a6fb9a)", coverage complete. PR 5 (trailer `gpt-6.1-sol`, reviewer claude): inspect on 24f76cc failed `no-reply`; `@claude review` got no answer; a person's forged `aayudha-review` merge block ran the reply job (run 38835900239: "no reply from claude for this head; nothing to re-run") and the check stayed red. Run 2, the full test passing (1083 s, commit 8356fb7, kept as `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-1792115145474361918`): PR 4 on 221013b failed `no-reply`, the request (issuecomment-6206294437) drew Codex's receipt (comment 6206322212), the reply job (run 38838087590: "re-ran the failed jobs of run 38837238758 so its inspect check reads the reply from codex") turned the check green with `merge`; PR 5 on 930df99 stayed red after the forged block (run 38837368351: "no reply from claude for this head; nothing to re-run"). Codex did not review either PR on open. Not shown live: the stated format from a real app, a Codex P0/P1 finding turning `fix`, a malformed app reply (unit-tested with Codex's "create an environment" answer) |
| D-4.8c.10 | S-4.8c: CI runs only deterministic checks in every repository aayudha sets up; aayudha's own gateway for evals only | Not changed here, pending the founder: the `aayudha-triage.yml` template (WP2.9) still classifies intake with a model in the product's Actions, in the `ai-gateway` environment; this repository's `inspect-live.yml`, `aayudha-model-lanes.yml` (D-8.1c), `models-live.yml`, and `ux-review-live.yml` call the gateway for things other than evals | Raised as founder decisions in the report of this work; no code changed |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP3.2c (agent environments)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers. Each host's documentation was read on
2026-10-16; CLI behaviour was checked against the CLIs named in the rows.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-3.2c | WP5.5b and the research note §6.2: `.agents/setup` is hand-written; `doctor` checks that Amp has it, writes `.cursor/environment.json`, and prints the Codex values; Claude Code needs a workflow running it before claude-code-action | Founder request (2026-10-16, relayed by the orchestrating thread): aayu sets up agent environments for any repository it is used in, as well as Amp's orb setup does: discover from the code, interview only for what the code cannot say, generate each host's native configuration from one spec, verify in a clean environment and fix until it passes, record a verified environment per host, and report verified, stale, or missing; detect which agent CLIs and clouds the user has; hand-offs run on the cloud agent by default (the local CLI is an explicit switch); no AI keys in a product repository's CI. This extends the plan; the hand-written script is replaced by a generated one | WP3.2c, one PR to `dev`, owned by this thread; routing (cloud by default, the missing-receiver message) stays with the delegation thread (WP5.5c), which calls `agentenv.Detect` and `agentenv.Ensure`. Placement: `install --env` (with `--verify`, `--cloud`, `--record`) and two `doctor` rows (`agent-env`, `agents`); no new verb (V7). `internal/delegate/env.go` moved to `internal/agentenv` (agreed with the delegation thread) |
| D-3.2c.a | S-3.2c: "one shared setup script that every host calls is the likely shape; prove or disprove that" | Sources: Cursor [cloud agent setup](https://cursor.com/docs/cloud-agent/setup) and the [environment.json schema](https://www.cursor.com/schemas/environment.schema.json) (`install`, `start`, `terminals`, `build.dockerfile`, snapshots; read from the default branch; a build keeps the disk only); Codex [cloud environments](https://developers.openai.com/codex/environments/cloud-environments) (UI only: Install script, Start skill, the Package managers domain preset, network secrets) and [Legacy environments](https://developers.openai.com/codex/cloud/environments) (setup and maintenance scripts, a 12 h cache, secrets only during setup); Claude Code [cloud environments](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments) (setup script as root on Ubuntu 24.04, Trusted network allowlist, environment variables) and [on the web](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web) (`claude --cloud`, `SessionStart` hooks with `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE`); Amp [orb customizing](https://ampcode.com/docs/orbs/customizing) (`.agents/setup`, `.agents/resume`, the project pre-setup script, 72 h snapshots, Debian 12) and [spawning orbs](https://ampcode.com/docs/cli/spawning-orbs). Every host either runs a repository file (Amp, Cursor, the Claude Code hook) or accepts one command in its UI (Codex), and every base has bash, apt, and root or sudo | Proven: one generated `.agents/setup` (pinned versions with SHA-256 for every download; Go by `GOTOOLCHAIN` when a Go exists, else the tarball; Node by the nodejs.org index and `SHASUMS256.txt`; pnpm and yarn through a pinned corepack; uv from its GitHub release; rustup from static.rust-lang.org; system packages through `need_apt`, which makes no apt call on a warm run; root or a sudo user), `.agents/check` (asserts each toolchain version on a login shell's `PATH`, starts the services, runs the build and tests, prints `aayu-env ok <fingerprint>`), and, with services, `.agents/start` and `.agents/resume`. Each host's native file calls them: Amp natively, Cursor `.cursor/environment.json` (`install`, `start`; aayu owns `name`, `install`, `start` and keeps the owner's keys), Claude Code a `SessionStart` hook in `.claude/settings.json` that runs setup only when `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE` is `true`, Codex the Install script `bash .agents/setup` printed with the Start skill. A Cursor `build.dockerfile` is not generated: one script serves every host, and Cursor snapshots its result |
| D-3.2c.b | S-3.2c: "where a host can only be configured in its web UI, aayu prints the exact values and steps, then verifies that the result works" | Codex environments are created only in its UI (codex 0.163.0's `codex cloud` has `exec`, `status`, `list`, `apply`, `diff`, and nothing that creates an environment). Claude Code's `/remote-env` only selects an environment; network access and variables are set in the UI. Cursor needs a dashboard step only for network rules and secrets | `install --env` prints, per host, numbered steps with the exact values: the Install script and Start skill, the domains beyond each host's default allowlist (computed from the spec, including hosts named in custom steps), and the variables by name. It then verifies the live result with a probe task that runs `.agents/check` and replies with its last line: `install --env --cloud` runs it itself where a CLI can start a task and read the reply (Amp `amp -ox --stream-json`, whose probe thread is archived once its reply is read, by `amp -x`'s default and again with `amp threads archive`, founder decision 2026-10-16, while its URL stays in the record as evidence; Codex `codex cloud exec --env <id>` then `codex cloud status`), and `install --env --host <h> --record '<reply>'` records a reply copied from Cursor or Claude Code. A reply counts only when it carries the current fingerprint |
| D-3.2c.c | WP5.5b: Claude Code is reached through claude-code-action | Founder rule 6 (2026-10-16): no AI key in a product repository's CI; the action needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` as an Actions secret | No workflow is generated or checked; the doctor row that looked for one is gone. Claude Code's cloud is `claude --cloud` from the user's claude.ai sign-in (the delegation thread's WP5.5c) |
| D-3.2c.d | S-3.2c: "runs the generated setup in a clean container, or the closest equivalent" | No host publishes its exact machine image except Codex (`ghcr.io/openai/codex-universal`, about 44 GB unpacked). An Amp orb is Debian 12 but already carries a current git (2.55 in `/opt/git` on the orb that built this); plain `debian:12` has git 2.39, and aayu requires git ≥ 2.40 (`internal/doctor/checks.go`), so three of this repository's tests failed in the first clean container. bookworm-backports has no newer git. With `set -e`, a failure inside an `a && b \|\| c` list does not stop a script, so a step written that way failed silently | Profiles (`agentenv.Profiles`): Amp `debian:12` as `user` with sudo, Cursor `ubuntu:24.04` as `ubuntu` with sudo, Codex `codex-universal:latest` as root, Claude Code `ubuntu:24.04` as root, each with only git, CA certificates, and sudo added. codex-universal's entrypoint configures its language runtimes and then runs bash with the container's arguments, so it is kept (`Profile.BashEntrypoint`). The check runs in a fresh login shell with the image's own environment and nothing setup exported: codex-universal puts its own runtimes (uv 0.7.22 in `~/.local/bin`, nvm's Node, mise's Go) ahead of `/usr/local/bin`, so setup now puts `/usr/local/bin` first in later shells when a pinned uv is shadowed, as it already did for Go; and its mise sets `GOROOT` to its Go 1.25.1, which made the pinned Go 1.27.2 compile with the old tools (`compile: version "go1.25.1" does not match go tool version "go1.27.2"`), so setup resets a foreign `GOROOT` for later shells. `Verify` tars the clone as it would be with the generated files committed, runs setup cold, setup warm (idempotence), then the check in `env -i bash -lc` (an agent's fresh login shell), and records per-phase seconds. Its fix loop (three attempts) adds the apt package for a missing command and marks a dubious-ownership checkout safe, recording each fix as an answer so rediscovery keeps it. This repository's spec carries one human step: build git 2.55.0 from kernel.org (SHA-256 pinned, `NO_RUST`) only when git is older than 2.40, written as `if …; then …; fi` so a failure stops setup. Step labels are now single-quoted, so a label never runs the step's `$(…)` |
| D-3.2c.e | D-3.2a: `install --apply` is refused for agents (exit 4) | `install --env --apply` writes only repository files (no home edits, no machine paths) that a human reviews in the PR, and the delegation thread's `Ensure` must regenerate and verify before a cloud hand-off | Agents may run `install --env --apply` and `--verify`; `install --apply` for skills is still refused, and installing skills stays human-only. Confirmed by the founder (2026-10-16), recorded here against D-3.2a. `--cloud` starts its probe task through the user's own signed-in CLI, never with a key aayu holds |
| D-3.2c.f | S-3.2c: "know what the user has"; delegation calls detection on every hand-off | The delegation thread asked for no model, task, or network call, injectable probes, no secret values, and exact install and sign-in commands. Local signals checked against the CLIs (installed in a scratch HOME on 2026-10-16): `codex login status` exits 1 when signed out and names ChatGPT or an API key; `claude auth status --json` gives `loggedIn` and `authMethod` (`claude.ai`, `oauth_token`, `api_key`, `apiKeyHelper`, `third_party`, `none`); `cursor-agent status` prints `Not logged in` and exits 0; `amp account list` prints `No saved accounts.` or the `AMP_API_KEY` override | `agentenv.Detect(ctx, Probe) []Agent`: per agent the CLI path and version, `SignedIn`, `Cloud`, `CloudDetail`, and `Install` and `SignIn` command lines. Cloud: Codex needs a ChatGPT login and `[delegate] codex_env`; Cursor a `cursor-api-key` secret or `CURSOR_API_KEY`; Claude Code `authMethod` `claude.ai`; Amp a sign-in and an Amp project recorded for this repository by a live probe. `agentenv.Ensure(ctx, root, host, EnsureOptions{})` regenerates and verifies a missing or stale environment before a cloud hand-off, and returns an error wrapping `ErrNotLanded`, whose text is the next command, while the files are not on the default branch. `doctor`'s `agents` row reports Detect; its `agent-env` row reports each host |
| D-3.2c.g | S-3.2c: stale means "the toolchain changed" | A spec can drift three ways: the code asks for another toolchain, the generated files are edited, or the spec changes after verification | `.aayudha/env.toml` carries a fingerprint (SHA-256 of the requirements and setup steps, 16 hex digits). `agentenv.Status`: missing (no spec, no files, or never verified), stale (rediscovery differs, a generated file differs from the spec, or the record's fingerprint is not the spec's), verified (the container record matches; `live` when a cloud probe matched too). The generated scripts also load the dotenv file (`.env`, copied from `.env.example`) without overriding what a host sets: setup adds the loader to the shell profile, and `check` and `start` run it, since a test that reads `DATABASE_URL` failed in a clean container without it |

### Scope change and deviations recorded while retiring triage from product CI (WP2.9b, 0.2)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers. Founder decisions of 2026-10-16 (relayed by the
orchestrating thread) on D-4.8c.10; D-4.8c.4's limits (Amp cannot review, Cursor needs `app_families` and Bugbot,
Claude is not installed, the solo-mode fallback approval is attributed, not verified) were accepted as recorded.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-2.9b | WP2.9: `aayudha-triage.yml`, installed by `init --gates`, classifies new intake issues in Actions with the triage lane's model (keys in the `ai-gateway` environment); ground rule 5: "`triage` in Actions"; WP2.6: `--gates` installs the triage workflow; the registry's triage `ModeNote` "direct in Actions" | Founder decision (2026-10-16): product CI never calls a model. Triage runs from the user's own agent (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, or Amp) on request, or by @-mentioning an installed app. A repository that already has the workflow is told and offered its removal | `init --gates` no longer installs `aayudha-triage.yml`: the template, its embed, `initrepo.TriageWorkflow`, and its golden are removed, and this repository's own copy is deleted with its `.aayudha/state.json` entry. `doctor.RetiredWorkflows` names it with the reason. Every `aayu init` (with or without `--gates`) runs `retired` first: for each retired workflow present whose `name:` is aayudha's, a dry run plans its removal, a human confirms (`--yes` for a human; refused for an agent) before it is deleted and forgotten from the init state, and without confirmation it is kept with a note. A product's own file of the same name is never touched. `doctor` gains a `retired-workflows` row that warns while one is present. The triage verb is unchanged: the host's model passes `--as` (the skill), and without it the binary calls the triage lane only for a person who holds a key; its `ModeNote` now says "the host's model passes --as; never in product CI". `TestModelChecksNeverGateAndNeverShip` fails if any workflow `init --gates` installs names `ai-gateway`, `AAYU_SECRET_`, `AAYU_MODELS__`, or `AAYU_AI_GATEWAY`. Rule 5, WP2.6, and WP2.9 are unchanged in text; this row records where their "triage in Actions" no longer applies. The guides (adopt, how-architecture, how-models) no longer list a model key for CI |
| D-2.9b | Founder decision (2026-10-16): keep this repository's `inspect-live`, `aayudha-model-lanes`, `models-live`, and `ux-review-live`, only as non-gating checks of aayudha's own model code; none is a required check or part of a promotion gate, and none is ever installed into a product repository | Checked on 2026-10-16: the `dev-integration` ruleset requires build, generate-drift, lint, commits, security, docs, and release-snapshot; `protected-paths-codeowner-review` requires protected-paths; `rolling-main-promotion` requires no status check; ci.yml's rolling and stable gates (integration, benchmarks, evals-gate, extended integration, security scans, smoke) name none of the four, and evals-gate reads `evals.yml` only. The four run on `workflow_dispatch`, and `models-live` also on pushes to dev and a schedule, never on a pull request. `embed.go` embeds no `.github/` file, and `init --gates` installs only `initrepo.GateWorkflows` | No behaviour change. Each of the four files gains a header saying it is non-gating, never a promotion gate, and never installed into a product. `TestModelChecksNeverGateAndNeverShip` (workflows_test.go) holds what a file can show: none of the four runs on a `pull_request*` event, so none can report a required check on a PR head; ci.yml names neither their files nor their check names. The rulesets are GitHub settings outside the repository, so the test cannot hold them; this row records them as checked The `ai-gateway` environment stays for `evals.yml` and these four |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP7.1b (native scheduled and event loops, 0.2)

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-7.1b | WP7.1 and D-7.1a: a loop is scheduled only (`cron`), rendered for Amp (`set_schedule` input), Cursor (Terraform), and the Actions fallback | Founder decisions (2026-10-16, confirmed as gating v0.2.0): aayu sets up scheduled automations (daily, weekly, monthly, any cron) and event-based ones (an issue opened, a PR opened or updated, a required check failed, a Feedback issue, a release published, map drift or a failed sweep) in each agent's native tooling, Amp, Cursor, Claude Code, and Codex, per repository and project, in the user's own session; the Actions fallback never answers an event with a model. This extends WP7.1; it replaces nothing | WP7.1b, four PRs: the format and renders in #247 and #248, the default loops in #249, and the entry point in #250. A loop sets `cron` or `on`, never both: `on` is one of `issues.opened`, `issues.labeled`, `pull_request.opened`, `pull_request.synchronize`, `check_suite.completed`, `release.published`, with an optional `filter` (`label` on issues and PRs, a required one for `issues.labeled`; `conclusion` `failure` or `success` on check suites), and its command may name the subject (`{number}`, `{tag}`). `Parse` refuses both or neither, an unknown event, a filter the event has no field for, a label outside `[A-Za-z0-9:._/ -]`, the wrong placeholder, and `setup` on an event loop (`TestParseRefuses`). Renders: Amp `loops/amp/<name>.json` is the `set_schedule` input for a schedule or the `create_github_subscription` input (an orb spawner, `destination` new-orb) for an event; Cursor `loops/cursor/aayudha-loops.tf` gains `git_pull_request` (`pr_action` opened or pushed), `git_ci_completed` (`condition`), and `git_label` (issues, `on_added`, `label_name`) triggers; Claude Code `loops/claude/routines.md` and Codex `loops/codex/scheduled-tasks.md` give the exact values per loop (D-7.1e); Actions gets one record job per event loop, which runs the halt check and writes the event to the run summary, with no checkout, no install, and no model. A host without the trigger does not render the loop and says why. The guard `aayu hook loop:<name>` starts every run on every host and is due for an event loop whenever its own event started the run; halt (exit 4) and `[budget]` (exit 6) still stop it first (`TestLoopGuardEvent`). Golden: `TestGolden` renders one fixture of scheduled and event loops for all five outputs |
| D-7.1e | S-7.1b: render each host's native form, or its exact steps where only a web page exists, citing its documentation; founder (2026-10-16): research session hooks and repository event triggers apart, build the host × event matrix, render the native form wherever one exists, and render a session hook where only that works | Read again on 2026-10-16 from the primary sources below. **Two kinds of hook.** Session hooks fire only inside a session the user started, never on a repository event: Claude Code's `SessionStart`, `PreToolUse`, `Stop`, and the rest (<https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks>; `SessionStart` stdout becomes the agent's context), Codex's `.codex/hooks.json` or `[hooks]` in `config.toml` (<https://developers.openai.com/codex/hooks>; `SessionStart` stdout becomes developer context), Cursor's `.cursor/hooks.json` (<https://cursor.com/docs/agent/hooks>; `sessionStart` returns `additional_context`, is fire-and-forget, and does not run in Cursor cloud agents), and the Amp plugin's `session.start`, which aayudha's plugin turns into a notice to the user, not agent context. Repository event triggers start an agent on a GitHub event. **Amp** (<https://ampcode.com/docs/github>, and `create_github_subscription`'s own schema): orb spawners only for a newly opened issue or PR, each with an optional label (`issue_label`, `pr_label`), for one repository or every repository of a personal installation (`installation_id`); "comments and CI updates cannot be used to spawn new orbs"; comment (`issue-comments`, `pr-comments`, which also reports the merge) and CI subscriptions (`ci`: `workflow_run`, `check_run`, or `status`) need one issue, PR, or exact commit and continue an existing thread; a spawned thread is subscribed to its source's comments and reviews, not to pushes; a spawner keeps saved `instructions`. Live finding: a `pr_label` spawner fired for PR #252, whose label `gh pr create --label` added just after creation, although the docs say the label must be present at open. **Cursor** (<https://cursor.com/docs/cloud-agent/automations>, <https://cursor.com/help/ai-features/automations>, changelog <https://cursor.com/changelog/06-18-26>): Draft opened, PR opened, PR pushed, PR merged, Push to branch, PR commented; on GitHub also PR and issue label changed, CI completed, Workflow run completed, Issue comment, PR review comment, PR review submitted, Review thread updated; PR approved is GitLab and Bitbucket only; Webhook (an HTTP POST with the automation's key); no issue-opened and no release trigger; runs as Me or a service account. The `cursor/cursor` Terraform provider v0.8.0 (`terraform providers schema -json`) expresses `cron`, `git_pull_request` (`pr_action` opened, pushed, merged, commented; `ignore_draft_prs`, `comment_contains`), `git_push`, `git_ci_completed` (`condition` failure, success, any; `branch`, `ignore_base_failures`), `git_label` (issues or pull requests, `on_added`, `on_removed`, `label_name`), and `webhook`; draft opened, workflow run completed, issue comment, PR review comment, review submitted, and review thread updated are web-app only. **Claude Code**: routines' GitHub triggers are still Pull request (opened, closed, labeled, synchronize, …) and Release only, with no issue, check, or comment event (<https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines>); auto-fix answers CI failures and review comments on one PR a person turns it on for (`/autofix-pr`; <https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web>); Code Review reviews PRs on open or push, Team and Enterprise only (<https://code.claude.com/docs/en/code-review>); `@claude` on GitHub runs claude-code-action in Actions with an API key (<https://code.claude.com/docs/en/github-actions>). **Codex**: scheduled tasks run on a schedule and, on ChatGPT web and mobile for eligible plans, "from supported app events", which the docs do not enumerate and do not name GitHub; they are created in the app or by asking ChatGPT, with no API or CLI (<https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/automations>); Team Tasks take event triggers through the team's app connections, Business and Enterprise only, as the team's service account, with Gmail and Slack as the examples (<https://learn.chatgpt.com/codex/enterprise/teams>, `help.openai.com/articles/20001540`); the GitHub app reviews PRs automatically on PR events, and a person's `@codex review` or `@codex <task>` on an issue or PR starts a cloud task as that person (<https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/third-party/github>). **GitHub**: a `check_suite` created by Actions starts no workflow | The matrix, default-loop events (native = the host starts the run; chained; session = the user's next session; mention = a person posts it): issue opened (`triage-new`, label `intake`): Amp native spawner, Cursor native `git_label` (the form adds the label), Claude session, Codex session or a person's `@codex`; Feedback filed (`feedback`, label `type:feedback`): the same; PR opened or updated (no default loop): Amp native for opened, session for updated, Cursor native `git_pull_request`, Claude native routine, Codex session, review only by its app; CI failed (`ci-failed`): Amp **chained**, Cursor native `git_ci_completed` (`failure`), Claude session plus per-PR auto-fix, Codex session or a person's `@codex`; release published (`release-measure`): Amp session, Cursor session, Claude native routine, Codex session. Rendered: Amp's `ci-failed` is now `loops/amp/ci-failed.json`, a `pr-opened` spawner whose instructions subscribe the new thread to the PR head's CI (`ci`, this-thread), re-subscribe when a later event shows the head moved, and run the loop on a failure; it covers each PR's opening head and any head the thread sees, not a push made after the last event it received, and it starts one Amp thread per new PR. Cursor's provider pin is `~> 0.8`. Where a host has no trigger, the rendered file says so plainly ("Picked up by your next session") and `aayu hook session-start`, already each host's session-start hook, lists the events: for each event loop no host runs with its current digest in `loops/active.json`, it reads GitHub as the user (5-second limit, read-only, numbers and tags only, no untrusted titles), since the cursor in `.aayudha/state/loop-events.json` (first session: 24 hours), and prints each loop's events with its steps, which start with the halt and budget guard; the agent offers them to the person. Nothing runs between sessions. Codex and Claude also name what a person can do (`@codex <task>`, auto-fix), never posted by a workflow (no `allowed_bots`, D-4.5b.7); `@claude` is not installed, because it needs an API key in CI. A Cursor webhook trigger is not used: its key in product CI would be an agent credential. doctor and `install --loops` show such a loop as `session` (`TestSessionStartLoopEvents`, `TestGolden`, the doctor golden). **Proven live (2026-10-16):** Amp spawner `aayudha ci-failed (probe, label)` (ID 9c9cf356-b80f-4565-bf0e-71c576e41256, `pr_label` aayudha-loop-probe) and an unlabelled twin (f2401934-d066-444a-bd99-947a7cc86f0a, deleted after it fired) both spawned threads for probe PR #252 (T-eee086f2-d34b-42ca-a61a-d1ceddabf795, T-f3f2c175-afd2-4e27-ba02-21bfb082623f); the labelled thread followed the chained instructions: it subscribed itself to CI for the head commit 0e87572 (subscription fa0a91e1-d617-4895-8f53-9844afe227b5, `workflow_run`, this-thread), received the deliberate `docs` failure (run 38869261694), checked that the head had not moved, ran `aayu hook loop:ci-failed` (exit 0, "event: check_suite.completed with conclusion failure") and `AAYU_AGENT=1 aayu status --work` (no work in flight, so no `build --delegate`), and did not run again for the rollup event of the same commit. Both spawners were then deleted, the PR closed, and the probe label removed. The scheduled `aayudha triage` and spawner `aayudha triage-new` (D-7.1d) stay live. Rendered and documented only: Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, and the session hook on a real host session (tested against the fake GitHub) **Superseded for 0.2 by S-7.1e (2026-10-16):** event loops are deferred, so nothing above is rendered or installed in 0.2; the research and the matrix stay as the basis for the later version, the session pick-up is removed, and the live spawner `aayudha triage-new` is paused. **Correction:** this row and S-7.1b said Claude Code had no file or API route to create a routine; it has a CLI route, `/schedule` in Claude Code v2.1.225 or later with a claude.ai subscription login, which creates a scheduled routine conversationally, `/schedule update` sets a custom cron, `/schedule run` runs one now, and the shortest interval is one hour (<https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines>, read 2026-10-16) |
| S-7.1c | WP2.9 and S-2.9b: triage runs from the user's own agent on request; WP5.5: `gc`; WP1.7: doctor; WP4.5b: `verify --map`; #231 (P3): a failed required check goes back to the builder in `build --delegate`'s sync | Founder decisions (2026-10-16): a weekly triage loop replaces `aayudha-triage.yml`, retired from product CI (#243), and a weekly housekeeping loop runs `aayu gc`, stale leases and worktrees, stale Ready issues, doctor drift, and feature-map drift, with existing verbs only; the event examples above | #249. New loops in `loops/`, rendered here and copied by products: `triage` (Mondays 09:30) and `triage-new` (`issues.opened` with label `intake`, which every intake form adds); `housekeeping` (Fridays 16:00: `gc`, then `doctor`, `verify --map`, and `ready --check` on each `status:ready` issue untouched for 14 days); `feedback` (`issues.opened` with `type:feedback`: report the skills `evolve` lists as ready); `ci-failed` (`check_suite.completed`, `failure`: for a delegated story, `build <story> --delegate <receiver>` once, whose sync sends the failed check back, #231); `release-measure` (`release.published`: `measure`). `triage` and `housekeeping` have no Actions job (they need a host's model, and `gc` writes as the human) |
| D-7.1c | S-7.1c: "label, prioritise and route new issues with `aayu triage`"; "stale Ready issues" | `aayu triage` took one issue and, without `--as`, called the triage lane's model, so a loop could not run it first with no model. triage only labels the class (feedback, bug, incident), which routes the issue; no verb sets a priority. No verb lists Ready issues | `aayu triage` with no issue lists the open intake issues, oldest first, read-only and with no model call (`--as` without an issue is a usage error; `TestTriageListsIntake`); the loop's agent then runs `aayu triage <issue> --as <class>` for each. Priority stays with `slice` and the humans. The housekeeping prompt reads Ready issues with `gh issue list` (a read) and checks each with `aayu ready --check`. **Founder decisions (2026-10-16), accepted as built:** triage sets only the class, never a priority; drift and failed-sweep hand-offs stay with `verify-sweep` and `verify-maintain`, with no separate event loop |

With Amp recorded for every loop, the Actions fallback's guard answered `not due here` for all of them (until the cost decision below). **Founder decision (2026-10-16) on loop cost, built:** a loop that needs no model and runs with the workflow token runs in the free GitHub Actions fallback, not in an agent tool. It is a loop attribute, `agent = false` (unset means an agent loop), so product repos get the same split: `loops.Parse` refuses `actions` or `setup` on an agent loop and an agent tool, `bot = true`, or a `prompt` on an `agent = false` loop; `aayudha-loops.yml` renders only `agent = false` loops (no bot-token step any more); `install --loops` sets up only the agent loops in the chosen tool and names the ones Actions runs; doctor shows each loop with one runner, its tool or `actions`, and `double` when an `agent = false` loop is still recorded in a tool; the guard's `not due here` branch is gone, since an agent loop has no Actions job (`TestParseRefuses`, `TestInstallLoops`, `TestChecksGolden`). Classification of the default loops: **Actions** `digest` (`status --digest` is deterministic, reads with the workflow token, and pages through the `[notify]` file channel, kept as a run artifact; `TestLoopDigestOnSchedule`) and `verify-sweep` (`verify --check all` runs every behaviour check with no model and no GitHub write; a failed check turns the run red; its `--maintain drift` hand-off is dropped, because in Actions it needs the bot App and only records the brief, D-4.5b.7, so drift is caught on every PR by `verify --map` and handed off weekly by `verify-maintain`). **Agent** `ci-failed` (judges which failed check to send back, and `build --delegate` acts as the person), `decisions` (`decide` writes as the bot, `bot = true`, and applies recommendations), `evolve` (model-written skill rewrites and PRs), `housekeeping` (`gc` writes as the human, and the agent reads doctor, map drift, and Ready issues), `release-measure` (needs integration credentials or exports and the agent's release check), `retro` (model findings filed as Feedback under the bot or the person), `seat-review` (a per-seat judgement), `triage` and `triage-daily` (the agent chooses each `--as`), and `verify-maintain` (`bot = true`; the agent follows the maintain brief and opens the map PR). `decisions` and `ci-failed` now run once each weekday morning, at 09:15 and 09:45 `[time] tz`, not three times a day. **Live on this repository (2026-10-16, 14:15 UTC):** `update_schedule` from each holder thread moved `decisions` (e0a167fd-c25c-58b0-800b-ca29297ec7ce on T-01a144e7-5bbf-71ff-91b9-be10e830bf7c) and `ci-failed` (ea64c910-00cb-5e3e-b362-61495500aa51 on T-01a144e7-4ef7-7682-8b58-e734cd8e355e) to `Mon–Fri at 09:15` and `09:45 Europe/London`, next run 2026-10-19 08:15 and 08:45 UTC; the Amp schedules of `digest` (938323bb-30c5-57e6-bde5-160b8277edf1) and `verify-sweep` (daa6a559-32cb-55a4-ae75-f4013822812f) were cleared and their holder threads T-01a144e7-684a-73ef-b6e9-5200a82cec3b and T-01a144e7-e51d-709c-815c-6c14a69e8053 archived; `loops/active.json` forgets both and re-records the two moved loops. Doctor's `loops` row passes with 12 active (2 in Actions), 0 stale, 0 missing, 0 double: 10 in `amp`, `digest` and `verify-sweep` in `actions`. Amp threads a week (fresh mode starts one per firing, the guard's `not due` included): about 22.5 (ci-failed 5, decisions 5, triage-daily 7, triage 1, housekeeping 1, release-measure 1, retro 1, verify-maintain 1, and the two monthly loops about 0.46), down from about 54.5 (ci-failed 15, decisions 15, digest 5, verify-sweep 7, and the same others). Actions starts two runs per firing of each `agent = false` loop, one per UTC offset of the zone, and the guard skips the one for the other offset: 24 runs a week. The spawner `aayudha triage-new` stays paused. **Founder decision (2026-10-16) on triage, built:** `triage-daily` runs on weekdays only (Mon–Fri 09:00 `[time] tz`), and the weekly `triage` loop leaves the defaults. | D-7.1g | S-7.1e: `aayu install --loops` is the one entry point that sets a repository's loops up in one tool | Defect from the live megaman test (D-5.5c.12, defect 5; PR #269, 68b65fa): in a fresh repository, `install --loops` stopped with "no loops/*.toml in this repository; `aayu init --gates` adds the defaults", but `init --gates` only renders the loops a checkout already has and writes no `loops/*.toml`, so the Claude Code and Codex plans failed until the person copied aayudha's `loops/` in | Founder decision (2026-10-16): fix before v0.2.0 (#268). The default loops (`loops/*.toml`) are embedded in the binary (`aayudha.Assets`). In a repository with none, `install --loops` writes them (`loops.Defaults`) and every tool's render (`loops.Files`: `loops/amp/`, `loops/cursor/`, `loops/claude/`, `loops/codex/`, and `.github/workflows/aayudha-loops.yml`), says so, and then plans as usual; `--record` there still refuses. `loops.Defaults` leaves out `setup` and `setup_run` with the comment above them, because they are this repository's own (its example's Playwright, D-8.1b); a product adds its own. Regression test `TestInstallLoopsAddsDefaults` (claude and codex in a fresh checkout; it fails without the fix with that exit 2). **Proven on a scratch repository (2026-10-16, a local `git init` with origin `rahulnakmol/scratch-loops`, deleted afterwards):** `aayu install --loops --host <tool>` without `--apply`, a fresh scratch per tool, exit 0 for amp, cursor, claude, and codex; each added 25 files, listed digest and verify-sweep as run by Actions, and gave its tool's steps: amp `--apply` for 9 loops (`loops/amp/*.json`), cursor `loops/cursor/aayudha-loops.tf` and `automate.md`, claude 8 `/schedule` prompts in `loops/claude/routines.md`, codex 8 prompts in `loops/codex/scheduled-tasks.md` (seat-review runs only in amp and cursor); no `setup_run` or `aayudha/floors` in the scratch's `verify-sweep.toml` or workflow; a second run added nothing |
| D-7.1h | D-7.1g: a product's default `verify-sweep` has no `setup`, so a web product's behaviour checks have no dependencies or browser in Actions | D-7.1g left `setup` and `setup_run` out of the defaults because they install this repository's own example tooling; a product then had to write its own | Founder-approved follow-up (2026-10-17). `loops.Defaults(assets, root)` replaces a default loop's `setup` and `setup_run` with the product's, from its lockfiles (`loops.ProductSetup`): `pnpm-lock.yaml` gives node and pnpm, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps chromium`; `bun.lock` or `bun.lockb` gives bun, `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, `bunx playwright install --with-deps chromium`; both give node, pnpm, and bun, installing with pnpm; `package-lock.json` gives node, `npm ci`, `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`; no lockfile, no setup. A comment names the lockfile and says to edit it. New pinned tool setup `bun`: `oven-sh/setup-bun@0c5077e51419868618aeaa5fe8019c62421857d6 # v2.2.0` (the v2.2.0 tag commit). Tests: `TestDefaultsSetup`, one row per case, including the pinned `uses:` lines in the rendered workflow; regression `TestInstallLoopsAddsDefaults`, whose codex run now has a `pnpm-lock.yaml` and fails without the change (no `pnpm exec playwright install` in the workflow). **Proven (2026-10-17, plan mode, `install --loops --host claude`, local scratch repositories deleted afterwards):** a pnpm scratch (`pnpm-lock.yaml` from pnpm 12) got `setup = ["node", "pnpm"]` and the two pnpm commands, with `actions/setup-node` and `pnpm/action-setup` in the workflow; a Bun scratch (`bun.lock` from `bun add -d @playwright/test`) got `setup = ["bun"]` and the two Bun commands, with `oven-sh/setup-bun@0c5077e… # v2.2.0`. In each, the install command passed locally (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`; `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, then `bunx playwright --version` 1.64.1); the browser install with `--with-deps` needs root and was left to the runner |

### Deviations recorded while adding the tell skill's scored eval (D-7.5b, 0.2)

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-7.5b | WP7.5 and the founder's skill bar (2026-10-17): every skill in scope scores at least 4.0 of 5 (mean over k = 5, judge gpt-6.1-sol, the `evals/eval.toml` harness) with no deterministic floor failure; a new group starts advisory and gates only after two consecutive full runs at 4.0 or above, proposed in its own PR | `tell` had no scored eval (before: none). `aayu tell --from` checked only links (a work item linked must be a proven Feature) and single avoided words, so copy could name an unproven Feature without linking it ("Team workspaces are coming soon"), describe a proven one without its proof link, call itself "the fastest", or use the exclamation marks a brand avoids, and pass. Tier 2 runs only where the `ai-gateway` environment allows (dev, rolling, main; D-3.4a), so the cases merge before their first tier-2 run | `tell.CheckDraft` (what `aayu tell --from` now runs; the repository's site pages and `release`'s generated notes keep `tell.Check`): no superlative (`tell.Superlatives`, and "most" or "least" with a quality only a comparison proves); no exclamation mark when the brand's avoid list names them; no sentence that names a Feature without authoritative packs, linked or not (a sentence names a Feature when it has every content word of its title, plurals folded, within a run of three words more than the title has, so "Note: the file holds the claims" does not name Claim notes); a proven Feature the copy names is linked at least once; release, changelog, and launch copy links a proven Feature when there is one. Paraphrases ("reassign claims in bulk" for Bulk reassignment) are left to the judge: a stemmer would refuse more honest sentences than it catches. Eval: a `tell` case type (`evals/README.md`; `internal/evals/tell.go`): the skill gets `aayu tell`'s brief, the case's own `design/brand.yaml`, and the grounding as untrusted data, writes the copy, revises once on `CheckDraft` and `scope` findings, and scores 1 without the judge if it still fails; three cases on one grounding (`evals/cases/tell/grounding.json`, Benefits QA: proven #7, #15, #16; unproven #9 with a shipped story on an advisory pack, #20 with none): `release-notes` (v1.5.0 notes that describe #15 and #16 from their packs and count the other two changes), `guide-export` (a how-to for #7 that names only the controls its criteria name), and `launch-saved-filters` (a request that also asks for #20, "the fastest", and hours saved; `scope` rules for a time saving and a hint at an unproven Feature). Rubrics: every claim restates a met criterion, a Feature field, or a Result; the voice matches the case's brand. Group `tell` (8 USD, `gate = "advisory"`, dispatch only). The skill body gains the claims rules, the brand's tone words as visible choices, and a shape per kind, with examples outside the eval's fixture. Tests: `TestCheckDraft`, `TestTier1TellRules`, `TestTier2TellRevisesOnceThenJudges`, `TestGroups`. Tier-2 runs and their scores follow in D-7.5c onwards |
| D-7.5c | D-7.5b round 1: the first tier-2 runs of the tell group, on the skill as D-7.5b merged it (cab1b99), filtered first as the founder asked | Run [39007003137](https://github.com/rahulnakmol/agentic-ayudha/actions/runs/39007003137) (dispatch, `-f cases='^tell/launch-saved-filters$'`, 2.17 USD, judge gpt-6.1-sol, skill claude-opus-5-5): launch-saved-filters 3, 2, 2, 3, 3 (mean 2.60). Run [39007395282](https://github.com/rahulnakmol/agentic-ayudha/actions/runs/39007395282) (`-f cases='^tell/(release-notes\|guide-export)$'`, 3.39 USD): guide-export 5, 5, 5, 2, 3 (4.00); release-notes 3, 5, 5, 3, 5 (4.20). Before, over the 15 runs: mean 3.60; no floor failure, no error, one run revised. Spend 5.56 of 30 USD. From the judge's reasons (who was wrong): **skill**, launch copy carried the stage artefact as a visible "Positioning line" heading and sections on scope and checks, which the persona's "put the stage's artefact in your output as a heading" invited; **skill**, extrapolation beyond a criterion: "one click away", "only you can rename or delete it" (the pack proves only that another reviewer cannot), "today's claims", the Columns picker as a way to reorder columns (the criterion proves only that the export keeps the order shown), "keep scrolling and working"; **skill**, who it helps written as an achieved outcome ("the next reviewer does not start again", "no longer sit in personal notebooks"); **skill**, "built to be used by everyone" from the a11y and keyboard floors; **case**, the rubrics allowed claims only from met criteria, Feature fields, and (launch only) a Result, so the judge marked down an exactly stated passing floor (a launch run's "0 critical and 0 serious") and the guide's Result as untraceable, although both are in the pack the founder's rule says claims trace to | Rubrics (demonstrably too narrow, so changed): a claim also traces to a pack when it states a passing floor as exactly what it checked, or a Result in its own unit and population, in all three. Skill: restate, never extrapolate (a control does only what its criterion shows; no interaction counts, exclusive or inverse rules, "always", "every", or times the criterion does not state; an optional behaviour stays optional); who it helps is a need from the Feature's problem, never an outcome ("no longer", "never again"); floors stay out of release, launch, and brand copy and are stated exactly where a reader needs one; the copy never names the stage artefact as a heading or label; a launch has no scope, limits, or checks sections; a how-to leaves usage numbers out. Recorded drafts follow the same rules. Harness: a tell run keeps its final copy (`answer`, as persona and pack runs do since #285), revised through the shared `reviseOnce` and judged through `h.judge`, so the next round can read what was scored |

### Deviations recorded while fixing inspect's false alarms on clean diffs (WP4.8d, after v0.2.0)

Founder request (2026-10-17, relayed by the orchestrating thread): inspect averaged 4.19 over the review group's 36
case-variants in full run 38980397572 (7542b85) but scored 1, 1 on `inspect/clean-retry-backoff` in every lane; fix
the skill or the review plan so a clean diff gets a clean verdict without losing recall, add a clean-diff case, bring
every inspect case to at least 4.0, and decide for every failure who was wrong (the skill, the case, or the rubric),
within 40 USD of tier-2 spend. The review group scores by precision and recall against the planted defects, with no
judge, at its configured k = 2 (D-4.8b.5); it stays advisory.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-4.8d.1 | WP4.8b's reviewer instruction (`inspect.System`): "report each real defect once", with a precision paragraph | Run 39005146661 (dispatch, dev at 191cd26, the findings now recorded, D-4.8d.5) showed what the false positives were. Besides the control's real faults (D-4.8d.3): an unstated design choice (`sql-injection-search@peer`: "no-match search encodes as JSON null", P2, confidence 0.9), a guess about a fact the diff does not show ("name match is case-sensitive" under a case-sensitive collation, P1, 0.7), and one defect anchored on two lines (`nil-map-store`: the constructor at 13 and the panicking write at 20), which peer mode posted twice because `Dedupe` merges only within 3 lines. **Skill wrong** on these | `inspect.defectRule`, used by `System` and by the app review request (`apps.go`): a real defect is shown from the diff and the context, with an input or sequence that triggers it, the wrong result, and the rule it breaks (a criterion, the story, the code's documented contract, or no crash, security hole, lost or leaked data or resources, or race); behaviour that the story, criteria, and code all leave open is a design choice; a problem resting on what the diff does not show is a question for the summary; a change that does what it says has no findings. A risk in the review plan is a question to answer, not a finding. `quote` is the line the fix changes, the cause rather than the symptom, with the other line named in the `detail`, which now carries the trigger, what goes wrong, and the rule broken. No floor, filter, confidence threshold, or scoring rule changed; the 0.1 prompt (the eval's `legacy` arm, the "before") is unchanged |
| D-4.8d.2 | `nil-map-store`: the planted defect sits at line 13 (`return &Store{}`) | **Case wrong**: in run 39005146661, four of six runs posted a finding on line 20 (`s.values[k] = v`), the write that panics, each naming the nil map that `NewStore` leaves; three were scored as a false positive plus a missed defect (legacy 1, 1; general 5, 1) and one as a duplicate (peer 4, next to a finding on line 13). Run 38980397572's three misses (one in each lane) have the same counts; their lines were not recorded. The defect's own title says "so Set panics", and line 20 is one of its two fix sites (initialising lazily under the lock) | The defect's lines are `[13, 20]`, constructor to write. One finding still matches it once; a second finding on it is still a duplicate (false positive) |
| D-4.8d.3 | `clean-retry-backoff` is a clean control (no defect) | **Case wrong, skill right**: in run 39005146661 every lane posted the same two P1 findings (general and peer at confidence 0.98–1.0), and both hold; runs 38690306973 (D-4.8b.6) and 38980397572 posted two (once three) in every lane, unrecorded. `wait := base` is never capped, so `Retry(ctx, 3, time.Minute, f)` waits a minute against the story's 30 s cap; and f is called without a check of ctx, so an already-cancelled request still makes a call (and a timer and a cancellation ready together let `select` choose another), against "stop when the request is cancelled". The legacy arm also noted that the tests used 1 ms rather than criterion 1's 10 ms and cancelled inside f rather than during the first wait | The control does what its story says: the first wait is `min(base, maxWait)`, a base ≤ 0 is refused, ctx is checked before every call and after the last, the doc comment states that contract, and the tests match both criteria (10 ms; cancelled 10 ms into a 1 s wait), plus the last error and bad arguments. The code was compiled and passed `go test -race -count=3` in a scratch module before being stored as `.txt` |
| D-4.8d.4 | Twelve review cases, three clean controls (D-4.8b.5) | One clean control that every reviewer failed hid behind a group mean of 4.19 | New `inspect/clean-snapshot-locked`: `race-snapshot`'s story, criteria, and base, with the snapshot copied under the lock, a handler test for criterion 1 and a 100-goroutine test for criterion 2 (passes `go test -race`). It is the clean twin of a defect case, so a reviewer that flags correct locking, or treats the plan's concurrency risk as a finding, fails it. Thirteen cases, four clean controls |
| D-4.8d.5 | D-4.8b.5: a review run's reason gives counts ("2 posted: 0 true, 2 false") | The counts in run 38980397572 could not say what was flagged | PR #282 (191cd26): each review run keeps its posted findings, marked `true`/`false` against the planted defects, and its dropped findings with why, clipped and redacted |
| D-4.8d.6 | The filtered tier-2 run before the full group run (founder's budget rule) | Run 39007777502 (dispatch, dev at d0200fc, cases `clean-retry-backoff`, `clean-snapshot-locked`, `nil-map-store`, `sql-injection-search`, k = 2, 4.81 USD). On `clean-retry-backoff` every completed run posted one P1, and it holds: D-4.8d.3's rewritten doc comment promised ctx's error "also when ctx ends during the last call", but a last call that succeeds after cancellation returns nil. **Case wrong again** (the author's doc comment, not the code). The legacy arm's false positives on `clean-snapshot-locked` ("show the route is registered", "supply executed race evidence") and `sql-injection-search` ("no supplied execution evidence", on the test file) are what the 0.1 prompt had no rule against: v2's test-file rule and D-4.8d.1 cover them. No existing verb produces a data or AI design before planning, and `plan` has one Accountable seat (the Code Architect) | **Thirteen seats and 33 verbs.** The design is Decision #283 (option 1 of three). `verbs.Seats` gains `data-ai-architect` after the Code Architect; the twin's name, Mnemosyne, is proposed for the founder's veto (as D-5.1e). `seats/data-ai-architect/persona.toml` has the fields and checks of the other twelve and five sourced practices (data design: Data sketch, Data design, Data architecture record; data contracts: Source contract, Data contract, Data product specification; measurement: Measurement note, Measurement review, Measurement audit record; evidence-grounded AI: Evidence sketch, Evidence design, Evidence dossier; ML baselines: Baseline check, Model card, Model risk review); its hand-written charter is new, and every charter's first line, `seats/README.md`, the AGENTS.md seat sentence (`internal/seats/seats.go`), GLOSSARY.md, and the guide's seat and verb counts say thirteen and 33. Layers: L05 moves to the new seat (model behaviour, retrieval, and evaluation are its craft; the AI Native Engineer still builds it and stays Accountable for `evolve`, now Consulting the new seat); L08 moves to it (the analytics and telemetry pipelines; the Revenue Architect keeps what the numbers mean: `experiment`, `readout`, and the standard `measure` runs against, and owns no layer); L07 stays with the AI Native Engineer (secrets, identity, the actions log, and the ledger are running state under the SRE hat; the new seat's interest there, schemas, retention, and deletion, is served by being Consulted on `plan`). One owner per layer holds (`verbs.LintRACI`). `measure`'s Accountable seat becomes the new seat with `Owns` the Revenue Architect, so its skill block reads "whose voice, whose standard" as `verify`'s does, and its body gains how to read a Result with a person. New verb `chart` (WP5.1g: stage Break it down, lane spec, layers L05 L07 L08, gate halt, CLI, skill, and MCP read with `--create` pinned off, identity bot, mode host), appended after `doctor` in `wantVerbs`; `internal/chart` holds the parts of each kind and their checks, `internal/cli/chart.go` the grounding, check, and post. RACI: `chart` Consulted Code Architect and Revenue Architect, Informed AI Native Engineer and Quality Architect; the new seat is Consulted on `plan`, `experiment`, and `evolve`. `decide.OwningSeat` routes data pipelines, warehouses, analytics, dashboards, retrieval, embeddings, vector indexes, knowledge graphs, machine learning, LLMs, and eval sets to it, ahead of the Revenue Architect's metrics and DevOps's pipelines (`TestOwningSeat`, including the three decide case questions).
