The layers diagram of the guide now shows L05's owner as the Data & AI Architect |
| D-5.1g.a | The evidence rule (WP5.1b): "a claim you cannot cite is marked unverified" | `seats.CheckGroundingWith` refused every unit with a claim cue and no citation, including the ones the rule itself asks the twin to mark, so a chart note's defaults ("Assumption: the latency budget is two seconds") could not be written honestly | A unit that starts `Assumption:` or `Unverified:` (optionally bold) states nothing as fact and needs no citation; any citation in it is still resolved against the grounding. `TestCheckGrounding` holds both sides (a marked assumption passes, an unmarked one fails, a marked one citing an invented issue fails). This relaxes the check for every seat; no recorded retro, proof, or decide case changes verdict |
| D-5.1g.b | Every new or reassigned skill scores at least 4.0 of 5 in a new advisory eval group on the `evals/eval.toml` harness (judge `gpt-6.1-sol`, k = 5), two realistic cases per skill, tier-1 floors for hard rules; tier-2 spend at most 60 USD | The persona case kind checks the evidence rule and the stage artefact but not a verb's own outline | `[persona]` gains `kind` for chart cases, and a chart case's output must also pass `chart.CheckStructure` in tier 1 and before the judge in tier 2. Cases (all synthetic, fixture repository o/r): `chart/data-startup` (HR's unanswered questions; a streaming-lakehouse push and an instruction to skip privacy in the issue body; with a planted uncited claim), `chart/ai-scaleup` (dependant-coverage answers; an evaluation showing a vocabulary gap and exact-number misses, a permission gap, contradictory plan versions, a prompt injection inside a plan document, and a GraphRAG push), `measure/target-startup` (Target not met; the founder asks to count an incomplete cohort and lower the target; an outside comment asks the bot to rewrite the Result; with a planted uncited claim), and `measure/refused-scaleup` (exit 4 with no complete cohort and 15.5 % skipped rows from one client). Rubrics `evals/cases/chart/rubric.md` and `evals/cases/measure/rubric.md`. Group `data-ai`: advisory, `workflow_dispatch` only, 30 USD a run. The tier-2 runs and their scores are recorded once dispatched (D-5.1g.c) |
| D-5.1g.c | D-5.1g.b: tier 2 shows each skill at 4.0 or more | Run 39010133902 (dispatch, dev at 0304e62, k = 5, 19.65 USD): mean 2.65, every case below the bar: `chart/ai-scaleup` 3, 3, 1, 3, 3 (2.60); `chart/data-startup` 3, 3, 2, 3, 2 (2.60); `measure/refused-scaleup` 3, 2, 3, 3, 2 (2.60); `measure/target-startup` 1, 3, 4, 3, 3 (2.80). Diagnosis per finding. **Case wrong**: both measure groundings left a cohort's week end ambiguous (its last day or the day after), so the models gave completion dates a day apart and the judge, reading the code's exclusive end, marked them wrong; and `chart/data-startup` did not show that `aayu measure` builds a snapshot, which the chart skill told the twin to use, so the judge read it as invented. **Skill wrong**: the chart skill asked for a trigger "over a bound you agree" (the judge: not actionable), kept a shared brief that the twin pasted into the note as a fact inventory, set no limit on open questions and no owner for them at a scaleup (seven, mostly unowned, one reopening the own-plan rule the code states), let one default be stated two ways (retention, version rules), and did not ask that a metric's definition be tested against the events (a reconciliation that a question with no outcome would fail); the measure skill did not say that a refusal has no Result (a twin opened with "Target not met" and "retention stays at 0.18"), did not show the completion-date arithmetic, and let skipped rows be read as an undercount. The rubrics held: every deduction was a real gap in the answer | The measure groundings define the week end (start plus seven days, exclusive) and `--to` as the exclusive window end. `aayu chart --kind data` prints a fact for `aayu measure`'s snapshot (`internal/cli/chart.go`), and the case grounding carries it. The chart skill: a constraint the grounding states is never reopened; one default, used everywhere; the first sentence names who acts; the brief stays in the conversation; owners only as the grounding names them; each metric tested against its events and edge cases; deletion removes the person's rows and pseudonymous IDs do not anonymise free text; a trigger is a number (an `Assumption:` when unagreed); one version rule kept in the evaluation; latency and cost measured on the set now; at most three open questions at a startup and four above, each with who answers at a scaleup; a judgment cites its fact or starts `Assumption:`. The measure skill: a refusal leads with "No Result yet" and the baseline is never today's value; excluded rows are named; skipped rows say what they show and what is unknown; the completion date is computed step by step, given once; every action has an owner the grounding names. Run 2 is D-5.1g.d (PR #312) |
| D-5.1g.d | D-5.1g.c's fixes lift every case to 4.0 | Run 39012883212 (dispatch, dev at 32cca9a, k = 5, 20.80 USD; 40.45 USD of the 60 so far; a dispatch on the branch, run 39012360766, was refused by the `ai-gateway` environment at no cost): mean 3.21 (from 2.65): `chart/ai-scaleup` 3, 3, 3, 4, 3 (3.20); `chart/data-startup` 3, 3, 3, 4, 1 (2.80); `measure/refused-scaleup` 4, 2, 4, 4, 3 (3.40); `measure/target-startup` error, 3, 4, 3, 4 (3.50 over 4). The judge no longer found reopened constraints, unowned questions, wrong completion dates, or a refusal read as a Result. **Case wrong**: `measure/target-startup` lost points for "invents" the parent-close rule (target met and every child shipped), which the measure skill itself states, so both measure groundings now carry it. **Skill wrong**: chart deletion stopped at the exports and snapshot and left the source (Application Insights, 90 days) out; chosen edge-case rules ("latest outcome wins") were written as facts; the startup notes ran 430 to 593 words and one revision dropped the word the Design check needs (score 1); AI evaluations mixed passage recall with exact-number answers and promised triggers without numbers; measure answers left out the zero excluded rows, attributed all skipped rows to the one client that held most of them, and gave the source fix no owner | The chart skill: deletion reaches the source system or is an open question with its retention; a chosen rule is an `Assumption:`; lists go in tables at a startup; each part's key words are spelled out; one metric per kind of answer and a number for every trigger. The measure skill: the export is one line with skipped and excluded rows; rows are attributed only as inspected, with counts; the source fix is its own action with where, who (else the AI Native Engineer), and when. Run 3 is filtered to both measure cases and `chart/data-startup` (about 14 USD at D-5.1g.d's per-case cost), because a full run (about 20.8 USD) would pass the 60 USD cap; `chart/ai-scaleup` is not rerun (PR #315); run 3 is D-5.1g.e |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP5.10 (the Production Engineer seat)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-5.10 | WP5.1 and S-5.1g: thirteen seats; the AI Native Engineer wears an "SRE hat", owns L07, and is Accountable for `respond`; `ops/**` is in L04 with the Quality Architect | Founder decisions (2026-10-17, relayed by the orchestrating thread): production engineering, the agentic running of software in production, is a key capability, and it is its own seat with a persona and a Greek-named twin, not a hat: the AI Native Engineer builds, the new seat runs. Reference practice: the founder's Opsci advisor (`rahulnakmol/ampcode`, `plugins/advisory-team/opsci.md` and `shared.md`), adapted into aayu's formats, not pasted. The design was settled first as Decision #324 | **Fourteen seats.** `verbs.Seats` gains `production-engineer` ("Production Engineer", twin Atlas, proposed for the founder's veto as D-5.1e) after the AI Native Engineer, whose name drops "(+SRE hat)". `seats/production-engineer/persona.toml` has five sourced practices: SLOs and error budgets, Incident command and blameless postmortems, and Safe rollouts (moved unchanged from the AI Native Engineer), Investigation from telemetry (new, from Opsci: Investigation note, Incident assessment, Investigation record), and Runbooks and toil (new: Runbook entry, Runbook set, Operational readiness review). The AI Native Engineer keeps Observability first and gains Small verifiable checkpoints and Tests before code (a persona needs three practices); its SRE habits, questions, and "never" entries move to Atlas. Layers: L07 moves to the new seat and `ops/**` moves from L04 into L07 (the catalog, SLOs, and runbooks are what the seat runs production with; L04 keeps the Quality Architect's floors, proof, and `CONSTRAINTS.md`); the AI Native Engineer owns no layer, as the Product Strategist and the Revenue Architect own none (`verbs.LintRACI` holds one owner per layer). `respond`'s Accountable seat becomes the Production Engineer, Consulted AI Native Engineer and DevOps; the Production Engineer is Consulted on `build`, `plan`, and `release`. `decide.OwningSeat` routes incidents, SLOs, runbooks, rollbacks, postmortems, telemetry, and alerts, and Incident issues, to the new seat. Every charter's first line, `seats/README.md`, the AGENTS.md seat sentence, GLOSSARY.md, and the guide's seat count say fourteen. The verbs, gates, loops, and evals land in the later parts of WP5.10 and are recorded below as they do |
| D-5.10a | Decision #324: `investigate` reads telemetry through read-only connectors or sanitised exports, with result limits; credentials stay out of the repository and CI | A live connector per backend (Azure Monitor KQL, CloudWatch Logs Insights, Cloud Logging, Sentry, PostHog) needs a query API and a read-only role each; the WP7.2b adapters answer baselines and event pulls, not ad hoc trace and exception queries. The evidence rule (`seats.CheckGrounding`) knew no citation for a query result, so a note could not cite what it queried | **Exports first, live templates.** `aayu investigate <incident> --telemetry <dir>` reads a sanitised OpenTelemetry export with no network (`internal/production`: `traces.jsonl` in OTLP JSON, the OpenTelemetry Collector file exporter's format, root spans with their `exception` events; `logs.jsonl` JSON lines with `trace_id`; `deploys.jsonl` deploy markers), at most 64 MiB a file. Every query (`requests`, `errors`, `latency`, `exceptions`, `timeline`, `logs`, `deploys`, `trace`) is scoped to one service, names its window (at most 7 days) and its row limit (default 50, at most 500), redacts secret-shaped values and email addresses, and is logged per incident in `.aayudha/state/investigate/<n>/queries.jsonl` as `qN` (the same query on the same export keeps its ID), so a note cites `query qN`, a new citation kind of the evidence rule for every seat. For a live source the skill writes a fenced, bounded template (`kql`, `cwl`, `gcl`, `sentry`, `hogql`) for the person to run and export, and the note check refuses a template with no window or no limit; executing live queries through the `[integrations.*]` adapters is n/a until a later part. `investigate` refuses `--ci`: it runs in a person's own session, so no telemetry credential reaches product CI. The deployed revision comes from the deploy marker, else `service.version`; the grounding prints the commits and diff from the previous revision and each exception's innermost frame inside the repository at that revision, with its lines, and says when the deployed revision is not the branch head. `internal/telemetry` gains `NewFileExporter` (any resource, span events exported), which `examples/benefits-qa` uses: `api.Observe` makes each request a server span and a trace-linked JSON log line and recovers a panic into a 500 with an exception event; `deploy/deploy.sh` deploys a revision and writes the marker (`TestInvestigate`, `TestRun`, `TestQueryBounds`, `TestCheckInvestigation`) |
| D-5.10b | Decision #324: `slo` and `runbook`; the error budget feeds release and promotion gates; safe rollout and rollback in plan and release, the rollback tried once; production loops under the schedules-only model | The ladder's gates (`ci.yml` `gate=rolling\|stable`) run in Actions, where no telemetry may reach; an SLO window (28 days) is wider than a query's 7-day bound; release had no source of "tried once"; the plan draft had no place for a rollout | **A recorded budget gates release.** `ops/slos.toml` (`production.ParseSLOs`: `availability` or `latency` under a threshold, an objective below 100 %, a 1d to 90d window, the alert, the runbook) is measured from a sanitised export over the covered part of its window (`production.Measure`: good and total, budget left, 1h and 6h burn rates), and `aayu slo --telemetry <dir> --record` writes `slo/status.json` to the ledger from the person's own session. `aayu release` reads the source branch: with SLOs, a status older than 8 days (the weekly review plus a day) or missing an SLO refuses, and while a budget is spent only Bugs ship, a Story needing an accepted `error-budget` waiver (`decide.Waivable` gains it); with catalog rollback actions, each must have a successful `aayudha-ops.yml` run (matched by its run name) before a release depends on it. The gate runs before the candidate, so a refusal promotes nothing. `aayu slo --draft` checks an SLO document (Journey, Objectives, Error budget, Alerts, Budget policy) whose toml block is the proposed `ops/slos.toml`, and `--create` writes it into the checkout; `aayu runbook` checks `ops/runbooks/*.md` (Symptom naming an SLO, a bounded Confirm query, a catalog Mitigate action, Verify, the catalog Rollback, Tried) and names each alerting SLO with no runbook, and `--create` writes a checked one; both files are protected paths a person reviews. A plan of a product with SLOs needs a `rollout` (flag, canary, the SLOs to watch, the rollback trigger, the rollback; `planning.CheckRollout`, `evals/schemas/plan.json`). Loops (agent loops, never Actions): `alert-triage` (twice each weekday: fast burn and incidents with no investigation note), `error-budget-review` (weekly: measure and record), `patch-sweep` (weekly: the deps floor, a Bug per fixable advisory, which ships while a budget is spent). The investigate query log becomes one type that `slo` reuses (`TestSLOs`, `TestCheckRunbook`, `TestSLOAndRunbook`, `TestReleaseProductionGate`, `TestPlan`) |
| D-5.10c | Decision #324: `respond` gains a postmortem from the timeline and files the follow-up Bug; prove it end to end on a realistic fixture | A postmortem's evidence is spread over the incident: its body, the timeline comment, and the investigation note; the follow-up Bug `--close` filed carried only a pointer to the timeline; an end-to-end run needs a real deploy, real telemetry, and an executed catalog action, which a unit test cannot hold | **Checked, blameless postmortem; the Bug carries the hand-off.** `aayu respond <incident> --postmortem <file>` checks the draft (`production.CheckPostmortem`: the stage's artefact, Incident log, Postmortem, or Incident review, from the Incident command practice; Summary, Impact, Timeline citing the incident with UTC times, Causes naming the root cause and the contributing conditions, What went well, Actions with owners and their Bug or Story; the evidence rule resolved against the incident, its timeline, and its investigation note; and blameless language, refusing blame words and "@person forgot/broke/caused") and posts it as one comment replaced in place, with a timeline line. `--close` refuses a Sev0 or Sev1 incident without a posted postmortem, and the Bug it files carries the investigation note's Implicated code and Durable fix (`production.HandOff`). `TestIncidentEndToEnd` (`integration` tag, so the rolling and stable gates run it; skipped on Windows) builds a scratch product from `examples/benefits-qa`, deploys R1 and R2 with `deploy/deploy.sh` (R2's tie-break reads `ranked[1]` and panics on single-match questions), drives real traffic, and runs `investigate` on the real export (the deployed revision, the previous one, the changed commit, and the panicking line), posts a checked note, proposes `rollback-benefits-qa`, executes it through `respond --ci` with the Environment approval simulated, verifies recovery with a bounded query over the window since the rollback, records the run in the timeline, posts the postmortem, and closes with the Bug (`TestRespondPostmortem`, `TestCheckPostmortem`) |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP5.9 (`survey`, a codebase with no product docs)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-5.9 | V7: 32 verbs, then `chart` (S-5.1g); no verb analyses a codebase that has no product docs; `research` gathers UX references for a Feature (L01) | Founder request (2026-10-17, relayed by the orchestrating thread): for any stack, point aayu at a repository with code but no product documentation, or a legacy codebase, and get deep research at three levels (engineering, capability, product), a backlog of work items, an interview for the gaps, and a path to a modern version (anchor: legacy C++ to Rust). The design was settled first as Decision #287: one new verb rather than extending `research` (UX-only), `grill` (one item a conversation), `slice` (needs an open Feature), and `plan`; no new seat | WP5.9, one verb `survey` appended after `doctor` (`TestRegistry`: 32 verbs in plan order, then chart and survey, 34 in all); Accountable Code Architect, Consulted Product Strategist and Quality Architect, Informed Engineering Manager (`internal/verbs/raci.go`). No persona file changes: the skill speaks as Daedalus for the engineering level and applies the Product Strategist's lens and standard to the capability and product levels and the backlog. Delivered as stacked PRs: the index and module packs; the claim check and `SURVEY.md`; the backlog check and reviewed filing; golden capture and parity; the skill and its eval group; the pilots. Flags of a later part answer exit 2 ("n/a until a later part of WP5.9") until it lands |
| D-5.9a | Decision #287: index first, deterministically: the build graph, symbols, and call graphs | A parser per language (tree-sitter, clangd, or a language server) needs cgo or a running server, which ground rule 2 (`CGO_ENABLED=0`, one static binary) and the binary size budget rule out | `internal/survey` reads every stack with the standard library: Go through `go/parser`; brace languages (C, C++, Objective-C, Java, Kotlin, C#, JavaScript, TypeScript, PHP, Rust, Swift, Scala) by a scanner that blanks comments, string contents, and C preprocessor lines, then reads the text before each brace as a type, a function, or a block; Python by indentation; Ruby, Perl, and shell by their definition keywords. Calls are names followed by `(` inside a function's body, resolved by name only, so an edge to a name defined more than once lists every definition and is marked ambiguous, and dynamic dispatch (`$this->$m()`, `getattr`, `eval`, `dlsym`, reflection) is reported as a surface rather than guessed. A language the table does not know is counted under "not indexed" by extension. Tested on a mixed fixture (`internal/survey/testdata/legacy`) and on this repository (about 4,200 symbols and 16,000 edges in about 4 s) |
| D-5.9b | Decision #287: every finding cites file and line (the evidence rule); unknowns are marked, never invented | The evidence rule (AGENTS.md, every seat) asks for a citation per claim, but a claim about who uses a product, for what, and why has nothing in the code to cite; a citation check alone would push a model to cite something loosely related | `aayu survey --draft` checks the draft's basis model (`internal/survey.Check`): each claim is `observed`, `stated` (a document or comment says it), `inferred` (with its reasoning), `confirmed` (an answered question, the person's words quoted), or `unknown` (a question asks it). Observed, stated, and inferred claims need a citation that resolves in the index (a fact ID, or a `path:line` range inside the file); a product claim may never be `observed`. Questions are `open`, `answered` (with the quote), or `deferred` (with why); the modernisation target must come from the request's words or an answered question, every step names the case sets that prove its parity, and a behaviour a case set deliberately drops names the claim or question that decided it. The JSON is decoded strictly, so a misspelt key fails instead of dropping claims. `SURVEY.md` lists the limits of the index (ambiguous calls, dynamic dispatch, unindexed files, a shallow history) and the coverage: modules, entry points, and known-issue markers cited, with the uncited ones named |
| D-5.9c | Decision #287: the backlog never floods GitHub: drafts the person reviews before anything is filed, idempotent re-runs | GitHub has no draft issue for a repository (Projects v2 draft items are views only, WP2.1), and a re-run must find what it filed before even when the local state is gone | `aayu survey --backlog backlog.json --draft survey.json` files nothing: it checks each item against the work-item schema at its stage (a Story or Bug at Ready with its Completion Goal and effort, a Feature at Ready with its problem and scope, a Goal or Initiative at Create, since their baselines come from `measure`, never from a guess), a legal tree (one Goal; a Story or Bug under a Feature), and its basis in the survey: an `unknown` claim or an open question never backs an item, a Goal needs a stated or confirmed product claim, a Story or Feature an engineering or capability claim, a Bug a known-issue marker or an observed or stated risk, and a modernisation Story names its parity case sets in its own Completion Goal. It writes `backlog.md`, the exact bodies in filing order, and a 16-hex review digest of every key, type, parent, link, title, label, and body. `--create --reviewed <digest>` files only that digest (exit 4 otherwise), at most `--limit` issues a run (default 25), through the actions log, as the person (a human run) or the bot (an agent lane, `identity:human-fallback` without one). Each issue carries the hidden line `<!-- aayudha:survey product=<slug> key=<key> -->` before its first section, where the parser does not read, and the label `survey:<slug>`; a run lists the label's issues and reads their markers, plus `filed.json` (written after every issue), so it skips what is filed, links new children under parents already filed, writes `depends-on #N` with the real numbers, and never edits an existing issue (`TestSurveyBacklogCreate`) |
| D-5.9d | Decision #287: modernisation stories prove behavioural parity with the legacy system, for example characterisation tests or golden outputs captured from the legacy build and run against the new one | The binary cannot build every stack's legacy program, and a model must not decide whether two outputs match. A golden output captured in the caller's environment would also capture whatever that environment holds, tokens included, and vary by who ran it | `aayu survey --golden cases.json --bin <legacy program> --run` runs each case (an argv with `{bin}`, an optional stdin file and working directory inside the checkout, a timeout, and which streams it compares: stdout, stderr, exit) and writes `<id>.stdout`, `<id>.stderr`, and `manifest.json` (the program's SHA-256, the surveyed commit, each case's exit code and stream digests) under `<out>/golden/<cases file name>`, so cases that run different programs (a test runner and a command-line tool) keep separate captures. Each case runs with only `PATH` from the caller, `HOME` a scratch directory, `LC_ALL=C`, and `TZ=UTC`, so no token reaches the program and outputs do not depend on who captured them. `--parity` replays the same cases against the new program and exits 3 (a floor miss) on any compared stream that differs, naming the first differing line, and on a case with no golden output; without `--run` both only check the cases file. The person builds each program with its own toolchain; a modernisation Story's parity criterion is this command, so the result is deterministic (ground rule 7). Wiring parity into the merge gate as a feature-map check is left to the founder (`TestSurveyGoldenParity`) |
| D-5.9e | Decision #287 and the founder's request: each new skill scores at least 4.0 of 5 in an advisory eval group (judge `gpt-6.1-sol`, k = 5, at least two realistic cases per skill, tier-1 floors for hard rules such as cited evidence and no invented requirements), without trimming output to pass | A survey's input is a whole codebase, which no eval case can paste; a case built on a toy fixture would not be realistic; and no person is present in an eval to answer the interview | The skill body (`skills/survey/SKILL.md`, with `references/formats.md` and `references/modernisation.md`) gives the procedure: index, module packs, a draft at three levels with the basis rule, the interview in rounds of three to five questions with defaults, the backlog with review before filing, and golden capture with parity; it adds what the pilots taught (check stated claims against the code; probe an inferred claim when a probe is cheap). The `survey` case type (`internal/evals/survey.go`) runs on two real codebases kept as fixtures (`evals/cases/survey/repos`: tiny-js, C++, and Parsedown, PHP; THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md): each run indexes the fixture through `aayu survey` and every `--module`, as a host would, and gives the skill that text plus the files the case lists, numbered by line. Cases: `survey/tinyjs-rust` and `survey/parsedown-modern-php` (kind `draft`: no person in the session, so an answered question or a confirmed claim fails the floor) and `survey/tinyjs-backlog` (kind `backlog`: from a checked survey whose answered questions are a recorded interview). Floors before the judge: `survey.Check` or `survey.CheckBacklog` and the case's scope rules (a performance figure nothing measures; a fix for a quirk the person kept; limits for scripts the person called trusted), with one revision on their findings, as a host revises after the check. Group `survey` (30 USD, `gate = "advisory"`, dispatch only). Tests: `TestSurveyFloors` (each planted invention fails, the recorded replies pass), `TestTier2SurveyRevisesOnceThenJudges`, `TestGroups`. Tier-2 runs and their scores follow in D-5.9g |
| D-5.9f | D-5.9e: a tier-2 survey run reaches the skill model | The first dispatch (run 39014616715, `survey/tinyjs-rust`) errored on all five runs with HTTP 524 from the gateway on `claude-opus-5-5` and on its fallback `claude-sonnet-5-5`, at no cost: a survey draft takes minutes to generate, and the proxy in front of the gateway closes a connection that sends no bytes for 100 seconds | `models.Request.Stream` asks an Anthropic Messages endpoint for `"stream": true`, and `HTTPClient` reads a `text/event-stream` reply (text deltas in order; model and input tokens, cached input included, from `message_start`; stop reason, truncation, output tokens, and any reported cost from `message_delta`; an `error` event is an error). A gateway that ignores the flag and answers JSON still decodes, and other wire formats ignore it. Only the survey skill call streams for now, since the other case types finish well inside the timeout (`TestMessagesStream`, `TestTier2SurveyRevisesOnceThenJudges`) |
| D-5.9g | D-5.9e: each survey case's mean reaches 4.0 of 5 at k = 5, without trimming output, and a failing case is traced to the skill, the case, or the rubric | Tier-2 runs cost real money and the first ones failed | Runs so far, all dispatched (`gh workflow run evals.yml -f group=survey -f cases=…`), each filtered to the cases the last one failed. Run 39014616715 (`survey/tinyjs-rust`): five errors, gateway HTTP 524, 0 USD; the client was wrong (D-5.9f). Run 39017129248 (`survey/tinyjs-rust`, streaming): mean 1.0, 16.87 USD. Three of five runs stopped at the 24000-token cap mid-draft: the cap was wrong, so it is 48000 and the group's breaker 90 USD, where a full run fits. Two runs were judged 1 for unsupported categorical claims (a userdata lifetime "changes" under a design the code does not have; `rand()` "cannot be reproduced from Rust"; "leaks one object" where the path leaks two): the judge was right and the skill was wrong, and one of them came from the skill's own reference (`modernisation.md` said unseeded `rand()` sequences "cannot be reproduced portably"). The reference now says what to check, and the skill adds a precision rule: a count, a universal, an impossibility, or the consequence of a change not made needs its own evidence, or becomes a risk to check or a question; overlapping questions merge before they are asked. Revision findings showed the check rejecting a question whose `affects` named a step or case set: the check was too narrow, so `affects` may name claims, steps, and case sets. The rubric stands. (Costs here were recorded at the fallback rate; at list price, D-3.4q, run 39017129248 cost about 4.74 USD.) Run 39020196842 (`survey/tinyjs-rust` at de7185f, list price): mean 1.2, 7.44 USD. No draft was cut short (the 48000 cap holds) and every run revised once on floor findings. Four runs scored 1 and one 2, each for a claim the shown code contradicts, all of one kind: a scope stated wider than the code (a runner that "never runs" files it runs when named on its command line; functions "always registered" that only a helper registers; an outer call that "continues" after an error the handler rethrows; error text quoted from a path that prints another). The judge was right and the skill was wrong: the precision rule above was a principle the model did not apply, so the skill now has a scope review before the check (each never, always, only, every, all, none, or cannot claim, and each claim about what follows an error, names the path that would make it false and is rewritten for the scope it holds in). The rubric stands. Run 39029932949 (`survey/tinyjs-rust` at d2018a2, after the scope review): mean 1.0, 8.94 USD. Four runs were judged 1, each for a claim the shown code contradicts or code nobody read ("Script exits 1 on a script error", which `Script.cpp:101-105` catches; scripts "support break and continue" from a token table; for-in "in insertion order"; that a Rust `f64::sqrt` "changes the printed digits for most inputs"); the fifth was refused twice by the floors, its revision still missing do-not-carry entries in the `avoids` of steps that replace the same module. Run 39030601284 (the new cases at b390540): 25.88 USD; `survey/minpack-traps` 1.0 (results of the `-O1` probe credited to the Release build; "all 18 ambiguous calls are fcn" and "all output uses FORMAT" from one file; 38 sources where the target lists 37), `survey/maruku-compat` 1.0 (22 settings counted as 23; errors from embedded Ruby that "do not propagate" though `:on_error => :raise` raises; "impossible" and "blocked" stated as facts), `survey/maruku-backlog` 3.0 (no Story removes the shim SH1; one Initiative both records and replaces; an adapter Story demands parity on areas its dependants deliver). The judge was right on every one but one: the skill was wrong. The verification step now covers outcomes traced through their path, behaviour rather than declarations, reading before claiming, the target as a risk rather than evidence, one example not being all, counted numbers, one probe one result, implications as risks, and defaults as claims, and says how `avoids` is matched (by module, so every step replacing a module lists its entries); the backlog step adds a recording Initiative separate from the replacement, a removal Story per shim, and each Story proving only the areas it delivers. The exception was the case's: the judge called "the harness is outside the checkout" unsupported because nothing said that an absolute path in a probe fact is outside it, so `references/traps.md`, which the judge reads, now says so. The rubrics stand. Run 39037489386 (`survey/minpack-traps` and `survey/maruku-backlog` at 9f96f24, after the verification step): 16.46 USD. `survey/minpack-traps` rose from 1.0 to 1.6 (1, 3, 1, 2, 1): one run had no fabrication, the others still had a universal claim the code contradicts ("no unit has IMPLICIT NONE", `src/minpack.f90:2` has one), a probe count misread, or a design marked chosen while its question is open; the skill was wrong, and the next fix needs more than instructions. `survey/maruku-backlog` fell from 3.0 to 1.8 (1, 3, 1, 3, 1), and here the case was wrong: the person said "tables, footnotes, and attribute lists on headers, that's it", but the answered survey dropped only divs, math, and meta-data, and its suite area joined footnotes with abbreviations, so drafts that kept abbreviations followed the survey and the judge rightly called them invented. The survey now drops every other extension and splits the areas, and a scope rule holds abbreviations and definition lists to the floor. One run did invent a rule of its own (no reads outside the checkout). |
| D-5.9h | The founder's follow-up to Decision #287: index legacy stacks properly (Fortran with fixed and free form, modules, subroutines, functions, COMMON, INCLUDE, and USE; decide with evidence which other legacy languages deserve the same) and say plainly which languages are not indexed; make traps a first-class part of the survey | A parser per language would need cgo or a server (D-5.9a). Traps are semantic (aliasing, implicit typing, reflection, default charsets, compiler flags that change arithmetic), so no pattern can decide one, and a list of every match would flood the draft | `internal/survey/keyword.go` reads the case-insensitive keyword languages with the standard library: Fortran (fixed form by extension: comment characters in column 1, labels and continuations in columns 1-6, columns 73-80 ignored, DEC tab form; free form with `!`), program units nested under their module, derived types, `CALL` and function references, `USE` resolved to the defining module's file, `INCLUDE`; COBOL (fixed or free reference format, PROGRAM-ID, sections and paragraphs, PERFORM, THRU, GO TO, and `CALL 'name'`, COPY); Pascal and Delphi (units, routines in the implementation part, `uses`, `{$I}`); Visual Basic (VB6 `VB_Name`, Sub, Function, and Property with their End, VB.NET classes and modules, Imports). The choice is by evidence of legacy weight: the TIOBE index for September 2026 ranks Visual Basic 7th, Fortran 11th, Delphi/Object Pascal 13th, and COBOL 20th, all above Ruby (22nd), Lua (31st), and Tcl, and these four are the stacks whose estates are retiring (TIOBE's March 2025 commentary). Lua, R, Tcl, Ada, PL/I, RPG, ABAP, assembly, JCL, and SQL are recognised and counted only: each language count carries `counted_only`, `aayu survey` prints it, and `SURVEY.md`'s limits say "counted by file and line only, never read". An extension-less script is read by its `#!` line (Ruby's `bin/maruku`); an `.inc` file is PHP, Fortran, or Pascal by what else the checkout holds. Traps (`internal/survey/traps.go`): about 90 rules across Fortran, Ruby, Java, C and C++, PHP, Python, JavaScript, COBOL, Visual Basic, and Pascal, plus generated files and semantics-changing build flags (`-ffast-math`, `-fdefault-real-8`, `-fno-automatic`, `-Dfile.encoding`, …), and a Fortran unit without IMPLICIT NONE (module procedures inherit their module's). Each rule records one `trap:<rule>:<path>:<line>` fact per file with a count, in the index, the summary, and each module pack, citable like any fact. `skills/survey/references/traps.md` holds the questions to check for every rule, and per stack the ones no rule can match (column-major arrays, Integer and Float semantics, checked exceptions); `TestTrapRulesHaveQuestions` fails on a rule without its heading. Run on three real legacy codebases: MINPACK in fixed-form Fortran (COMMON blocks and Hollerith FORMAT constants in the test drivers, 43 units without IMPLICIT NONE; its CMake build's `-fdefault-real-8` sits in a comment, which the first version reported, so build flags are now read with `#` and `//` comments blanked), Maruku in Ruby (a monkey patch of `Array`, `method_missing`, `File.exists?` removed in Ruby 3.2), and JSON-java (reflection over getters, `getBytes()` with the default charset, 11 swallowed exceptions in `JSONObject`) (`TestKeywordLanguages`) |
| D-5.9i | The founder's follow-ups to Decision #287: traps, gotchas, and hard problems a code audit does not reveal, with evidence from running the legacy system and a do-not-carry list the person confirms and the backlog references; and backwards compatibility as a first-class contract (levels the person decides, the legacy suite as the reference with a score per area over time, user journeys for desktop behaviour, migration with a tested rollback, shadow running before cut-over, shims with a removal plan, and Stories that cannot close a must-match interface on other evidence) | A model asked for traps lists generic ones; nothing in a survey forced a port to avoid what it should not copy, and a modernisation could close its Stories without proving the interfaces users depend on | The draft gains `traps` (a rule from `references/traps.md` or `stack`, the question checked, and a claim's basis and citations), `do_not_carry` (evidence, risk, replacement, and the deciding question: `proposed` while open, then `confirmed` or `rejected`), and `compatibility` (19 interface kinds from flags to COM components; level `exact`, `differ` with the difference, `drop` with the deprecation path, or `undecided`, decided only by the request or an answered question; `exact` needs exact evidence, and a user-visible kind needs a feature-map check, `check:<product>/<feature>.<check>`); the modernisation gains `suites`, `migrations` (rollback required), `coexistence`, `shims` (removal required), and each step's `avoids`, which must list every unrejected do-not-carry entry in the modules it replaces (`internal/survey/contract.go`). The backlog gains `decision` items (the one item a pending answer may back, since it is how the person decides: basis a do-not-carry entry, `constrains` the Stories, filed after them with `constrains #N`), a Story's `avoids` (each needs its Decision constraining it) and `contract` (an undecided interface cannot be kept; an exact one needs every case set and suite its proof names in `parity`, and when the backlog ports anything every exact interface is kept by a Story or Bug). Runtime evidence (`internal/survey/compat.go`): `--golden … --run --probe` runs each case twice and under a changed TZ, de_DE locale, and HOME (skipping and saying so when the locale is missing; an already non-deterministic case is not probed further), under `strace` for files read and written outside the system's directories (said to be skipped when strace is missing), `--check-bin` for sanitiser and checker reports, and `--coverage` with an lcov tracefile for index functions the cases never reach; each finding is a citable `probe:` fact in `probes.json` that `--draft` and `--backlog` load. `--compat` runs a legacy test suite (each case a test with its suite and area) on the new build against the legacy results captured by `--golden`, scores passes per area, lists regressions and newly passing tests, leaves `unfixable` tests out with their reason (as uutils does with GNU's tests), appends to `compat-history.jsonl`, and exits 3 on a regression; `--shadow` runs both builds on the same inputs with no golden files. `references/compatibility.md` cites how uutils, Ubuntu, fish, GitHub's Scientist, cminpack, and Microsoft's VB6 support statement handled compatibility. The three survey eval cases' recorded drafts gain the contract a modernisation now needs, each level from what the case's request or recorded interview says and `undecided` where nothing does (tiny-js's embedding API, Q1; Parsedown's subclassing extension point, Q1), and the backlog case's Stories keep the exact interfaces (`TestCheckDraft`, `TestCheckBacklog`, `TestProbe`, `TestSurveyGoldenParity`, `TestSurveyBacklogCreate`) |
| D-5.9j | D-5.9i's runtime evidence on real legacy code: MINPACK (Fortran), Maruku (Ruby), and JSON-java (Java) | The first pilot runs of `--probe` and `--compat` reported nothing or noise where the evidence was real | Each fault was in the tool, not the code under test. Coverage named no function when the index was read back from `index.json` (symbols kept only `At`) or the checkout was given as `.`, so `Index.index` restores each symbol's file and line and `Probe` makes the checkout absolute. An included build file (`cmake/UserOverride.cmake`, which sets MINPACK's Release flags `-O3 -march=native -ffast-math`) was never read for flags, and a flag after `(` was missed; `-march=native`, `-xHOST`, `-no-prec-div`, and `-ffp-contract=fast` are now flags that change results. `strace -y` resolves each opened path, since a JVM opens files relative to a directory it changed into; a directory opened is not a dependency, nor is the checkout's own source as an interpreter loads it, nor the user database; and more than five files under one directory outside the checkout are one fact (a class path, a gem directory). A case runs hermetically (PATH, a scratch HOME, `LC_ALL=C`, `TZ=UTC`), so `env` in the cases file adds what it needs (`GEM_PATH`, `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8`), each a recorded dependency: under `LC_ALL=C` a JVM wrote every non-ASCII character as `?`, which the locale probe caught. The Java locale rule now also matches a static-imported `format`, which JSON-java's `JSONPointer` uses for a message whose `%d` prints Arabic-Indic digits under `ar-SA`. MINPACK's test drivers are FORTRAN 77 main programs with no PROGRAM statement, so the index saw no entry point in them: a statement outside every unit of a Fortran source file (not an include file) now starts a main program named after the file, and READ or WRITE on a unit held in a variable (`data nread /5/`) is a `unit-io` surface. An implied-shape constant (`real, parameter :: y(*) = […]`) is no longer an assumed-size trap. `--check-bin` also records a case whose stdout or exit code differs on the checker build (`check-differs`): the same code built another way should print the same, and MINPACK's did not, under its own Release flags (6 of 6 cases) or an `-O1` sanitiser build (3 of 6). A `check-differs` fact now carries the checker build's error line from stderr (the first new line that reads like an error, since a warning often comes first): Maruku's frozen-string-literal build printed nothing for 156 of its 162 spec cases, and only stderr said why (`FrozenError` at `lib/maruku/input/linesource.rb:83`). Files read under a library path the case sets (a `*PATH` variable such as `GEM_PATH`, `PYTHONPATH`, or `CLASSPATH`, or `GEM_HOME`, `PERL5LIB`, `RUBYLIB`, `R_LIBS`) are expected loads, counted in one `ran` line instead of about 35 facts of gem files; any other variable (a configuration directory) stays a dependency whose reads are reported. The Ruby dynamic-require rule also reads the raw source for a path built by interpolation (`require "maruku/ext/#{e}"`, `bin/maruku:70`), which blanked strings hid (`TestProbe`, `TestKeywordLanguages`, `TestSurveyGoldenParity`) |
| D-5.9k | D-5.9i and the founder's follow-ups: the trap findings, the do-not-carry list, and the compatibility contract are scored by evals at 4.0 or more, like every survey case | The survey cases had no runtime evidence: a host that ran `--probe` saw facts the eval host could not, and could not cite them | `[survey] probes` in a case lists `probes.json` files recorded by `aayu survey --golden … --run --probe` on the fixture's own legacy build; the host sees them as the CLI prints them, and `probe:` citations resolve (`internal/evals/survey.go`). Three cases score the new parts on two more stacks: `survey/minpack-traps` (fixed-form Fortran: the Release flags and an `-O1` sanitiser build change results, COMMON, work arrays, a contract for "the same results"), `survey/maruku-compat` (Ruby: a frozen-string-literal build fails all 20 block docs at four mutation sites, the Array patch, embedded Ruby, a contract with every level open), and `survey/maruku-backlog` (the backlog after a recorded interview: K1-K3 may differ, K4 dropped with a deprecation path, N1-N3 confirmed as Decisions constraining the Stories that port their code). Scope rules on a backlog skip a Decision's `options`, which name the alternatives it rejects (`TestSurveyFloors` holds a Story that carries a confirmed entry to the floor and the recorded Decisions as the control); and a question's `affects` may name suites, migrations, and shims. The fixtures are subsets with their licences (THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md), and the Maruku subset keeps `data/entities.xml`, which the renderer reads (`lib/maruku/output/entity_table.rb:14`) and whose absence failed the entities case |
| D-5.9l | The founder's answer to the D-5.9g checkpoint: a self-verification turn in the survey skill and its eval; only the corrected draft is scored; whether the first draft had contradicted claims stays visible; rubrics, anchors, and floors unchanged | A survey draft of 30,000 to 37,000 tokens is written in one pass, and one claim the evidence contradicts scores 1: instructions alone moved the mean from 1.0 to 1.6 (run 39037489386) | The skill's step 2 ends with a **Self-check**: once `aayu survey --draft` passes, every claim, trap finding, do-not-carry entry, interface, question default, risk, and step is re-read against the lines or probe facts it cites, corrected, narrowed, removed, or turned into a risk or a question, and the check runs again; step 4 does the same for the backlog against the claims and answers each item rests on. The eval (`internal/evals/survey.go`) mirrors it: the draft that passes the floors is judged once for the record (`self_check.first_score`, never counted), the skill gets the self-check turn with the draft that passed (not the refused one), the corrected draft must pass the floors again (one revision; a miss scores 1), and only it is judged and scored. `self_check.changed` lists the items the turn changed, removed, or added, and the eval command prints both per run (`TestTier2SurveyRevisesOnceThenJudges`). The deterministic universal-claim floor is held, as the founder asked; if it is added later, every rule needs a match, a negated, and a near-miss fixture (Story #333's pattern). The survey group's breaker rises to 160 USD, twice the estimated full run with the extra calls, and the group gates only after two consecutive full runs at 4.0 or more per case |
| D-5.9m | The founder's answer to the D-5.9l checkpoint: wire `--parity` and `--compat` into the merge gate for modernisation Stories; exact numeric parity needs a pinned reference build with pinned flags; a cheap check for `decided_by "request"`; a narrowly scoped secret for scratch-repo filing tests; evals dispatched on `dev` only | Before this, a parity criterion was only text: nothing in `aayu verify` ran it, so a rewrite whose output differed could merge on other evidence. MINPACK's own Release flags change the numbers all six test drivers print (`probe:check-differs:hybrd1`, D-5.9j), so goldens from an unnamed build prove nothing. `decided_by "request"` was taken on trust. Survey filing needs only one repository's issues and its ledger branch, not the admin token that creates and deletes repositories (D-0.4f) | **Merge gate:** a `parity` floor in `verify.Standard()` (`internal/verify/parity.go`) applies when a Ready criterion runs `aayu survey --parity` or `--compat`. The Story's evidence map (`.aayudha/evidence/<story>.toml`) gives each such criterion a `[[criterion.parity]]` or `[[criterion.compat]]` run (`cases`, `bin`, an optional `build` run in the sandbox without network, and `ids` or `areas` for the part the Story delivers); the floor replays the case sets or suites the criterion names against the committed capture under `.aayudha/survey/golden/`. Any difference or regression fails it, a criterion with no run or a failed build blocks it, and only a holding run evidences the criterion, so the rewrite cannot merge while its contract fails; it is not waivable (`TestParityFloor`). The compat score is computed on a copy of the capture, so verify leaves the checkout as it found it. The evidence map, not a new config file, carries the runs, because it already maps criteria to evidence. **Reference build:** a case set that proves an `exact` interface names its `reference` (commit, toolchain and version, flags), or `aayu survey --draft` fails. **Request quotes:** an interface `decided_by "request"` carries `quote`, the request's own words, which must appear in the request (case and white space folded); whether those words mean that level stays the judge's call, a known gap. **Scratch secret (founder to create):** a permanent private repository `rahulnakmol/aayu-survey-scratch`; a fine-grained personal access token with access to that repository only, Issues read and write (issues, labels, sub-issues), Contents read and write (the ledger branch the actions log writes), Metadata read, expiring in 90 days; stored as `AAYU_SURVEY_SCRATCH_TOKEN` in the `live-tests` environment. Until it exists, filing tests stay on the fake GitHub. **Evals** are dispatched on `dev` only. The standing rule of D-5.9l holds: the survey group gates only after two consecutive full runs at 4.0 or more |
